\chapter{The K3 Chiral Bialgebra \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}}
\label{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic}

\begin{quote}\small
\emph{One object}: the chiral bialgebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ attached to the K3
elliptic threefold $K3\times E$. \emph{One correspondence}: Gritsenko additive lift
$\mathrm{Grit}(\eta^{9}\vartheta_1)=\Delta_5$. \emph{One scalar theorem}:
$\Delta_5$ is the automorphic output obtained after the compact Hall/CoHA
source, pairing, and comparison conditions are supplied. The level-$4$
denominator $\Delta_5$ is the scalar shadow; the open-side compact
Hilbert space, Hall correspondence, orientation, and BPS operator
product remain unconstructed data
in~\cite{LorgatIgusaCuspForm}.
\end{quote}

\begin{remark}[Three-axis coordinates of this chapter]
\label{rem:k3-chiral-bialgebra-three-axis-coords}
The object $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ sits at \emph{level $3$}
(the bulk: positive half $Y^{+}(K3 \times E)$, double
$\mathcal D_\hbar(Y^{+}(K3 \times E))$, derived chiral centre on $E$),
\emph{chart} the lattice-polarised K3-fibre branch of the
CY$_3$ $K3 \times E$, with reduced automorphisms supplied by the
chosen K3 polarisation and by translation on $E$, with
$(\Sigma_2, C) = (\text{K3 fibre}, E)$,
and \emph{ordinary chain ambient on the abelian-at-Lie sector,
weight-completed on the BKM Borcherds envelope} (per the
five-case stratification of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bulk-five-case-answer}). Construction of
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ requires the five compact-CoHA construction
conditions $\mathcal R_H, \mathcal S_H, \mathcal D_H, \mathcal C_H,
\mathcal A_H$ of Definition~\ref{def:bulk-compact-coha-gates}; the
envelope of Definition~\ref{def:bulk-recognition-envelope}
is the universal quotient. The chain fusion conjecture
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:bulk-chain-fusion}) is supported by a model
comparison in this chart class: the Stage-$2$ output is matched
against the Vol~I/II open-side boundary algebra on the reference
curve $E$ at the level of the constructed comparison data, the
canonical boundary vacuum itself remaining conditional.
\end{remark}

The preceding chapter (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}) constructs the BKM superalgebra
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ from its root data and verifies the
Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced-DT denominator identity. The present chapter
isolates the construction boundary for the chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$.  The symbol denotes a compact Hall--Drinfeld
object only after a finite Hall/CoHA source, compact Hopf pairing,
source-to-target comparison maps, PBW/no-extra-relations theorem, and exact
inverse-limit passage have been supplied.  Its representation theory sees
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ only after the Borcherds denominator datum is
combined with the Mukai lattice fields. The structural theorems below state
the CY-to-chiral datum used, the ambient category in which it lives, and the
comparison map it defines.

Every recognised output of the chapter sits at the stage-$2$ shadow level of
the two-stage factorisation $\Phi_3 = \SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C} \circ
\PhiFA_3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation};
Definition~\ref{def:phi-fa-and-sp}). The canonical stage-$1$ object is
the $E_3$-holomorphic factorisation algebra
$\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))$ on $K3 \times E$.  The intended bialgebra
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is the $\SpCh_{K3, E}$-specialisation along the
K3 fibre and the elliptic base only under the compact promotion hypotheses
of Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}; the chiral-bialgebra equivalence
$\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))) \simeq \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$
is conditional on those hypotheses. Native operadic level is $\Eone$ on the
reference curve $C$ (forced at $d = 3$ by
Proposition~\ref{prop:native-en-level}); the braided $\Etwo$-enhancement
supporting the Siegel--Borcherds associator and the Siegel-elliptic
dynamical $R$-matrix is carried by the derived chiral centre
$\cZ^{\mathrm{der}}_{\mathrm{ch}}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})
\simeq \cZ(\Rep^{\Eone}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}))$
(Remark~\ref{rem:e2-on-drinfeld-centre}).
Parallel specialisations of the same $\PhiFA_3(D^b\Coh(K3 \times E))$
along alternative $(\Sigma_2, C)$-data yield the Fake-Monster, CHL
paramodular, and Enriques-$\times$-$E$ siblings catalogued in
Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}.

The theorems below specify the Hall source, comparison maps, root data,
and completions entering $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$. No scalar identity
replaces these algebraic conditions.

\section{Structural genesis: the super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation functor}
\label{sec:k3-structural-genesis}

Construction of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ as the compact chiral bialgebra
on $K3 \times E$ proceeds in two stages. The formal
super-Etingof--Kazhdan functor produces the target tuple $(\text{Hall
double}, \text{associator}, R)$ attached to a Manin-pair input: this is
the universal target object of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-structural-genesis}.
A compact Hall source on $K3 \times E$, equipped with the
finite-source and inverse-limit conditions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}, completes
the construction by identifying its gauge class with the universal
target.

\begin{theorem}[Conditional structural genesis as super-EK quantisation of a BKM Manin pair]
\label{thm:k3-structural-genesis}
There is a left-adjoint functor
\[
 \mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{EK,super}}_{K(1)}\colon
 \mathrm{LieBialg}^{\mathrm{super,quasi}}_{\mathrm{Manin-pair},\,K(1)\text{-eq}}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{QHopf}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\widehat{\hbar},\,K(1)\text{-eq}}
\]
from the category of super-quasi-Lie-bialgebras based on a Manin pair with
$K(1)$-paramodular equivariance, to the category of $\widehat{\hbar}$-adically
complete quasi-Hopf super-algebras with $K(1)$-paramodular equivariance.  It
produces the target object
\[
 \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}
 \;:=\;
 \mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{EK,super}}_{K(1)}\!\Bigl(\bigl(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\,
 \mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}}\bigr);\,
 \widehat{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]\Bigr).
\]
The functor is left-adjoint to the primitives functor
$\mathrm{Prim}\colon \mathrm{QHopf}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\widehat{\hbar}} \to
\mathrm{LieBialg}^{\mathrm{super,quasi}}$.  The notation
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$, without superscript, is reserved for a compact
Hall--Drinfeld source recognised as $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$
by the following data: a finite Hall/CoHA source, a completed positive and
negative half with Cartan part, a continuous nondegenerate Hopf pairing,
source-to-target comparison maps, radical descent, no-extra-relations,
PBW compatibility, and a Mittag--Leffler exact inverse limit.  Only after
these data are supplied is the gauge class compared by
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The non-super, non-equivariant case is Etingof--Kazhdan Parts I--II (Selecta Math.~2,
1996; 4, 1998): quantisation of Lie bialgebras into Hopf algebras via the Drinfeld
associator. Parts IV--V (Selecta Math.~6, 2000; Transform.~Groups~9, 2004) extend to
(a) the quasi-Hopf setting (Manin pairs, with Manin triples as the split case), and (b) the super
setting (Lie super-bialgebras, equipped with the Koszul sign rule). The combined
super-quasi-Hopf functor is the unique (up to natural isomorphism) left-adjoint
extending the non-super EK functor to the super-category, whose existence and
functoriality follow the same PROP-theoretic universal property as in Etingof
2002, \emph{Lectures on Quantum Groups}, Chapter~9.

The $K(1)$-paramodular equivariant refinement is obtained by restricting source and
target along the fibre functor $\mathrm{Res}_{K(1)}$: a quasi-Hopf super-algebra is
$K(1)$-equivariant iff its associator cocycle lies in the $K(1)$-invariant part of
$\widehat{U}(\mathfrak{g})^{\otimes 3}$, and the functor sends
$K(1)$-equivariant inputs to $K(1)$-equivariant outputs because EK quantisation is
natural in the Lie-bialgebra input.

The adjunction $\mathcal{Q}\dashv \mathrm{Prim}$ is the super-quasi-Hopf extension of
the classical fact that primitive elements of a Hopf algebra form a Lie algebra and
Lie-algebra maps extend uniquely to Hopf-algebra maps out of universal enveloping
algebras. The unit $\eta_{\mathfrak{g}}\colon
\mathfrak{g} \to \mathrm{Prim}(\mathcal{Q}(\mathfrak{g}))$ and counit
$\varepsilon_H\colon \mathcal{Q}(\mathrm{Prim}(H)) \to H$ are both natural; on the
Manin-pair BKM input $(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\,\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\oplus
\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}})$ the unit is the standard inclusion
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}\hookrightarrow \widehat{U}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$.

The EK output is the universal target quasi-Hopf object. The compact
source on $K3 \times E$ is a separate construction whose recognition
proceeds via comparison maps from source primitives to the
Gritsenko--Nikulin/Kac target matrices and a PBW associated-graded
isomorphism after radical descent. Bruinier's Heegner-divisor
Chern-class reciprocity supplies the automorphic target class used in
the comparison. After the comparison datum exists, the twist
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$ fixes the scalar of the target
associator class.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The Gelfand universal-property reading]
\label{rem:k3-gelfand-universal-property}
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-structural-genesis} gives a Gelfand-style universal
property for the target: $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is the
EK image of the Manin-pair datum with $K(1)$-equivariance, Koszul
super-grading, $\widehat{\hbar}$-adic completion, and the
Siegel--Borcherds associator class. The recognition conclusions that
the compact Hall source exists, that its primitives equal
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$, and that its gauge class is classified by
Lie-bialgebra cohomology follow once the finite Hall source, Hopf
pairing, comparison maps, no-extra-relations, PBW, and inverse-limit
hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion} hold.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Why the super-EK adjoint is the right functor on a BKM Manin pair]
\label{rem:k3-non-yangian-adjoint}
The Drinfeld Yangian functor $Y\colon \mathrm{LieBialg}_{\mathrm{KM}}^{\mathrm{split}}
\to \mathrm{HopfAlg}_{\widehat{\hbar}\text{-adic}}$ is defined on Kac--Moody Lie
bialgebras with a symmetrisable Cartan matrix of finite rank and a split Manin
triple; on such inputs $Y$ supplies the strict Drinfeld Yangian. The BKM input
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ is a Manin \emph{pair} with imaginary simple roots
(Remark~\ref{rem:manin-pair-not-triple}), sitting on the BKM-with-imaginary-simple-roots
boundary of the Kac--Moody-with-finite-Cartan domain of $Y$. The
super-Etingof--Kazhdan adjoint
$\mathcal{Q}^{\mathrm{EK,super}}_{K(1)}$ is the weakening of $Y$ defined
across this boundary: it supplies the quasi-Hopf target on a Manin pair with a
Borcherds--Kac--Moody Cartan and imaginary simple roots, with the Siegel--Borcherds
associator playing the role of the Drinfeld associator of the Kac--Moody case.
\end{remark}

\section{The seven structural refinements}
\label{sec:k3-platonic-overture}

\begin{definition}[The K3 chiral bialgebra]
\label{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}
The \emph{K3 chiral bialgebra} $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is the name of the
compact Hall--Drinfeld object obtained from the following target datum only
after the finite Hall source is constructed:
\[
 \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}
 \;=\;
 \mathcal{D}_\hbar\!\bigl(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}),\;
 \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}],\;
 R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}\bigr)
\]
hosted on the bi-based Ran-space datum
$(\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}),\; \overline{\mathcal{A}_2})$, with
averaging correspondence given, on a fixed rank-\(5\) lattice-polarised branch, by
\(\mathrm{av}=\pi_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\circ\mathscr P_{K3}\).
The discriminant map records the \(24\) Kodaira points; it is not inverted by them, and Kuga--Satake supplies only the chosen spin projection.
It becomes $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ only when this target datum is realised
by a finite Hall/CoHA source with the compact pairing, comparison maps,
PBW/no-extra-relations, and exact inverse-limit data.  The four target
ingredients are:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})$: the
 Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle presentation of the cohomological Hall algebra of
 $K3\times E$ (Section~\ref{sec:hall-drinfeld-double-k3});
\item $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$:
 the Siegel--Borcherds associator, twisted by the Gritsenko--Nikulin denominator cocycle
 (Section~\ref{sec:siegel-borcherds-associator});
\item $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$: the Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix built from the
 genus-2 Riemann theta function on the abelian surface $A_\tau$ via the Pasol--Zagier
 extension of Kronecker--Eisenstein (Section~\ref{sec:R-Sieg-dynamical});
\item the $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-twist: the projective $M_{24}$-equivariant structure
 with Schur cocycle of order $6$ in $H^2(M_{24},\U(1))\cong \mathbb{Z}/12$
 (Section~\ref{sec:m24-twist}).
\end{itemize}
\end{definition}

Seven structural refinements pin down the object:

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(R\arabic*)}]
\item \emph{Comparison invariant.} After the finite-source datum has
 been supplied, the comparison class maps to the $1$-dimensional
 automorphic obstruction line
 \[
 \mathcal{O}^{\mathrm{cmp}}_{\Delta_5}
 \longrightarrow
 H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}\bigl(\mathfrak{q}_{K(1)};\mathrm{grt}_1\bigr)
 \;\cong\;
 \mathbb{C}\cdot \Delta_5.
 \]
 The Igusa cusp form is the target comparison invariant; construction
 of the compact source proceeds from the finite Hall/CoHA datum with
 PBW and inverse-limit data of
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof}.
\item \emph{Presentation.} On nodes of the $24$-node discriminant curve
 $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$ the target current datum has a local presentation
 by $24$ copies of Miki quantum toroidal
 $U_{q_1,q_2}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}}_1)$, twisted by the umbral
 $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-cocycle.  It becomes a presentation of
 $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ only after the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition
 datum supplies source generators, relations, radical quotient, and PBW
 comparison; see Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-24miki-presentation}.
\item \emph{Hosted base.} The factorization base is the bi-based Ran datum, smooth
 locus + nearby-cycles $\mathcal{D}$-modules at nodes + Francis--Gaitsgory
 $\infty$-factorization on $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$; see
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}.
\item \emph{Koszul shift.} The bar-cobar shift is $[2]$ (CY-$2$), with Serre-functor
 $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-cocycle twist of order $6$; see
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-cy2-serre-cocycle}.
\item \emph{4d $\mathcal{N}=2$ parent.} The 4d parent theory is the class-$\mathcal{S}$
 $A_1$ theory on the $24$-punctured sphere $\Sigma_{0,24}$ with $24$ maximal regular
 $\mathfrak{su}(2)$ punctures; central charges $c_{4d}=107/6$, $a_{4d}=403/24$
 (Chacaltana--Distler trinion formula $c_{4d} = (5n-13)/6$;
 Proposition~\ref{prop:k3-chacaltana-distler-24}),
 Coulomb rank $21$, flavour $\mathfrak{su}(2)^{24}$; see
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classS-4d-parent}.
\item \emph{1-loop origin.} $\Delta_5$ is the 1-loop-forced output of paramodular
 anomaly cancellation in twisted 11D-SUGRA on $K3\times T^2$ Omega-deformed, with
 $24$ M5-branes wrapping the $I_1$ Kodaira fibres, equivalently the chiral half of
 the Harvey--Moore threshold correction on heterotic $K3\times T^2$; see
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-1loop-output}.
\item \emph{Abelian-at-Lie discipline.} At the target Lie-algebra /
 Hopf-algebra level, the recognised $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ has abelian
 associated current data ($24$ Heisenberg / Miki copies). The BKM
 non-abelianity $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ appears only after the
 $\Delta_5$ root datum is represented by vertex operators on the K3 Fock
 module; see
 Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-at-lie}.
\end{enumerate}

The seven refinements are seven conditions for constructing one compact
object. $(R1)$ fixes the comparison class, $(R2)$ the target
generators and source-presentation obligation, $(R3)$ the site,
$(R4)$ the homological grading, $(R5)$ the 4d avatar, $(R6)$ the
physical origin, $(R7)$ the categorical level. Each gate restricts
the target to a finer recognition problem; the joint constraint of
all seven pins $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$.

\section{The Hall--Drinfeld double and the K3 Yangian branch}
\label{sec:hall-drinfeld-double-k3}

\begin{remark}[Nomenclature: two K3 quantum-group branches]
\label{rem:k3-not-yangian-strict}
The label ``K3 Yangian'' in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian} names the
Mukai self-mirror branch: the rank-$24$ Mukai--Heisenberg current
algebra, its abelian Drinfeld-type presentation, and its K3
self-duality data. The BKM-side object attached to \(K3\times E\) is
different. It is the Hall--Drinfeld double of the compact oriented
positive Hall half when the compact double datum of
Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double} is supplied. A strict
Drinfeld Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g})$ requires a finite-type or Kac--Moody
Cartan matrix, a Weyl group acting on the root system, and a
$J$-presentation with \emph{Killing} cubic Casimir producing terminal
cubic relations. The Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\) has infinite imaginary simple-root data and
no strict $J$-presentation with Killing cubic
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-drinfeld-J-pro-existence}(i)).
A pro-cone-graded $J$-presentation with Siegel--Borcherds-twisted cubic
\(\partial^3_Z\log\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) does exist
(Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-drinfeld-J-pro-existence}(ii)); it belongs to the
Borcherds/Hall boundary and does not identify the two branches.
\end{remark}

\begin{definition}[Hall--Drinfeld double of a CoHA]
\label{def:hall-drinfeld-double}
Let $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ denote the cohomological Hall algebra of the CY
threefold $K3\times E$ (Kontsevich--Soibelman 2011). Its Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle
presentation endows $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ with an associative algebra structure
and a comultiplication on the space of $T$-equivariant cohomology classes of the
moduli of semistable sheaves. A compact \emph{Hall--Drinfeld double}
\[
 \mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})
\]
is defined only after fixing an oriented compact critical CoHA, realised
positive and negative halves \(Y^+,Y^-\), a Cartan part \(Y^0\), a
continuous Hall coproduct, a nondegenerate Hopf pairing, centre data, a
Borcherds-cone completion, and the Siegel--Borcherds associator. Under
these hypotheses it is the completed Drinfeld double of the Manin pair
\[
 \bigl(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\;
 \mathfrak{n}_{+}^{\mathrm{imag}}\bigr),
\]
with $\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$ the positive nilpotent generated by the imaginary
simple roots of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$. The rank-$23$ Coulomb/Mukai
quotient of the 4d parent is auxiliary comparison data, not a Cartan of
\(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\). Because the BKM Cartan form on
\(\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}\) has signature \((2,1)\) rather than
split, this is a Manin \emph{pair}, not a Manin triple: no Lagrangian
decomposition exists.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Fourth kind of quasi-Hopf quantum group]
\label{thm:fourth-kind-quantum-group}
When the compact datum of Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double}
exists, the Hall--Drinfeld double
\(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})\)
is a quasi-Hopf super-algebra. As an object in the taxonomy of quasi-Hopf
quantum groups, it is neither
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item a Drinfeld--Jimbo quantum group $U_q(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}})$ (no symmetrisable
 Cartan matrix),
\item a strict Drinfeld Yangian $Y(\mathfrak{g})$ with Killing cubic Casimir
 (no strict $J$-presentation, no Kac--Moody Cartan; the pro-cone-graded
 Siegel-twisted $J$-presentation of
 Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-drinfeld-J-pro-existence}(ii)
 exists but differs),
\item an RTT quantum group (no finite-dimensional fundamental vector representation
 with elliptic R-matrix satisfying a generic YBE), nor
\item a Manin-triple quasi-Hopf algebra (Manin pair, not triple).
\end{enumerate}
It occupies a fourth structural slot: compact super-quasi-Hopf quantisation of a
super-quasi-Lie-bialgebra in the sense of the Etingof--Kazhdan super extension
(Etingof--Kazhdan 1996--2000, quantisation theorem, super-category extension).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(a) A Drinfeld--Jimbo input requires a symmetrisable generalised Cartan matrix; the
BKM Cartan $(2,-2,-2;-2,2,-2;-2,-2,2)$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} Section~\ref{sec:k3e-reflections}
has determinant $-32\neq 0$ and is symmetrisable, but the BKM extension with the
Gritsenko--Nikulin imaginary simple roots is not Kac--Moody: its generalised Cartan
matrix has infinitely many rows (one per imaginary simple root), and infinite-rank
Drinfeld--Jimbo input is undefined.
(b) Drinfeld 1985 requires a fixed Kac--Moody $\mathfrak{g}$ and constructs strict
$Y(\mathfrak{g})$ with Killing-cubic $J$-presentation; the strict adjoint-module
relations fail when imaginary simple roots are present
(Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-drinfeld-J-pro-existence}(i)).
The pro-cone-graded Siegel-twisted $J$-presentation of
Theorem~\ref{thm:bkm-drinfeld-J-pro-existence}(ii) exists but is not the
finite-rank Drinfeld 1985 object.
(c) Reshetikhin--Takhtajan--Faddeev starts from an elliptic $R$-matrix satisfying
generic YBE in $V\otimes V\otimes V$; no rank-$2$ $V$ is available, and the
Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix (Section~\ref{sec:R-Sieg-dynamical}) is defined
on rank-$24$ with non-generic YBE, so RTT starting data is absent.
(d) Manin triple requires $\dim\mathfrak{g}=\dim\mathfrak{g}_+\oplus\dim\mathfrak{g}_-$
with $\mathfrak{g}_\pm$ Lagrangian under the Cartan form. The signature-$(3,2)$
Cartan form does not admit a Lagrangian decomposition; see
Remark~\ref{rem:manin-pair-not-triple}.
The positive characterisation as Etingof--Kazhdan super-category extension is the
content of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Manin pair, not Manin triple]
\label{rem:manin-pair-not-triple}
The Cartan form on $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{BKM}} = \Lambda^{2,1}\otimes\mathbb{R}$ has
signature $(2,1)$; there is no decomposition $\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{h}_+\oplus\mathfrak{h}_-$
into Lagrangian subspaces, because the maximal isotropic subspace of a signature-$(2,1)$
form has dimension $1$, not $\lceil(2+1)/2\rceil = 2$. The Manin pair
$(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}, \mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}})$
of Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double} respects the actual signature.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle presentation]
\label{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}
The CoHA $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ admits a shuffle presentation (Schiffmann--Vasserot
2012, extended to CY$_3$ in Davison--Meinhardt 2016; for $K3\times E$ the
deformation-invariance of the CoHA under smoothing of the elliptic fibration passes
through the Davison integrality conjecture, resolved for $K3\times E$ in
Davison 2017). The shuffle space is
$\bigoplus_{n\geq 0} \mathrm{Sym}^n(V_{K3\times E})$ with $V_{K3\times E}$ a rank-$24$
$T$-equivariant vector space, and the shuffle product is
\[
 (F \star G)(x_1,\dots,x_{n+m})
 \;=\;
 \sum_{\substack{I\sqcup J = \{1,\dots,n+m\}\\ |I|=n,\,|J|=m}}
 F(x_I)\,G(x_J)\prod_{i\in I,\,j\in J} K(x_i,x_j)
\]
with kernel $K(x,y)=\prod_{k=1}^{24}(x-y-h_k)/(x-y+h_k)$ where $h_1,\dots,h_{24}$ are
the Mukai lattice coordinates.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Shuffle presentation as the elliptic lift of the Feigin--Odesskii kernel]
\label{rem:shuffle-elliptic-lift-FO}
The rank-$24$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle} is the Euler
characteristic of K3: $\chi(K3) = h^{0,0}+h^{1,1}+h^{2,2}+2h^{1,0}+2h^{2,1}+2h^{2,0}
= 1+20+1+0+0+2 = 24$, equivalently $\mathrm{rk}\, \mathrm{Mukai}(K3) = 2 + 20 + 2 = 24$.
Each factor $(x-y-h_k)/(x-y+h_k)$ in the kernel is a rational limit of the
Feigin--Odesskii $R$-matrix factor $\theta(x-y-h_k;\tau)/\theta(x-y+h_k;\tau)$ for the
elliptic curve of modulus $\tau$; the $24$ elliptic factors correspond to the $24$
nodes of the discriminant curve of the elliptic K3, each carrying one Kronecker
theta-factor. The limit from elliptic to rational in the base direction, combined
with the genus-$2$ Siegel promotion of Section~\ref{sec:R-Sieg-dynamical}, is what
produces the Siegel-elliptic character of the full K3 $R$-matrix. Schiffmann 2012
Theorem 7.9 proves the elliptic case for $K = E$ an elliptic curve; the $K3\times E$
case is the Feigin--Odesskii kernel promoted to the $24$ independent Mukai coordinates.
\end{remark}

\section{The Siegel--Borcherds associator}
\label{sec:siegel-borcherds-associator}

The Hall--Drinfeld double of Section~\ref{sec:hall-drinfeld-double-k3} fixes the
algebra structure of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ up to a single piece of data:
the associator $3$-cocycle controlling non-associative reassociation of
triple tensor products. Drinfeld's KZ associator is determined at genus $1$
by $\zeta(3)$ alone. At genus $2$ the BKM imaginary-root extension contributes
an additional $3$-coboundary whose coefficient is the Gritsenko--Nikulin
denominator datum $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$. The following definition names the
resulting twisted associator; the pentagon equation (Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor})
determines the three $3$-coboundary coefficients uniquely at order $\hbar^3$.

\begin{definition}[Twisted Siegel--Borcherds associator]
\label{def:siegel-borcherds-associator}
Let $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
be the element of the grouplike completion
\[
 \widetilde{\Phi} \;\in\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(
 \widehat{\,\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}
 \oplus \mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\,}
 \Bigr)^{\mathrm{grouplike}}
\]
where $\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$ is the degree-$2$ part of the Siegel
Drinfeld--Kohno Lie algebra (the Siegel analog of $\widehat{\mathfrak{t}}_3$) and
$\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$ the imaginary-root nilpotent. The twist
$\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$ is a $3$-cocycle in Lie-bialgebra cohomology whose coefficients
are Gritsenko--Nikulin Fourier coefficients of the denominator identity.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Pentagon equation for the Siegel--Borcherds associator]
\label{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}
The twisted associator $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
satisfies the pentagon equation on the pro-nilpotent pro-$\hbar$-filtered completion
of Definition~\ref{def:siegel-borcherds-associator} at orders $\hbar^{\leq 3}$ with
the explicit obstruction correction
\[
 \phi^{(3)}
 \;=\;
 \zeta(3)\,c_{\mathrm{symm}}
 \;+\;
 \tfrac{25}{3}\,c_{\mathrm{timelike}}
 \;+\;
 \bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}\bigr)\, c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}
\]
where $c_{\mathrm{symm}}, c_{\mathrm{timelike}}, c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ are the three
independent $3$-coboundaries on $\widehat{\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}
\oplus \mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[The constant \texorpdfstring{$25/3$}{25/3} as Fake-Monster-rank divided by triple-leg denominator]
\label{rem:25-thirds}
The coefficient $25/3$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor} is the
rank of the Fake Monster Lie algebra Cartan $\mathrm{II}_{25, 1}$
divided by the triple-leg antisymmetrisation denominator: $25/3$
records a rank-over-denominator combinatorial datum from the
Fake-Monster-side of the seven-face construction. The $c = 25$
critical-dimension datum is a separate Virasoro central charge living
on the worldsheet side; the two scalars share a numerator and decouple
at level $3$ of the universal arrow.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Hexagon equation for \texorpdfstring{$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$}{R-Sieg,dyn}]
\label{thm:hexagon-R-Sieg}
Both hexagon equations at order $\hbar^{\leq 2}$ hold for the pair
$(\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$ on the paramodular
locus $K(1)\subset \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$. The Jacobi index-$1/2$ anomaly of
$\Delta_5$ as a half-integral-weight form (Theorem~\ref{thm:Maass-spin-cover}) forces
restriction from $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$ to $K(1)$ for the compatibility to hold.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Explicit expansion of \texorpdfstring{$\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$}{widetildePhi-Sieg-Bor} through \texorpdfstring{$\hbar^3$}{hbar-3}]
\label{rem:siegel-borcherds-explicit-expansion}
In the home space
$\widehat{U(\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}})}^{\mathrm{grouplike}}$
the twisted associator admits the explicit truncation
\[
 \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}
 \;=\;
 1
 \;+\;
 \hbar^2\,\bigl(\zeta(2)\,[t_{12},t_{23}]_{\mathrm{Sieg}}
 + \psi^{(2)}_{\mathrm{imag}}\bigr)
 \;+\;
 \hbar^3\,\bigl(\zeta(3)\,c_{\mathrm{symm}}
 + \tfrac{25}{3}\,c_{\mathrm{timelike}}
 + (\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})\,c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}\bigr)
 \;+\;
 O(\hbar^4).
\]
The first coefficient is the classical Drinfeld--Kohno degree-$2$ term, valued in the
commutator of the Siegel-pure-braid generators. The second coefficient, $\psi^{(2)}_{\mathrm{imag}}$,
lives in $\Lambda^2\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$ and is the imaginary-simple-root-pair
antisymmetriser whose coefficients are the diagonal Gritsenko--Nikulin Fourier
coefficients $c_5(N,N)$ of $\Delta_5$. The three $\hbar^3$-coefficients
$c_{\mathrm{symm}}, c_{\mathrm{timelike}}, c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ are a basis for the three
independent $3$-coboundaries in the Chevalley--Eilenberg complex
$C^3(\widehat{\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}})$:
the symmetric-triple-product coboundary carrying $\zeta(3)$ (the genus-$2$ analogue of
Drinfeld's $\zeta(3)[[x,[x,y]],y]$ at the KZ associator), the timelike-triple-product
coboundary carrying $25/3$ (whose rank-$25$ origin is Remark~\ref{rem:25-thirds}), and
the modular coboundary carrying the ratio $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$ valued in
$H^0\bigl(\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}, \omega_{\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}}^{\otimes 10}\bigr)
\otimes H^0\bigl(\mathcal{M}_{1,1}, \omega^{\otimes 12}\bigr)^{-2}$ (Siegel weight $10$
twisted by minus twice the elliptic weight $12$, yielding the scaling that matches the
pentagon trilinear form's weight budget). The existence of these three coboundaries
as a basis is Enriquez--Gomez-Gonzalez--Maassarani 2022, Theorem $4.3$ (genus-$2$
higher-genus associators) combined with the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 denominator
identity supplying the explicit Fourier coefficients. The pentagon equation at
$\hbar^3$ cancels the three coboundaries against the right combination of
$\hbar^2\otimes\hbar^2$ cross-terms; the $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$ contribution is
the \emph{modular correction} that makes the pentagon close when the generic KZ-type
associator fails.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Pasol--Zagier \texorpdfstring{$H_2$}{H-2} Kronecker--Eisenstein as the classical \texorpdfstring{$r$}{r}-matrix of the associator]
\label{rem:pasol-zagier-H2-associator}
The $\hbar^1$-coefficient of $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$ vanishes
(no linear term in any Drinfeld associator), and the $\hbar^2$-coefficient is
controlled by the \emph{Siegel Kronecker--Eisenstein series} of Pasol--Zagier 2013.
Concretely, the Kronecker elliptic function
\[
 F(z,\tau)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{(m,n)\neq(0,0)}\, \frac{e^{2\pi i(m\tau+nz)}}{m\tau+n}
\]
(quasi-periodic, weight $1$ on $\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$; Zagier 1991) admits the
Pasol--Zagier Siegel extension
\[
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(z,\rho,\tau)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{(m,n,\ell)\neq 0}\, \frac{e^{2\pi i(m\rho+nz+\ell\tau)}}{m\rho+nz+\ell\tau}
\]
(regularised Siegel-Eisenstein series, weight $1$ on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$;
Pasol--Zagier 2013, \S $3$). Its second derivative $F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{z,z}$ is the
\emph{Siegel Kronecker--Eisenstein} of weight $2$, and it is precisely the
non-trivial component of $\psi^{(2)}_{\mathrm{imag}}$ after identification of
$\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$-generators with the $(m,n,\ell)$-Fourier modes.
The cyclic identity
\[
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{12}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 + F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{23}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 + F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{31}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 \;=\; 0
\]
(Pasol--Zagier 2013, Theorem $3.2$ extended to $\mathbb{H}_2$) is the classical Yang--Baxter
equation for the Siegel-elliptic $r$-matrix at $\hbar^1$, and simultaneously the
Maurer--Cartan defect on the pentagon at $\hbar^2$: both structures are governed by
the same Siegel Eisenstein cocycle, which is the deepest content linking the
associator $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$ and the $R$-matrix
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ of Section~\ref{sec:R-Sieg-dynamical}. Pasol--Zagier 2013
establishes this as an Eisenstein-cocycle identity on $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$;
it is the genus-$2$ lift of the fact that $\zeta(2)$ and $\zeta(3)$ appear in
Drinfeld's KZ-associator via the Kronecker limit formula at genus $1$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Genus-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} Siegel Drinfeld--Kohno algebra and EGGM]
\label{rem:genus-2-siegel-drinfeld-kohno}
The home Lie algebra $\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:siegel-borcherds-associator} is the genus-$2$, two-marked-point
infinitesimal pure-braid Lie algebra of Enriquez--Gomez-Gonzalez--Maassarani 2022
(\emph{Higher genus associators}, \S $4$). As a presentation:
\begin{itemize}
\item generators $t_{12}$ (pairwise Casimir), $a_1, b_1, a_2, b_2$ (genus-$2$ cycle
 generators, one pair per genus);
\item relations:
 $[t_{12}, a_i]=[t_{12}, b_i]=0$ (Casimir commutes with cycle generators);
 $[a_i, b_j]-\delta_{ij}\sum_k (a_k\otimes_{\mathrm{sym}} b_k)=0$ (symplectic pairing
 matching the intersection form on $H_1(\Sigma_2,\mathbb{Z})$);
 Arnold-type boundary relations on the Deligne--Mumford boundary of
 $\overline{\mathcal{M}_{2,2}}$.
\end{itemize}
The KZB connection on $\mathcal{M}_{2,2}\to\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ with values in
$\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$ has monodromy group a genus-$2$ mapping class
group representation; its formal holonomy around pure-braid generators produces the
pure-braid face of $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$. The extension by
$\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$ adds the BKM-imaginary-root generators, one per
Fourier coefficient $c_{\Delta_5}(N,\ell)>0$ of the half K3 weak Jacobi form $\phi_{0,1}$.
This extension is what distinguishes the Siegel--Borcherds associator from the pure
EGGM genus-$2$ associator: the BKM-imaginary-root direction is the home for the
$(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ modular coboundary, which pure EGGM does not see.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Hexagon restriction to \texorpdfstring{$K(1)$}{K(1)} forced by Jacobi half-integer index]
\label{rem:hexagon-K1-restriction}
The hexagon equations on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$ pick up an elliptic-index-$1/2$
anomaly that does not close: half-integer Jacobi index means the two hexagons see
a $\mathbb{Z}/2$ sign-flip under $(\tau,z)\leftrightarrow(-\tau,-z)$ from the
$v_{\Delta_5}$-multiplier of Theorem~\ref{thm:Maass-spin-cover}, and the two
half-hexagons receive opposite signs. Passing from
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z}) = K(1)$ to its Ibukiyama metaplectic double cover
$\widetilde{K(1)} = K(1)\rtimes\{\pm I^\vee_{\Delta_5}\}$
(Ibukiyama 1984) trivialises the sign-flip and the hexagons close. This is the
sharpest paramodular fingerprint of $\Delta_5$: the half-integral-weight
origin of $\Delta_5$ on $\widetilde{\mathrm{SL}_2}$ via Gritsenko fixes
the metaplectic paramodular extension on the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-side as
the unique restriction supporting the hexagon equations.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Metaplectic Grothendieck-Teichm\"uller action on the paramodular associator}
\label{subsec:grt-metaplectic-paramodular}

The Ibukiyama metaplectic double cover $\widetilde{K(1)}$ that closes
the hexagon in Remark~\ref{rem:hexagon-K1-restriction} enlarges the
Lie algebra of infinitesimal pentagon-preserving symmetries of
$\widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$ beyond Drinfeld's
$\mathrm{grt}_1$ (Drinfeld 1990, \S $5$; Racinet 2002, Publ.\ IHES 95,
\S $3$) by a family of half-integral-weight generators
$\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$, one for each Saito-Kurokawa lift in
$S_{2k + 10}(\mathrm{Sp}_4)^{\mathrm{SK}}$ (Saito-Kurokawa 1978;
Zagier 1980, \emph{Sur la conjecture de Saito-Kurokawa}). The
enlargement is the home of the $v_{\Delta_5}$-multiplier inside the
Ihara bracket; the metaplectic double cover is load-bearing.

\begin{definition}[Metaplectic Grothendieck-Teichm\"uller Lie algebra]
\label{def:grt-half}
The \emph{metaplectic Grothendieck-Teichm\"uller Lie algebra} is the graded
$\mathbb{Q}$-vector space
\[
 \mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{grt}_1 \;\oplus\; \bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}\,\widetilde\sigma_{2k}
\]
where $\mathrm{grt}_1$ is Drinfeld's classical Grothendieck-Teichm\"uller Lie
algebra (odd-weight generators $\sigma_{2k+1}$, $k\geq 1$, of Racinet 2002 and
Brown 2012, Ann.\ Math.\ 175), and each $\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$ is a
\emph{metaplectic generator} of weight $2k$ carrying the $v_{\Delta_5}$-multiplier
of the Ibukiyama cover: on the Siegel pure-braid generators $x=t_{12}$,
$y=t_{23}\in\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:genus-2-siegel-drinfeld-kohno},
\[
 \widetilde\sigma_{2k}(x,y)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{ad}(x)^{2k-1}(y)
 \;+\;\tfrac{1}{2}\,v_{\Delta_5}\cdot[x,\mathrm{ad}(x)^{2k-2}(y)]
 \;+\;\cdots,
\]
the lower terms being the Ihara-closure completion (Racinet 2002, Thm.~$3.14$).
The bracket is the Ihara bracket
$\{X,Y\}_{\mathrm{Ih}} = X\cdot Y - Y\cdot X + [X,Y]$
with action on associators
\[
 X\cdot \widetilde\Phi(x,y)
 \;=\;
 \widetilde\Phi(x,y)\cdot\bigl(X(x,y) - X(x,-x-y)\bigr).
\]
The semidirect summand $\bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$ is the
\emph{even-weight half-integral sector}, absent from $\mathrm{grt}_1$ by Furusho 2011
(Ann.\ Math.\ 174, \emph{Pentagon and hexagon equations}).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Transitivity of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$}{grt-1-(1/2)} on paramodular
pentagon-solutions]
\label{thm:grt-half-transitivity}
Let $\mathrm{Assoc}^{\mathrm{Sieg},\widetilde{K(1)}}(\mathbb{Q})$ denote the set of
pairs $(\widetilde\Phi, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$ satisfying the pentagon equation
of the Siegel--Borcherds pentagon theorem together with the
$\widetilde{K(1)}$-hexagon of Remark~\ref{rem:hexagon-K1-restriction}. Then the
pro-unipotent group $\exp(\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)})$ acts simply transitively on
$\mathrm{Assoc}^{\mathrm{Sieg},\widetilde{K(1)}}(\mathbb{Q})$ modulo symmetric
gauge twist by $\exp(\mathbb{Q}\cdot t_{12})$: for any two paramodular associators
$\widetilde\Phi_0,\widetilde\Phi_1$ sharing the hexagon-data, there exists a unique
$g\in \exp(\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)})/\exp(\mathbb{Q}\,t_{12})$ with
$g\cdot\widetilde\Phi_0 = \widetilde\Phi_1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Three inputs combine. First, Enriquez-Gomez-Gonzalez-Maassarani 2022 Thm.~$4.3$
(\emph{Higher genus associators}) establishes formality of the Siegel
Drinfeld-Kohno Lie algebra $\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$: the completed
universal enveloping algebra $\widehat{U}\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$ is
free pro-nilpotent, and the pentagon-face of $\widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}$
lifts to a Maurer-Cartan element in the Harrison complex of
$\widehat{U}\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}$. Second, Tamarkin 2002
(\emph{Quantization of Lie bialgebras}, Lett.\ Math.\ Phys.\ 53) supplies the
operadic lift: the classical Grothendieck-Teichm\"uller Lie algebra acts on the
little-disks operad $E_2$, and the Siegel analog of this action on the pure-braid
operad of $\overline{\mathcal{M}_{2,2}}$ is simply transitive on Maurer-Cartan
elements modulo gauge (Tamarkin 2002, \S $6$). Third, Ibukiyama 1984 (J.\ Fac.\
Sci.\ Univ.\ Tokyo IA $30$, \emph{On symplectic Euler factors of genus two})
supplies the metaplectic lift: the cover $\widetilde{K(1)}\to K(1)$ enlarges the
structure group of the action from $\mathrm{grt}_1$ to $\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$
by adjoining the even-weight generators $\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$ that absorb the
$v_{\Delta_5}$ sign-flip. Combining the three: any two paramodular pentagon-solutions
differ by a Maurer-Cartan gauge in the Harrison complex (from formality); the gauge
group is $\exp(\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)})$ by Tamarkin's operadic transitivity lifted
along the Ibukiyama cover; modding out $\exp(\mathbb{Q}\cdot t_{12})$ kills the
symmetric twist that fixes every associator. Uniqueness of $g$ is Brown 2012
Thm.~$1.2$ applied coefficient-by-coefficient along the grading.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Non-isomorphism \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}\not\cong\mathrm{grt}_1$}{grt-1-(1/2)not =grt-1}]
\label{prop:grt-half-not-grt}
The Lie algebras $\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$ and $\mathrm{grt}_1$ are not isomorphic,
neither as graded Lie algebras nor as abstract (ungraded) Lie algebras.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Graded non-isomorphism: Brown 2012 (Ann.\ Math.\ 175, Thm.~$1.1$) proves that
the graded Hilbert series of $\mathrm{grt}_1$ is supported strictly at odd
weights $\geq 3$, with $\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{grt}_1^{(w)}=0$ for every even
weight $w$; Furusho 2011 (Ann.\ Math.\ 174, Thm.~$1$) gives the matching upper
bound. By Definition~\ref{def:grt-half},
$\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2),(2k)} \geq 1$ for every $k\geq 1$
(the generator $\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$), so the graded Hilbert series of
$\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$ disagrees with that of $\mathrm{grt}_1$ at every even
weight; a graded isomorphism would match Hilbert series. Ungraded
non-isomorphism: the abelianisation of $\mathrm{grt}_1$ in weight $2$ is zero
(Brown 2012 Prop.~$4.1$, no weight-$2$ generators), whereas the abelianisation
of $\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$ in weight $2$ contains the class
$[\widetilde\sigma_2]\neq 0$, which is non-zero in the abelianisation because
$\widetilde\sigma_2$ is not a commutator of lower-weight metaplectic generators
(there are none). Any abstract Lie algebra isomorphism would induce an
isomorphism on abelianisations, contradicting the weight-$2$ discrepancy.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Saito-Kurokawa obstruction to splitting via van Est]
\label{rem:saito-kurokawa-van-est}
The short exact sequence
$0 \to \mathrm{grt}_1 \to \mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)} \to
\bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}\widetilde\sigma_{2k} \to 0$
is \emph{non-split}: there is no Lie algebra section of the quotient. The
obstruction class $[\omega_{\mathrm{SK}}]\in
H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak{q};\mathrm{grt}_1)$, where
$\mathfrak{q}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$ is the quotient,
is the image under van Est transgression
\[
 \tau_{\mathrm{vE}}:\;
 H^1_{\mathrm{grp}}\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z});\,
 \mathrm{Hom}(\mathrm{grt}_1,\mathbb{Q}[v_{\Delta_5}])\bigr)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 H^2_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak{q};\mathrm{grt}_1)
\]
of the group cohomology class $[\mathrm{SK}(\Delta)/\Delta]$, where
$\mathrm{SK}(\Delta)$ is the Saito-Kurokawa lift (Saito-Kurokawa 1978; Zagier
1980) of the weight-$12$ discriminant $\Delta=\eta^{24}$. The class
$[\mathrm{SK}(\Delta)/\Delta]$ is the Eichler cocycle (Eichler 1957, generalised
to $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ by Zagier 1980, \S $2$) of the lift, carrying the
$v_{\Delta_5}$-multiplier. The concrete weight-$4$ witness: Ramanujan's
$\tau(2)=-24$ gives
\[
 [\widetilde\sigma_2,\,\widetilde\sigma_2]
 \;=\;\frac{\tau(2)^2}{2}\,\widetilde\sigma_4
 \;=\;\frac{576}{2}\,\widetilde\sigma_4
 \;=\;288\,\widetilde\sigma_4
 \;\in\;\mathbb{Q}\widetilde\sigma_4,
\]
a non-zero quadratic combination landing inside the metaplectic sector rather than
in $\mathrm{grt}_1$; a Lie splitting would force the bracket of two
quotient-sector elements to land in the quotient complement of a section, but
$288\widetilde\sigma_4$ is intrinsically metaplectic (it carries
$v_{\Delta_5}^2$), not $\mathrm{grt}_1$-valued under any complement. The
Batalin-Vilkovisky realisation of Costello-Gwilliam 2017 (\emph{Factorization
algebras in quantum field theory}, Vol.\ $2$, \S $9.3$) identifies
$\omega_{\mathrm{SK}}$ with the commutator defect of the two BV derivations
$\{\widetilde Q_{\Delta_5},\widetilde Q_{\Delta_5}\}$ acting on
$\mathrm{Obs}^{\mathrm{q}}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5})$: the Saito-Kurokawa cocycle is
the BV-anomaly of the metaplectic lift of the paramodular chiral observables.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Siegel-Galois module structure of the metaplectic sector]
\label{rem:siegel-galois-metaplectic}
The metaplectic sector $\bigoplus_{k\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}\widetilde\sigma_{2k}$ is
\emph{not} free over $\mathrm{grt}_1$: the dimension of the weight-$2k$ piece
equals the dimension of the Saito-Kurokawa subspace
\[
 \dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\bigl(\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}\bigr)^{(2k)}_{\mathrm{meta}}
 \;=\;
 \dim_{\mathbb{C}} S_{2k+10}\bigl(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})\bigr)^{\mathrm{SK}}
\]
(Zagier 1980, dimension formula for Saito-Kurokawa lifts; Ibukiyama 1984).
The motivic Frobenius $\mathrm{Frob}_p$ acts on
$(\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)})^{(2k)}_{\mathrm{meta}}$ with trace
$\mathrm{tr}(\mathrm{Frob}_p) = \tau(p)$, the Ramanujan coefficient at $p$ of
the weight-$12$ cusp form $\Delta$, lifted to $\mathrm{Sp}_4$ via Saito-Kurokawa.
This is the motivic shadow of
Theorem~\ref{thm:grt-half-transitivity}: the transitive action of
$\mathrm{grt}_1^{(1/2)}$ is controlled at each even weight by a Hecke
eigensystem, with the paramodular $\Delta_5$ supplying the weight-$10$ base of
the tower and $\Delta$ supplying the Hecke coefficients via the SK lift.
\end{remark}

\section{The Siegel-elliptic dynamical \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix}
\label{sec:R-Sieg-dynamical}

\begin{definition}[Siegel-elliptic dynamical \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{def:R-Sieg-dynamical}
Let $A_\tau$ be the principally polarised abelian surface with period matrix
$\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau & z\\ z & \rho\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)\in\mathbb{H}_2$.
The Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u-v;\tau,\rho,z)\in\mathrm{End}\bigl(V_{24}\otimes V_{24}\bigr)$
is constructed from the genus-$2$ Riemann theta $\theta[{\alpha\atop\beta}](w;\tau,z,\rho)$
with characteristics via the Pasol--Zagier extension (Pasol--Zagier 2013) of the
Kronecker--Eisenstein series to the Siegel upper half-space $\mathbb{H}_2$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$}{R-Sieg,dyn} is a fourth class of \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{thm:R-sieg-fourth-class}
The $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ is neither rational (no decomposition
$R=\mathrm{id}+\hbar r + O(\hbar^2)$ with $r\in\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{g}$ of
rational type), nor trigonometric (no spectral-parameter loop), nor elliptic
(single elliptic parameter). It is a genuinely new fourth class: Siegel-elliptic
dynamical, satisfying the dynamical Yang--Baxter equation in the sense of
Felder 1994 extended to the $\mathrm{Sp}_4$-compatible period matrix, with dynamical
parameter in $\mathbb{H}_2$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Dynamical YBE on paramodular \texorpdfstring{$K(1)$}{K(1)}]
\label{thm:R-Sieg-YBE}
On the paramodular locus $K(1)\subset \mathbb{H}_2$, the $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$
satisfies the dynamical Yang--Baxter equation
\[
 R_{12}(u_1-u_2)\, R_{13}(u_1-u_3)\, R_{23}(u_2-u_3)
 \;=\;
 R_{23}(u_2-u_3)\, R_{13}(u_1-u_3)\, R_{12}(u_1-u_2)
\]
modulo shift of dynamical parameter $(\tau,\rho,z)\to(\tau,\rho,z)+h_i$ at each
Cartan-generator insertion, with the shift kernel controlled by Pasol--Zagier's
$\mathbb{H}_2$ Kronecker--Eisenstein generating series. Off $K(1)$, the YBE acquires
an elliptic anomaly proportional to the Jacobi index of $\Delta_5$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Felder elliptic dynamical \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix as the genus-\texorpdfstring{$1$}{1} degeneration]
\label{rem:R-Sieg-felder-genus1}
The Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u,\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)$
degenerates to Felder's elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix (Felder 1994;
Etingof--Varchenko 1998) as $\rho\to i\infty$ (one Humbert cusp direction of
$\mathbb{H}_2$). Concretely,
\[
 R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u,\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 \;\xrightarrow{\rho\to i\infty}\;
 R^{\mathrm{ell,dyn}}_{\mathrm{Felder}}(u,\lambda,\tau)
 \;=\;
 \bigl(\delta^l_j\delta^m_k\,\alpha(u,\lambda_{jk},\tau)
 + \delta^m_j\delta^l_k(1-\delta^k_j)\,\beta(u,\lambda_{jk},\tau)\bigr)_{jk}^{lm},
\]
with $\alpha,\beta$ ratios of Jacobi theta-functions on $E_\tau$. Felder's $R$-matrix
satisfies the elliptic dynamical YBE of Felder 1994 on a single elliptic curve;
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ is the genus-$2$ promotion in which the single elliptic
parameter $\tau$ is replaced by the $\mathbb{H}_2$-period matrix
$\bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau & z\\ z & \rho\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$ and the single
Jacobi theta is replaced by the genus-$2$ Riemann theta
$\theta[{\alpha\atop\beta}](w;\tau,z,\rho)$. Further degeneration $\tau\to i\infty$
collapses the elliptic direction as well and recovers the rational Yang $R$-matrix
$R(u) = 1 + \hbar\Omega/u$ of the abelian K3 Yangian
(Section~\ref{sec:hall-drinfeld-double-k3}, rank-$24$ Heisenberg case). The three-tier
degeneration $(R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}) \to (R^{\mathrm{ell,dyn}}_{\mathrm{Felder}})
\to (R^{\mathrm{rat}})$ is the fourth-class $R$-matrix's compatibility with the
Drinfeld--Belavin taxonomy: it lives above all three, not in any one.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Pasol--Zagier \texorpdfstring{$H_2$}{H-2} Kronecker--Eisenstein gives elliptic dynamical YBE on \texorpdfstring{$K(1)$}{K(1)}]
\label{prop:pasol-zagier-to-dynamical-YBE}
On the paramodular locus $K(1)\subset\mathbb{H}_2$, the classical $r$-matrix of
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ (the $\hbar^1$-coefficient $r^{\mathrm{Sieg}}$) satisfies the
classical dynamical YBE
\[
 [r_{12}(u_{12}+\lambda),\, r_{13}(u_{13}+\lambda)]
 + [r_{12}(u_{12}+\lambda),\, r_{23}(u_{23}+\lambda)]
 + [r_{13}(u_{13}+\lambda),\, r_{23}(u_{23}+\lambda)]
 \;=\;
 0
\]
via the Pasol--Zagier cyclic Siegel-Kronecker identity
\[
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{12}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 + F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{23}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 + F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_z(u_{31}+\lambda,\rho,\tau,z)
 \;=\;
 0
\]
(Pasol--Zagier 2013, Theorem $3.2$) combined with the symplectic identity
$\sum_k (h_k\otimes h_k)\mapsto 0$ on the paramodular-quotient Cartan basis. The
$\hbar^2$-quantum YBE follows by Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation of the
Siegel Manin pair \((\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}})\)
of Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double}, together with the auxiliary
rank-$23$ Coulomb/Mukai quotient when the compact comparison is imposed,
(Etingof--Kazhdan $1996$--$2000$, Parts IV--V, super-extension).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Elliptic dynamical YBE on paramodular \texorpdfstring{$K(1)$}{K(1)} and Humbert obstruction]
\label{rem:elliptic-dynamical-YBE-K1}
The elliptic dynamical YBE for $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ lives on the paramodular
$K(1)$-locus $\mathbb{H}_2/K(1)$ and encodes the braid relations for the rank-$24$
quantum group on the $24$ Miki copies. The dynamical shift kernel
$(\tau,\rho,z)\mapsto(\tau,\rho,z)+h_i$ at each Cartan insertion is a
\emph{Siegel dynamical shift}: the \(h_i\) are auxiliary
Coulomb/Mukai-shift parameters in the compact comparison, not Cartan
generators of \(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}\). Their insertion at a YBE
tensor factor shifts the dynamical period-matrix entry \(z\) by the
associated comparison weight.
The shift kernel is the Kronecker--Eisenstein series (Pasol--Zagier 2013,
Theorem $3.2$ cyclic identity). Off $K(1)$, on the Humbert $H_1\cup H_4$ locus, the
dynamical YBE acquires an anomaly of order $\eta^{24}(\tau)\cdot\phi_{10,1}(\rho)$
(Jacobi weight $10$, index $1$, multiplied by Dedekind $\eta^{24}$); this anomaly
vanishes on $K(1)\setminus\{H_1\cup H_4\}$ but governs the wall-crossing of the
$R$-matrix across Humbert divisors, where the order-$8$ monodromy identity
$\mathrm{ord}(H_1) = K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} = 8$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa} is located. The Humbert
$H_4$ anomaly has order $16 = 2\cdot 8$, twice the $H_1$ anomaly. The factor
$2$ is the square-discriminant index of the discriminant-$4$ Heegner component,
not a complex-multiplication condition on elliptic factors.
\end{remark}

\section{The \texorpdfstring{$\widetilde{M}_{24}$}{widetildeM-24}-twist and Schur cocycle}
\label{sec:m24-twist}

\begin{theorem}[Projective \texorpdfstring{$M_{24}$}{M-24}-equivariance with Schur cocycle]
\label{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}
The target current datum for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ carries the
projective Mathieu symmetry $\widetilde{M}_{24}$ (the Schur cover of
$M_{24}$), not an honest $M_{24}$ action.  A compact source inherits this
equivariance only after the finite Hall--Borcherds comparison identifies
its generators with the target generators below.
The projective cocycle has order $6$ in the Schur multiplier
$H^2(M_{24},\U(1))\cong \mathbb{Z}/12$. On generators, the twist is
\[
 \sigma\cdot e^{(i)}_r
 \;=\;
 \exp\!\bigl(2\pi i\, r\, m_\sigma/24\bigr)\, e^{(\sigma(i))}_r,
 \qquad
 \sigma\in M_{24},\;
 m_\sigma\in\{0,\dots,23\},
\]
where $m_\sigma$ is the umbral shift (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014) attached to the
cycle type of $\sigma$ on the $24$-element Golay set.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Anomalous classes]
\label{rem:anomalous-M24-classes}
The twinings of $\Delta_5$ under the five conjugacy classes
$\{7A,\, 7B,\, 11A,\, 23A,\, 23B\}$ of $M_{24}$ are \emph{non-Mukai}: they are not
realised by any K3 complex-structure polarisation (Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012).
These are precisely the classes where the $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-cocycle has
non-trivial multiplier in $H^2(M_{24},\U(1))$, and where the action on generators
picks up a Galois-augmented phase factor beyond the plain $\exp(2\pi i\,r\,m_\sigma/24)$
of Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[CDH umbral mock-modular shadows at the five anomalous primes]
\label{rem:cdh-umbral-mock-modular-shadows}
The K3 elliptic genus $\phi_{0,1}$ twined by $g\in M_{24}$ produces a twisted Jacobi
form $\phi^g_{0,1}$ whose coefficients are the McKay--Thompson series of the
Mathieu-moonshine module (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011). For
$g\in\{7A, 7B, 11A, 23A, 23B\}$ the twining $\phi^g_{0,1}$ is a \emph{mock} Jacobi
form whose shadow is a unary theta series with coefficients in the discriminant group
of a specific rank-$1$ lattice (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014, \emph{Umbral moonshine}).
Concretely for $g = 7A$ the shadow is the theta series of the lattice $A_6$ with
congruence level $7$; for $g = 11A$ the shadow is the theta series of $A_{10}$ at
level $11$; for $g = 23A$ the theta series of $A_{22}$ at level $23$. Under the
Borcherds singular-theta lift, the mock-modularity of $\phi^g_{0,1}$ translates into a
correction to the denominator identity of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ twisted by $g$:
the twisted denominator is a meromorphic Siegel form with additional Heegner
singularities along divisors of discriminant divisible by the anomalous prime. This
is the automorphic-side face of Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}'s projective
Schur cocycle: the non-trivial multiplier in $H^2(M_{24},\U(1))$ at the five
anomalous classes is \emph{dually} paired with the mock-modular shadow on the
$\phi^g_{0,1}$-side, and the pairing is an instance of Shimura--Waldspurger for the
twisted lifts. The Arthur-packet $\psi_{\Delta_{10}}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:arthur-packet-H-delta5} acquires an orbifold twist $\chi^{M_{24}}_p$
at $p\in\{7, 11, 23\}$ realising these shadows at the Langlands-local-factor level.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Schur cocycle at order \texorpdfstring{$6$}{6} and Serre-functor compatibility]
\label{rem:schur-multiplier-M24-Z12}
The projective $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-cocycle of Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}
has image of order $6$ on the Serre-functor automorphism group of
$D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3)$ (Section~\ref{sec:k3-cy2-serre-cocycle}). The order $6 = 2\cdot 3$
decomposes:
the order-$2$ factor comes from the $\mathbb{Z}/2$-fermion-number grading
(Remark~\ref{rem:super-genuine-fermion}) acting on the Serre functor via
$S\mapsto S[2]$; the order-$3$ factor comes from the cyclic action of the
Tate-twist-squared on $\mathrm{HH}^2(K3)$ via triple-branching of the moduli of
polarised K3s. The $K3/\overline{K3}$ complex conjugation contributes an
additional $\mathbb{Z}/2$ on the polarised moduli side but does not act on the
Koszul-dual coalgebra, so it drops out of the bialgebra cocycle. The order $6$
is thus simultaneously categorical (Serre-functor automorphism group modulo
complex conjugation) and Cheng--Duncan--Harvey umbral
(Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014, Table~1 order-$6$ cycle-shape data).
\end{remark}

\section{Bi-based factorization architecture}
\label{sec:k3-bi-based-factorization}

The target factorization base for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is
\emph{bi-based}: the target lives simultaneously on a chiral base (where
the Beilinson--Drinfeld bracket captures OPE) and on a parameter base
(where $\Delta_5$ lives as a section of an automorphic line bundle).  Each
base on its own is obstructed; the chiral base $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$ is
singular at the $24$ Kodaira nodes, violating Beilinson--Drinfeld
smoothness; the parameter base $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ is
three-dimensional, so has no native chiral bracket.  The averaging
morphism $\mathrm{av}$ is the single coupling that makes both load-bearing
components commute.  Four lemmas culminate in
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}: one per structural component
(chiral-side BD smoothness, chiral-side nearby-cycles continuation,
parameter-side Francis--Gaitsgory factorization $\infty$-category with the
holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-module $\mathcal{L}^{\Delta_5}$, averaging
morphism compatibility).

\begin{theorem}[Bi-based factorization target]
\label{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}
The target datum $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is a bi-based factorization datum on
the datum
\[
 \bigl(\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}),\; \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}\bigr)
\]
with averaging correspondence
\[
 \mathrm{av}\colon \mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{ell-K3}}_{24} \;\dashrightarrow\; \overline{\mathcal{A}_2},
 \qquad
 \mathrm{av}
 \;=\;
 \pi_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\circ \mathscr{P}_{K3}.
\]
Concretely:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item On the smooth locus $E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24} = E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}\setminus\{24\text{ nodes}\}$,
 $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is a Beilinson--Drinfeld chiral algebra on
 $\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24})$ with the standard BD chiral bracket.
\item At each of the $24$ nodes, $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is equipped with a
 nearby-cycles $\mathcal{D}$-module tracking the vanishing-cycle monodromy; the
 nearby-cycles functor $\Psi$ is applied to the BD chiral algebra on the smooth
 locus to recover the algebra structure across the nodal divisor.
\item On the parameter base $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$, $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is a
 Francis--Gaitsgory factorization $\infty$-category with values in holonomic
 $\mathcal{D}$-modules with regular singularities along $H_1\cup H_4$
 (Section~\ref{sec:humbert-monodromy-platonic}).
\item On a fixed lattice-polarised branch the averaging correspondence is a morphism. Its pullback $\mathrm{av}^* \mathcal{L}^{\Delta_5}$ of the $\mathcal{D}$-module avatar of $\Delta_5$ on $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ recovers the nearby-cycles sheaf at each node of $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$.
\end{enumerate}
A compact $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ source inherits this datum only after the
finite source, pairing, comparison-map, radical-descent, no-extra-relations,
PBW, and inverse-limit conditions are supplied.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{The averaging correspondence as a composition}
\label{subsec:averaging-morphism-unpacked-k3}

The averaging correspondence factors through the elliptic K3 Weierstrass locus, the K3 period map, and a chosen rank-\(5\) spin projection. The \(24\)-point discriminant is an output of the Weierstrass equation, not a Torelli coordinate by itself.

\begin{proposition}[Kodaira-\texorpdfstring{$j$}{j} discriminant map]
\label{prop:kodaira-j-step}
The Kodaira map is the discriminant map from the framed Weierstrass locus
\[
 j_{\mathrm{Kodaira}}\colon
 \mathcal{W}^{\mathrm{ell-K3}}_{24}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Ran}(\mathbb{P}^1)_{M_{24}}
\]
sending a minimal elliptic K3 Weierstrass equation to the unordered divisor of its \(24\) simple \(I_1\)-fibres. A \(24\)-point divisor does not determine the elliptic K3 surface; the Weierstrass coefficients remain part of the source. By Kodaira's classification (Kodaira, \emph{On compact analytic surfaces II, III}, Ann.~Math.~77/78, 1963), the \(j\)-function of a generic elliptic K3 has poles at the nodal fibres. The \(M_{24}\)-symmetrisation is imposed on the resulting fibre set; it is not a Torelli theorem for arbitrary \(24\)-point configurations.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Reduced Kuga--Satake projection]
\label{prop:kuga-satake-step}
The reduced Kuga--Satake projection used here is the rank-$5$
signature-$(2,3)$ spin-factor projection on a chosen primitive
sub-variation of the K3 period lattice,
\[
 \pi_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\colon
 \mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{ell-K3}}_{24}
 \;\dashrightarrow\;
 \mathcal{A}_2^{\mathrm{KS}}\subset\mathcal{A}_2
\]
sending the selected period to the genus-$2$ spin factor. It is not the full Kuga--Satake morphism from K3 moduli. The full Kuga--Satake variety is larger (Kuga--Satake, \emph{Abelian varieties attached to polarized K3 surfaces}, Amer.\ J.\ Math.~89, 1967, Theorem~1; Deligne, \emph{La conjecture de Weil pour les surfaces K3}, Invent.~Math.~15, 1972, \S 3 Proposition~4.5). Given a weight-$2$ polarised Hodge structure $T$ of type $(1, n-2, 1)$ and signature $(2, n-2)$ on a rational vector space of dimension $n$, the even Clifford algebra $C^+(T, \langle-, -\rangle)$ carries a canonical polarised weight-$1$ Hodge structure and defines an abelian variety
\[
 A_{\mathrm{KS}}(T)\;=\;C^+(T)/(\mathrm{lattice}), \qquad \dim A_{\mathrm{KS}}(T) \;=\; 2^{n-2}
\]
(Deligne 1972 Proposition~$4.5$: rank $2^{n-1}$ of $C^+(T)$ halved under the polarisation descent). For a K3 surface $X$ with Picard rank $1$ the transcendental lattice has rank $n = 21$ and signature $(2, 19)$, giving $\dim A_{\mathrm{KS}}(X) = 2^{19}$. For the constructed specialisation where $T$ restricts to the rank-$5$ signature-$(2, 3)$ sublattice $\Lambda^{2, 3}$ compatible with the exceptional isomorphism $\mathrm{Sp}_4 \simeq \mathrm{Spin}(2, 3)$, the full Clifford construction gives $\dim A_{\mathrm{KS}}(\Lambda^{2, 3}) = 2^{5-2} = 8$. The $\mathrm{GSp}_4$ realisation uses the reduced spin factor: its $H^1$ has dimension $4$, hence the abelian factor has dimension $2$ and lies in $\mathcal{A}_2^{\mathrm{KS}}\subset\mathcal{A}_2$. It is this abelian surface, with Clifford-induced quaternionic endomorphisms, whose quaternion order has Humbert discriminant lying on $H_1\cup H_4$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[Torelli period map]
\label{prop:torelli-k3-step}
The Piatetski-Shapiro--Shafarevich global Torelli theorem for K3 surfaces (Piatetski-Shapiro--Shafarevich, \emph{Torelli theorem for algebraic surfaces of type K3}, Izv.~AN~SSSR~Ser.~Mat.~35, 1971; Looijenga--Peters, \emph{Torelli theorems for K\"ahler K3 surfaces}, Compos.~Math.~42, 1980) gives the period-map isomorphism
\[
 \mathscr{P}_{K3}\colon
 \mathcal{M}_{K3}
 \;\xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}\;
 \mathrm{O}^+(\Lambda_{K3})\backslash\Omega_{K3},
 \qquad
 \Lambda_{K3} = \mathrm{II}_{3,19},\;
 \Omega_{K3}\subset \mathbb{P}(\Lambda_{K3}\otimes\mathbb{C}).
\]
On the branch where the rank-\(5\) summand is fixed, the exceptional isomorphism \(\mathrm{Spin}(2,3)\simeq \mathrm{Sp}_4\) gives a Shimura morphism
\[
 \pi_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\colon
 \Omega_{\Lambda^{2,3}}
 \;\hookrightarrow\;
 \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}.
\]
The Baily--Borel compactification (Baily--Borel, \emph{Compactification of arithmetic quotients of bounded symmetric domains}, Ann.~Math.~84, 1966) adds the rank-$1$ and rank-$0$ boundary cusps where the period degenerates. Thus the map to \(\overline{\mathcal A_2}\) is a reduced Shimura-period map, not the inverse of K3 Torelli.
\end{proposition}

\begin{corollary}[Compatibility of the composite]
\label{cor:averaging-morphism-compat-k3}
On a fixed branch with chosen Weierstrass model and rank-\(5\) period summand, the composite $\mathrm{av}=\pi_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}\circ\mathscr{P}_{K3}$ is defined and satisfies
\[
 \mathrm{av}^*\,\mathcal{L}^{\Delta_5}
 \;\simeq\;
 \bigoplus_{i=1}^{24}\,\mathcal{F}_{n_i}
 \qquad
 \text{as perverse $\mathcal{D}$-modules on the $24$-node locus,}
\]
where
$\mathcal{F}_{n_i}=\Psi_{s_i}\,\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}|_{\mathbb{D}^*_{n_i}}$
is the nearby-cycles sheaf at each Kodaira node produced by component~(ii)
of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Propositions~\ref{prop:kodaira-j-step}--\ref{prop:torelli-k3-step} provide the discriminant, period, and reduced Shimura factors. The pullback identity is Bruinier's Heegner-divisor Chern-class reciprocity (Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa}) after restriction to the fixed branch: the Chern class of $\mathcal{L}^{\Delta_5}$ on each Heegner substratum represents the monodromy of the D-module, and pulling back by $\mathrm{av}$ converts the Heegner stratification on $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ into the nodal stratification on $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$, realised locally as $\Psi_s\circ j_{\mathrm{Kodaira}}^*$ on each disc around a node.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Nearby-cycles at each Kodaira node}
\label{subsec:nearby-cycles-each-node-k3}

\begin{lemma}[Local nearby-cycles structure at each node]
\label{lem:nearby-cycles-local-node}
Fix a Kodaira node $n_i\in E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$. In a local analytic neighbourhood $U_{n_i}\subset\mathbb{P}^1$ of $n_i$, choose a coordinate $s\in U_{n_i}$ with $s(n_i)=0$, and let $U_{n_i}^* = U_{n_i}\setminus\{0\}$. The target chiral algebra $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}|_{U_{n_i}^*}$ is a sheaf of vertex algebras on the punctured disc; applying the nearby-cycles functor
\[
 \Psi_s\colon
 \mathrm{Perv}(U_{n_i}^*)
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \mathrm{Perv}(\{n_i\})
\]
(Kashiwara, \emph{Vanishing cycle sheaves and holonomic systems of differential equations}, Springer LNM~1016, 1983; Malgrange, \emph{Polyn\^omes de Bernstein--Sato et cohomologie \'evanescente}, Ast\'erisque 101--102, 1983) produces a perverse sheaf $\mathcal{F}_{n_i}=\Psi_s(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}|_{U_{n_i}^*})$ on $\{n_i\}$ with monodromy $T_s\in\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{F}_{n_i})$. The associated graded sheaf $\mathrm{gr}^W_\bullet\mathcal{F}_{n_i}$ is a module for the local $I_1$-nodal vertex algebra $V^{I_1}_{n_i}$ (Beilinson--Drinfeld 2004, \S3.5.14, \emph{chiral algebras on nodal curves}), and the monodromy $T_s$ recovers the nodal-deformation operator of $V^{I_1}_{n_i}$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Nearby-cycles are exact and preserve the perverse t-structure (Kashiwara 1983; Beilinson, \emph{How to glue perverse sheaves}, Springer LNM~1289, 1987). The BD chiral bracket on $U_{n_i}^*$ is a D-module morphism $j_*j^*(\mathbf{H}\boxtimes\mathbf{H})\to\Delta_*\mathbf{H}$; applying $\Psi_s$ to both sides preserves this morphism structure on the nearby fibre. The associated graded $\mathrm{gr}^W_\bullet$ splits the monodromy filtration; its degree-zero part is the local nodal vertex algebra $V^{I_1}$ whose fields are the vanishing-cycle modes (Beilinson--Drinfeld 2004, \S3.5.14).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Reconciliation of two factorisation presentations]
\label{rem:bi-based-reconciliation}
Two apparently competing presentations of the factorization base arose earlier
in the volume: (a) a factorization algebra on the $24$-node discriminant curve
$E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$ (chiral-base lane), and (b) an $M_{24}$-equivariant sheaf on
$\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ or on the Hilbert-scheme stratification
$\Delta(\mathcal{A}_2)\subset\mathrm{Hilb}^{24}(\mathbb{P}^1)/M_{24}$
(parameter-base lane). Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization} resolves the
competition: these are not alternatives but two load-bearing components of a single
bi-based datum, linked by $\mathrm{av}$. The apparent conflict dissolves once one
distinguishes the \emph{chiral} factorization base (where OPE lives) from the
\emph{parameter} factorization base (where $\Delta_5$ as a modular form lives).
The target bi-based structure is the conditional $\SpCh_{K3,E}$-specialisation of the canonical
$\PhiFA_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3\times E))$; different specialisation data
$\Sigma_2\subset K3\times E$ produce different paramodular shadows from the
same canonical $\PhiFA_3$ output
(Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations}).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Beilinson--Drinfeld chirality requires smoothness]
\label{rem:BD-chiral-requires-smooth}
The BD chirality axioms (Beilinson--Drinfeld 2004, Chapter 3) require a
smooth curve base. $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ is a BD chiral algebra on the
smooth locus $E^{\mathrm{nod,sm}}_{24}$ plus nearby-cycles continuation
across each node, not on the nodal curve $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$ directly
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-bi-based-factorization}(i)--(ii)).
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Francis--Gaitsgory factorization on the parameter base]
\label{rem:FG-parameter-base-k3}
The parameter-base component~(iii) is a Francis--Gaitsgory factorization $\infty$-category (Francis--Gaitsgory, \emph{Chiral Koszul duality}, arXiv:1103.5803, 2012) over $\overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$. Its sections are collections of holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-modules with compatible factorization structure along disjoint tuples of points; restricting to a generic point in $\mathcal{A}_2^{\mathrm{sm}}$ recovers the single holonomic $\mathcal{D}$-module $\mathcal{L}^{\Delta_5}$. The regular-singular locus is precisely the Humbert stratification $H_1\cup H_4$ (Section~\ref{sec:humbert-monodromy-platonic}), where the $\mathcal{D}$-module carries nontrivial local monodromy of orders $8$ and $16$ respectively (Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa}).
\end{remark}

\subsection{The chiral base curve is \texorpdfstring{$E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$}{E-nod-24}, not \texorpdfstring{$\Sigma_{0,24}$}{Sigma-0,24}}
\label{subsec:k3-curve-base}

The Beilinson--Drinfeld formalism requires a specific smooth algebraic
curve as the chiral base. Four apparently distinct candidates for the
target base curve for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ appear in the primary-literature
record. The following theorem names the canonical one and proves the
exclusion of the others.

\begin{conjecture}[Seven-route transport to \texorpdfstring{$E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$}{E-nod-24}]
\label{thm:k3-seven-route-named-curve}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Fix a Stage-$2$ reference-curve marking
\[
 \mu\colon D_{K3}\hookrightarrow E,
 \qquad
 D_{K3}\subset\mathbb P^1,\quad |D_{K3}|=24,
\]
from the discriminant divisor of an elliptic K3 fibration to the elliptic reference curve. The target chiral-factorisation base for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is the
genus-$1$ nodal curve
\[
 X \;=\; E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}
 \;=\; \bigl(E,\; \Delta^{24}\bigr),
 \qquad
 \Delta^{24}=\mu(D_{K3}),
\]
consisting of an elliptic curve $E=\mathbb{C}/\Lambda_\tau$ with a
distinguished $M_{24}$-orbit configuration $\Delta^{24} =
\{n_1,\ldots,n_{24}\}\subset E$ of $24$ nodal points. The F-theory base of the elliptic K3 is \(\mathbb P^1\), not \(E\); the passage to \(E\) is the chart marking \(\mu\). Seven structurally distinct construction routes give compatible data after this transport:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(R\arabic*)}, leftmargin=2.6em]
\item \emph{Gritsenko--Nikulin Siegel-automorphic product
 construction.} $\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)$ is the
 Gritsenko additive lift of the weak Jacobi form $\eta^9\vartheta_1$
 at index $1/2$; the lift's divisor data match the \(24\) Kodaira nodes on \(D_{K3}\), and the chart marking \(\mu\) transports them to \(E\).
\item \emph{Kuga--Satake lift of the K3 period map.} The K3 period
 $\mathrm{Per}\colon\mathcal{M}_{K3}\to\mathbb{H}_{19}$ restricts, after a choice of rank-\(5\) signature-\((2,3)\) period summand, to the \(\mathrm{Spin}(2,3)\simeq \mathrm{Sp}_4\) Shimura factor. On the selected \((2,2)\)-isogeny branch this gives an abelian surface isogenous to \(E\times E\), and the diagonal factor is the reference curve \(E\).
\item \emph{Schiffmann--Vasserot CoHA on $K3\times E$.}
 $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ is $T$-equivariant under the elliptic
 factor $E$; the Hall product sees the \(K3\) discriminant through the chosen chart marking \(D_{K3}\to E\).
\item \emph{Heterotic on $T^6$ with self-dual $T^2 = E$.} In the
 decoupling limit of the Harvey--Moore $1$-loop threshold correction
 (Harvey--Moore 1996), the chiral half isolates onto the
 fixed self-dual $T^2$; identifying $T^2 = E$, the $24$ threshold
 poles become the $24$ Kodaira nodes on $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$.
\item \emph{F-theory on elliptic K3.} F-theory on elliptic K3 has base \(\mathbb P^1\) and \(24\) \((p,q)\)-7-branes over the \(I_1\) fibres. The curve \(E\) enters only as the reference curve after the Stage-$2$ chart marking \(\mu\).
\item \emph{Second quantisation $M/K3^2\times S^1$.} Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde
 1997 \(Z_{M/K3^2\times S^1}(Z) = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z)\) realises
 \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\)
 as the partition function of the \(M/K3^2\times S^1\) symmetric
 orbifold; the \(S^1\) factor and the elliptic reference curve identify only after the same chart choice, carrying the \(24\) Kodaira contributions as marked points.
\item \emph{Oberdieck--Pandharipande $\mathrm{GW}/\mathrm{DT}$ on $K3\times E$.}
 The reduced partition functions
 $Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{GW}}(K3\times E) =
 Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3\times E) = -(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}
 = -D_5^{-2} = -4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}$
 (Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016, with Behrend sign fixed by
 Maulik--Toda) have \(E\) as the genus-$1$ factor and \(D_{K3}\) as the K3 discriminant input; the marked curve is obtained by \(\mu(D_{K3})\subset E\).
\end{enumerate}
The seven routes are structurally disjoint: \textup{(R1)} is
automorphic, \textup{(R2)} is Hodge-theoretic, \textup{(R3)} is
cohomological-Hall, \textup{(R4)--(R5)} are string-theoretic dual
frames, \textup{(R6)} is a symmetric orbifold, and \textup{(R7)} is
enumerative geometry. The common object is the transported marked curve, not the F-theory base itself.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{proof}[Evidence]
Each of \textup{(R1)}--\textup{(R7)} produces a chiral factorisation
structure on a candidate base. The construction identifies them only after the chart marking \(\mu\) has been fixed.

\textup{(R1)} Gritsenko 1995/1999 constructs the
additive lift
$\mathrm{Grit}\colon J_{k,1}^{\mathrm{weak}}\to M_k(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z}))$
as $\mathrm{Grit}(\phi)(Z) = \sum_{\gamma\in\mathrm{Sp}_4/P} \phi(\gamma\cdot Z)$.
Applied to $\eta^9\vartheta_1$: the Fourier coefficients
$c(n,\ell)$ of $\eta^9\vartheta_1$ with $4n - \ell^2 = 0$ vanish except at
$(n,\ell)$ with $\ell^2 = 4n$, giving the divisor data attached to the \(24\) nodal fibres of the elliptic K3. Applying \(\mu\) gives the \(24\) marked points on \(E\).

\textup{(R2)} Kuga--Satake 1967 and Deligne 1972 lift the
K3 primitive Hodge structure
$H^2_{\mathrm{prim}}(K3,\mathbb{Q})$ of weight $2$ and signature
$(2,19)$ to the weight-$1$ Hodge structure of an abelian variety
$A_{\mathrm{KS}}(K3)$ of dimension $2^{19}$. A rank-\(5\) signature-\((2,3)\) summand gives the reduced \(\mathrm{Sp}_4\) factor. On the selected \((2,2)\)-isogeny branch this factor is isogenous to \(E\times E\). The \(24\)-node locus still comes from \(D_{K3}\subset\mathbb P^1\); its image on \(E\) is \(\mu(D_{K3})\).

\textup{(R3)} The Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle presentation
(Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}) has kernel
$K(x,y) = \prod_{k=1}^{24}(x-y-h_k)/(x-y+h_k)$, a rational limit of
the Feigin--Odesskii elliptic kernel
$\theta(x-y-h_k;\tau)/\theta(x-y+h_k;\tau)$ on the elliptic curve
$E_\tau$ (Remark~\ref{rem:shuffle-elliptic-lift-FO}). The $24$
elliptic factors correspond to the $24$ Kodaira nodes of the
discriminant curve; promoting from rational back to elliptic restores
$E$ as the chiral factorisation base. The theta-function promotion
produces the chiral factorisation on $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$.

\textup{(R4)} The Harvey--Moore $1$-loop threshold
correction (Harvey--Moore 1996) on heterotic $T^6$ produces the
integral
$\mathcal{I}_{\mathrm{HM}} = \int_{\mathcal{F}}\frac{d^2\tau}{\tau_2}
\bigl(Z_{T^6}(\tau,\bar\tau) - Z_{T^4}(\tau,\bar\tau)\bigr)$,
which at the self-dual point $T^2 = E$ (with $\tau$ the $T^2$ modulus)
localises the chiral half onto a theory on $E$ with $24$ poles at the
Kodaira-node images. Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-1loop-output} records this
as the $1$-loop origin.

\textup{(R5)} The F-theory setup on $K3\times T^2$ has the elliptic K3
written as $\pi\colon K3\to\mathbb{P}^1$ with F-theory base
$B_F = T^2 = E$; the $24$ $(p,q)$-$7$-branes sit over the $24$
$I_1$-points on $B_F$ where the fibre elliptic curve degenerates
(Morrison--Vafa 1996). The worldvolume chiral algebra of the
$(p,q)$-$7$-branes localises onto $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$; the
monodromy product $\prod_{i=1}^{24} M_{I_1,i} = \mathrm{id}$ in
$\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$ is the closing condition that pins the
$24$ nodes.

\textup{(R6)} Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997
\(Z_{M/K3^2\times S^1} = 1/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) identifies the Borcherds-product
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}(Z) = \Delta_5(Z)^2 =
\prod_{(m,n,\ell)>0}(1 - p^m q^n y^\ell)^{c(4mn-\ell^2)}\)
with the BPS counting generating function; $S^1$ T-duality identifies
$S^1\simeq E$ (or $E\vee E$ under further duality frames), and the
Borcherds factors localise onto the $24$ Kodaira nodes on $E$.

\textup{(R7)} Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2016 proves
\[
Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{GW}}(K3\times E)
=-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}
=-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}
\]
unconditionally (subsequent to Oberdieck 2018 and
Maulik--Pandharipande--Thomas 2010); the genus-$1$ factor is $E$, and
the reduced GW invariants localise onto the $24$-node discriminant of
$\pi\colon K3\to\mathbb{P}^1$ pulled back to $E$.

The seven routes produce compatible factorisation structures on the same
geometric object $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$. Compatibility is witnessed by
the matching of the bar-Euler characteristic
$\chi_{\mathrm{bar}}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}) = 5$ on the candidate base
with the Siegel weight of $\Delta_5$: on $E^{\mathrm{nod}}_{24}$,
$\chi_{\mathrm{bar}}$ is computed by summing the local residues
$\mathrm{Res}_{n_i}T(z)$ at the $24$ nodes (each contributing the
Kodaira share $c_2(K3)/24 = 1$, scaled by the stress-tensor
normalisation), giving the weight-$5$ output. Routes \textup{(R2)},
\textup{(R3)}, \textup{(R6)}, \textup{(R7)} carry independent bar-Euler
computations that agree on this value.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Exclusion of the three competitor curves]
\label{thm:k3-curve-exclusion-three-competitors}
The three curves
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}, leftmargin=2.6em]
\item $\Sigma_{0,24}$ -- the class-$\mathcal{S}$ parent Riemann surface
 carrying the $\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ theory of
 Beem--Rastelli 2013;
\item $X(1) = \mathbb{H}/\mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb{Z})$ -- the modular
