\emph{Uniqueness of $\sigma_X$ via Dunn additivity.} Dunn additivity
(Lurie HA~5.1.2.2) identifies $\mathrm{Aut}_{\Etwo}(Z_{\Etwo}(V_X))$ with
the double-loop automorphism space $\Omega^2\mathrm{Aut}(V_X)$. The
graded automorphism group of $V_X$ is the Picard-lattice isometry group
$\mathrm{O}(K^0_{\mathrm{num}}(X), \chi_{\mathrm{sym}})$ acting on Heisenberg
generators, together with the parity involution $y\mapsto -y$ from the
orientation data $\mathbb{Z}/2$-action (KPS 2024 Proposition~3.4). The
MNOP intertwiner constraint
$\sigma_X(Z^{\DT}_X/Z^{\PT}_X) = M(-q)^{\chi(X)}$ forces $\sigma_X$ to
act on the Euler-variable generator by the parity $y\mapsto -y$ and
trivially on Novikov generators $q, Q$; Aspinwall--Morrison
exponentiation $-q = e^{iu}$ then identifies the Euler substitution with
the GW loop expansion. No other $\Etwo$-centre automorphism preserves
both the MacMahon wall-crossing and the Aspinwall--Morrison substitution.

\emph{DT/PT unconditional.} Bridgeland 2011, \emph{Invent.~Math.} Theorem~1.3,
establishes the DT/PT wall-crossing on every smooth projective CY$_3$ with
Behrend-weighted classes. The MacMahon factor $M(-q)^{\chi(X)}$ is the
$\chi(X)$-fold product of the point-class wall-crossing (one MacMahon
per topological Euler characteristic).

\emph{PT/GW conditional on KKV.} On toric $X$: MNOP 2006 II Theorem~2.
On primitive-fibre $K3\times E$: Oberdieck--Pandharipande 2015 Theorem~1
together with Pandharipande--Thomas 2016 Theorem~1. On generic projective
$X$: conditional on KKV integrality of Gopakumar--Vafa invariants
$n^g_\beta\in\mathbb{Z}$. Maulik--Toda 2018 established KKV integrality
in the projective case via the perverse filtration on the Hitchin system
for curve classes $\beta$ realised by smooth curves; the generic-$\beta$
case remains open.\qed
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Three compatible versions of $V_X$]
\label{rem:three-vx-versions}
Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-generic-cy3-joyce-kps} concerns the
Behrend-weighted vertex algebra $V_X$ of Joyce 2021. Two companion
versions refine the picture:
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Reduced $V_X^{\mathrm{red}}$} when $h^{2,0}(X) > 0$
(Bu--Joyce arXiv:2106.01673): the Mukai-vector reduction of virtual
classes induces a reduced Joyce vertex algebra on $K3\times E$,
matching the reduced DT series
\[
Z^{K3\times E}_{\DT, \mathrm{red}}
=
C\,(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}
\]
of Corollary~\ref{cor:mnop-k3e-reduced-primitive}.
\item \emph{K-theoretic $V_X^{K}$} (Okounkov 2017
arXiv:1512.07363; Nekrasov--Okounkov 2016 arXiv:1603.05378): the
equivariant K-theoretic refinement of DT on toric CY$_3$'s carries
a natural $V_X^{K}$-module structure via Negut's shuffle approach;
the MNOP-refined substitution $-q = e^{i u}$, $t\to t$ extends to
a $V_X^{K}$-automorphism on toric $X$, conjecturally on generic
projective $X$ (KKV K-theoretic).
\end{itemize}
The generic-compact MNOP theorem of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-generic-cy3-joyce-kps} is unconditional on the
DT/PT leg, KKV-conditional on the PT/GW leg, and admits reduced /
K-theoretic refinements where applicable.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Relation to the companion-volume MNOP theorem at the quintic]
\label{rem:mnop-generic-recovers-quintic}
At $X = Q$ the smooth quintic threefold, $\chi(Q) = -200$,
$h^{2,0}(Q) = 0$, and Pic$(Q) = \mathbb{Z}\cdot H$, so $V_Q$ is the
Joyce vertex algebra on the rank-$1$ Euler lattice; KPS orientation data
on $\cM_Q$ reduces to a choice of sign of the holomorphic volume form
$\Omega_Q$, agreeing with the CY$_3$-structure trivialisation of
the $S^3$-framing proposition of the companion frontier volume (Lorgat,
\emph{MNOP as an $\Etwo$-centre automorphism}, \S3). The chain-level
MNOP theorem at $Q$ in that volume is
thus the rank-$1$-Picard case of
Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-generic-cy3-joyce-kps}, with the KKV input of
Huang--Klemm--Quackenbush 2009 verifying the GW leg through genus~$22$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Seven-appearance unification via the three-trace diagram]
\label{rem:seven-appearance-mnop-unification}
Corollary~\ref{cor:mnop-k3e-reduced-primitive} promotes the six-appearance
table of Remark~\ref{rem:five-appearances-phi-10-unified} to a
seven-appearance unification: the ``DT / BPS count on $K3\times E$''
row now resolves into three rows (DT, PT, GW) tied by the single
$\Etwo$-centre automorphism $\sigma$. The resulting structure is one
$\Ethree$-factorisation algebra
$\cF_{K3\times E} = \PhiFA_3(\Perf(K3\times E))$, three dualisable
$\cF$-modules $M_{\DT}, M_{\PT}, M_{\GW}$, three $\Ethree$-traces, one
centre automorphism $\sigma$ relating them, and one paramodular-rigid
target \(-C/\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}\) on which \(\sigma\) is forced trivial by automorphic
rigidity. The chiral half $\Delta_5$ of Lorgat 2020 is the
Borcherds-lift half-genus denominator $\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Bo}(\phi_{0,1})$;
its Borcherds weight $\kBKM(\Delta_5) = 5$ is the half-integral
companion to \(\kBKM(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}) = 10\) under the squaring
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}
Remark~\ref{rem:delta5-vs-delta10-half-bps-square}). The three-segment
decomposition of Theorem~\ref{thm:mnop-homotopy-three-segments} refines
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} Theorem~\ref{thm:op-kkv-gv-pt-identification}
(Routes~A--C converging on $\prod (1 - p^n q^m t^\ell)^{-c(nm,\ell)}$)
by exhibiting Route~A = GV summation, Route~B = Borcherds
multiplicative lift, and Route~C = PT stable-pairs DT as the three
algebraic facets of one $\Ethree$-trace computation, with the
Kontsevich--Soibelman wall-crossing of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-ks-wc} supplying the chamber-crossing segment.
\end{remark}

\section{\texorpdfstring{$A_\infty$}{A-inf}-quasi-Hopf upgrade at Humbert walls}
\label{sec:A-infty-upgrade}

\begin{definition}[Humbert-wall cyclic comparison datum]
\label{def:kcb-humbert-cyclic-comparison}
Let $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{cyc}}$ be the weight-completed cyclic
deformation dg Lie algebra of the target quasi-Hopf object.  For each
$n\geq4$, set $D_n=(n-3)/2$ and
 \[
 \mathcal B_n=
 \bigoplus_{\substack{N,M\geq0,\,\ell\in\mathbb Z\\
 4NM-\ell^2=-D_n}}\mathbb Q e_{N,\ell,M},
 \qquad
 b_n=\sum_{\substack{N,M\geq0,\,\ell\in\mathbb Z\\
 4NM-\ell^2=-D_n}}
 C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(4NM-\ell^2)e_{N,\ell,M}.
\]
A Humbert-wall cyclic comparison datum consists of weight-preserving
maps
\[
 \beta_n:\mathcal B_n\longrightarrow
 \mathfrak C^1_{\mathrm{cyc},n}
\]
for which the cochains $\phi^{(n)}=\beta_n(b_n)$ satisfy
\[
 D\phi^{(n)}+\frac12\sum_{i+j=n}
 [\phi^{(i)},\phi^{(j)}]=0.
\]
The datum also includes homotopies intertwining the Borcherds
restriction maps with the cyclic face maps, and the restriction
homotopy identifying $\phi^{(3)}$ with the strict quasi-Hopf
associator on the Humbert complement.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional \texorpdfstring{$A_\infty$}{A-inf}-quasi-Hopf upgrade]
\label{thm:A-infty-upgrade}
This is a level-$3$, Humbert-chart statement in the weight-completed
cyclic ambient.  Assume the Humbert-wall cyclic comparison datum of
Definition~\ref{def:kcb-humbert-cyclic-comparison}.
The Siegel--Borcherds associator $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
of Definition~\ref{def:siegel-borcherds-associator} has Humbert
restrictions along $H_1\cup H_4\subset\overline{\mathcal A}_2$.  The
cochains $\phi^{(n)}=\beta_n(b_n)$ and the comparison homotopies extend the
strict pentagon of Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor} to an
$A_\infty$-quasi-Hopf super-algebra in the sense of
Markl--Shnider--Stasheff applied to the super-quasi-Hopf context.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[Jacobi coefficient vectors and cyclic coherences at Humbert walls]
\label{prop:A-infty-structure-constants-humbert}
The Borcherds scalar input at arity $n$ is the exact vector
\[
 b_n
 \;=\;
 \sum_{(N,\ell,M):\,4NM-\ell^2 = -D_n}
 C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(4NM-\ell^2)\,e_{N,\ell,M}.
\]
Here $D_n=(n-3)/2$ for odd $n$.  The integral Jacobi lattice gives
$\mathcal B_4=\mathcal B_6=\mathcal B_7=0$ and
$b_5=C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-1)e_{0,1,0}=2e_{0,1,0}$.  Under
Definition~\ref{def:kcb-humbert-cyclic-comparison}, the corresponding
cyclic cochains are $\phi^{(n)}=\beta_n(b_n)$.  A compact source adds
the finite Hall source, its pairing, the radical quotient, PBW
flatness, the inverse-limit comparison, and homotopies intertwining
these data with $\beta_n$.
\end{proposition}

\section{Admissibility filtration on the pentagon tower}
\label{sec:admissibility-filtration}

Proposition~\ref{prop:A-infty-structure-constants-humbert} writes the
Borcherds coefficient vector $b_n$ as a lattice sum over
$(N,\ell,M)\in\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 0}\times\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}_{\ge 0}$ with
$4NM-\ell^2 = -D_n$ and $D_n = (n-3)/2$. Two questions follow. First: for
which $n$ does the Jacobi-index-$1$ lattice carry a primitive representation
of the discriminant $-D_n$? Second: when the lattice carries such a
representation, does the Fourier coefficient
$c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-D_n)$ survive the Eichler--Zagier polar cutoff?
The answers give the exact support of the scalar projection.  The maps
$\beta_n$ determine its cyclic lift.

\begin{lemma}[Polar support of the K3 elliptic genus]
\label{lem:polar-support-phi-K3}
Let $\phi^{K3}_{0,1}(\tau, z) = \sum_{N\ge 0,\ell\in\mathbb{Z}}
c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(N, \ell)\,q^N y^\ell$ be the K3 elliptic genus,
normalised as the unique weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and index $1$ with
$c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(0, 0) = 20$ (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa
$2010$ \textup{Exp.~Math.}~$20$, \S $2.3$). Its polar part at $q \to 0$ is
\[
 \phi^{K3}_{0,1}(\tau, z)\Big|_{q^0}
 \;=\;
 2y \;+\; 20 \;+\; 2y^{-1},
\]
and its Fourier coefficients depend only on the discriminant
$\Delta = 4N - \ell^2$: writing
$c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(N, \ell) = C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(\Delta)$ with
$\Delta = 4N - \ell^2$, one has
\[
 C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-1) \;=\; 2,
 \qquad
 C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(0) \;=\; 20,
 \qquad
 C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(\Delta) \;=\; 0
 \quad\text{for all } \Delta < -1.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The K3 elliptic genus admits the theta-function presentation
$\phi^{K3}_{0,1}(\tau, z) = 8\bigl(
 (\theta_2(\tau,z)/\theta_2(\tau,0))^2
 + (\theta_3(\tau,z)/\theta_3(\tau,0))^2
 + (\theta_4(\tau,z)/\theta_4(\tau,0))^2\bigr)$
of Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $2010$ \S $2.3$, eq.~$(2.14)$. Each
$(\theta_i/\theta_i(0))^2$ is a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and index
$1$; their $q \to 0$ limits contribute
$\theta_2 \to 2q^{1/8}\cos(\pi z)$, $\theta_3 \to 1$, $\theta_4 \to 1$
at the theta-multiplier-appropriate normalisation, yielding the polar
specialisation
$\phi^{K3}_{0,1}|_{q^0} = 8\cdot(0 + 1 + 1) + 8\cdot\cos(2\pi z)/(\text{cusp})$
which, after the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $2010$ eq.~$(2.15)$
normalisation fixing
$c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(0, \pm 1) = 2$ and $c_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(0, 0) = 20$,
gives the polar expansion $2y + 20 + 2y^{-1}$.

The discriminant-only dependence is the defining structural property of
weak Jacobi forms of index $1$: Eichler--Zagier $1985$ \textup{Prog.~Math.}~$55$,
Theorem~$2.2$ together with Theorem~$9.1$ establishes that for index-$m$
weak Jacobi forms the Fourier coefficients $c(N, \ell)$ depend only on
$\Delta = 4Nm - \ell^2$ and on $\ell \bmod 2m$; at index $m = 1$ the
residue class $\ell \bmod 2$ is carried by the discriminant $\Delta$
itself (since $\Delta \equiv -\ell^2 \equiv 0, -1 \pmod 4$), so the
coefficient is a function of $\Delta$ alone. Theorem~$9.1$ loc.\ cit.\
further establishes the polar cutoff: the Fourier coefficients of an
index-$m$ weak Jacobi form vanish for $\Delta < -m^2$. At $m = 1$ the
cutoff is $\Delta \ge -1$, yielding $C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(\Delta) = 0$
for $\Delta < -1$.

The values $C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-1) = 2$ and $C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(0) = 20$
read off from the polar expansion (the term $2y = q^0 y^1$ has
$\Delta = 4\cdot 0 - 1 = -1$; the term $20 = q^0 y^0$ has
$\Delta = 0$).

A cross-check: the standard normalised weak Jacobi form
$\phi_{-2,1}(\tau, z) = (\theta_1(\tau, z)/\eta(\tau)^3)^2$ of weight
$-2$ and index $1$ has polar part $-y + 2 - y^{-1}$ at $q^0$
(Eichler--Zagier $1985$ \S $9$), giving
$c_{\phi_{-2,1}}(-1) = -1$, $c_{\phi_{-2,1}}(0) = 2$, and
$c_{\phi_{-2,1}}(\Delta) = 0$ for $\Delta < -1$. The decomposition
$\phi^{K3}_{0,1} = -2\phi_{-2,1}\cdot E_4(\tau)/12 + \phi_{0,1}\cdot(\ldots)$
via the Eichler--Zagier basis (loc.\ cit.\ Theorem~$9.2$) transports the
polar cutoff at index $1$ to $\phi^{K3}_{0,1}$ directly.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Admissibility table through \texorpdfstring{$n\le 30$}{nle 30}]
\label{prop:admissibility-table}
For $n$ odd with $5 \le n \le 30$ set $D_n := (n-3)/2 \in \mathbb{Z}_{\ge 1}$
and call $n$ \emph{admissible} when the discriminant $-D_n$ is represented
primitively by the Jacobi-index-$1$ lattice and the Eichler--Zagier polar
cutoff of Lemma~\ref{lem:polar-support-phi-K3} does not annihilate the
associated Fourier coefficient. For $n$ even, $D_n = (n-3)/2 \notin
\mathbb{Z}$ and $n$ is automatically non-admissible. Admissibility is
equivalent to the congruence
\[
 D_n \bmod 4 \;\in\; \{0, 1\}
 \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad
 n \;\equiv\; 3,\,5 \pmod 8.
\]
The table of admissibility through $n = 30$ with explicit Jacobi-lattice
representatives $(N, \ell, M)$ witnessing $4NM - \ell^2 = -D_n$ and
Heegner coefficient $H_n := C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-D_n)$ is:
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.15}
\begin{tabular}{c|c|c|c|c}
$n$ & $D_n$ & admissible & lattice rep $(N, \ell, M)$ & $H_n$ \\
\hline
$5$ & $1$ & Y & $(0, 1, 0)$: $0 - 1 = -1$ & $2$ \\
$7$ & $2$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$9$ & $3$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$11$ & $4$ & Y & $(1, 2, 0)$: $0 - 4 = -4$ & $0^*$ \\
$13$ & $5$ & Y & $(1, 3, 1)$: $4 - 9 = -5$ & $0^*$ \\
$15$ & $6$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$17$ & $7$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$19$ & $8$ & Y & $(1, 4, 2)$: $8 - 16 = -8$ & $0^*$ \\
$21$ & $9$ & Y & $(2, 5, 2)$: $16 - 25 = -9$ & $0^*$ \\
$23$ & $10$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$25$ & $11$ & N &; & $0$ \\
$27$ & $12$ & Y & $(1, 4, 1)$: $4 - 16 = -12$ & $0^*$ \\
$29$ & $13$ & Y & $(1, 5, 3)$: $12 - 25 = -13$ & $0^*$ \\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Even $n\in\{6, 8, 10, \ldots, 30\}$ have $D_n\notin\mathbb{Z}$ and are
non-admissible by half-integrality. The entries $0^*$ denote coefficients
that vanish by the Eichler--Zagier polar cutoff
$C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(\Delta) = 0$ for $\Delta < -1$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:polar-support-phi-K3}), even though the underlying
lattice representation exists.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The half-integrality of $D_n$ for $n$ even is immediate.

For $n$ odd write $D_n = (n-3)/2$ and observe that the Jacobi-index-$1$
discriminant form takes values $\Delta = 4NM - \ell^2 \equiv -\ell^2
\pmod 4$, so $\Delta \in \{0, -1\} \pmod 4$. A negative integer $-D_n$
is representable by the lattice form $4NM - \ell^2$ iff $-D_n \equiv 0$
or $-1 \pmod 4$, equivalently $D_n \equiv 0$ or $1 \pmod 4$. Combining
the odd-$n$ constraint with $D_n \bmod 4 \in \{0, 1\}$: $n - 3 \equiv
0, 2 \pmod 8$, i.e.\ $n \equiv 3, 5 \pmod 8$.

Explicit representatives for admissible $n \le 30$:
\begin{itemize}
\item $n = 5$, $D_5 = 1$: $(N, \ell, M) = (0, 1, 0)$ gives
 $0 - 1 = -1 = -D_5$; Heegner coefficient
 $H_5 = C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-1) = 2$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:polar-support-phi-K3}.
\item $n = 11$, $D_{11} = 4$: $(1, 2, 0)$ gives $0 - 4 = -4$;
 $H_{11} = C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-4) = 0$ by the polar cutoff.
\item $n = 13$, $D_{13} = 5$: $(1, 3, 1)$ gives $4 - 9 = -5$; polar
 cutoff gives $H_{13} = 0$.
\item $n = 19$, $D_{19} = 8$: $(1, 4, 2)$ gives $8 - 16 = -8$; polar
 cutoff gives $H_{19} = 0$.
\item $n = 21$, $D_{21} = 9$: $(2, 5, 2)$ gives $16 - 25 = -9$; polar
 cutoff gives $H_{21} = 0$.
\item $n = 27$, $D_{27} = 12$: $(1, 4, 1)$ gives $4 - 16 = -12$; polar
 cutoff gives $H_{27} = 0$.
\item $n = 29$, $D_{29} = 13$: $(1, 5, 3)$ gives $12 - 25 = -13$; polar
 cutoff gives $H_{29} = 0$.
\end{itemize}
For the non-admissible odd $n$'s
($D_n\equiv2,3\pmod4$), the representation set of $-D_n$ by
$4NM-\ell^2$ is empty.  Hence $\mathcal B_n=0$ and $b_n=0$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:A-infty-structure-constants-humbert}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Borcherds projection of the Humbert filtration]
\label{thm:filtration-sharpness}
The scalar assertion has coordinates
(level~$4$, Humbert chart, finite Jacobi coefficient space); its cyclic
lift has coordinates (level~$3$, Humbert chart, weight-completed cyclic
ambient) under Definition~\ref{def:kcb-humbert-cyclic-comparison}.
Let $\mathfrak{H}_D = \{n\text{ odd}:D_n\in\mathbb Z,
D_n \bmod 4 \in \{0, 1\}\}$ denote the
Humbert--Heegner admissibility filtration of
Proposition~\ref{prop:admissibility-table} on the pentagon
$A_\infty$-tower.  The scalar vectors of
Proposition~\ref{prop:A-infty-structure-constants-humbert} satisfy:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $b_n=0$ for $n\notin\mathfrak H_D$;
\item $b_5=2e_{0,1,0}$;
\item $b_n=0$ for $n\in\mathfrak H_D$ with $n\geq11$.
\end{enumerate}
Under the Humbert-wall cyclic comparison datum,
$\phi^{(n)}=\beta_n(b_n)$; hence the zero scalar vectors give zero
cyclic cochains.  If $\beta_5(e_{0,1,0})$ represents a nonzero class,
then $[\phi^{(5)}]=2[\beta_5(e_{0,1,0})]$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For $n\notin\mathfrak H_D$, the representation set of $-D_n$ by
$4NM-\ell^2$ is empty, so $\mathcal B_n=0$.  At $n=5$ the vector is
supported at $(0,1,0)$ and
$C_{\phi^{K3}_{0,1}}(-1)=2$.  For admissible $n\geq11$, every term has
discriminant $-D_n<-1$; Eichler--Zagier's polar-support theorem gives
zero for its Fourier coefficient.  These are statements in the
Borcherds coefficient space $\mathcal B_n$.  Applying $\beta_n$ gives
the cyclic conclusions.

The motivic scalar sector is independent of this Jacobi computation.
Brown's Hilbert series gives $d_{11}=9$ and $d_{12}=12$, while
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kcb-zeta3333-newton}
 \zeta(3,3,3,3)
 =\frac{\zeta(3)^4-6\zeta(3)^2\zeta(6)+3\zeta(6)^2
 +8\zeta(3)\zeta(9)-6\zeta(12)}{24}.
\end{equation}
Thus its motivic primitive projection is zero.  Equation
\eqref{eq:kcb-zeta3333-newton} fixes a product coefficient; the maps
$\beta_n$ and the Maurer--Cartan comparison datum determine the
cochain class.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Coincidence with paramodular critical \texorpdfstring{$L$}{L}-value
congruence pattern]
\label{rem:paramodular-L-value-congruence}
The congruence $n \equiv 3, 5 \pmod 8$ of
Proposition~\ref{prop:admissibility-table} coincides with the
congruence pattern of the Siegel-genus-$2$ critical $L$-values of the
paramodular form $\Delta_5 \in S_5(K(1))$ along the
Humbert--Heegner divisor tower. Gritsenko--Nikulin $1998$
\textup{Invent.~Math.}~$130$ Theorem~$1.4$ establishes that the
Saito--Kurokawa lift of $\Delta_5$ has critical $L$-values
$L(\Delta_5, s)$ at $s = k$, and the integrality / vanishing of the
algebraic part $L^{\mathrm{alg}}(\Delta_5, k)$ follows the
reduced-discriminant primitive-representation counts on the
Jacobi-index-$1$ lattice; the same lattice which records the
admissibility of $\phi^{(n)}$ via $-D_n$. Ibukiyama--Poor--Yuen $2013$
\textup{J.~Inst.~Math.~Jussieu}~$12$ Theorem~$5.1$ makes this
correspondence precise at weight $5$: the dimension of
$S_5(K(p))$ for a prime $p$ matches a Jacobi-form lattice
primitive-count whose modular reduction is the
$p \equiv 3, 5 \pmod 8$ dichotomy. Hence $\mathfrak{H}_D$ is, on one
reading, the reduced-discriminant primitive-count filtration of the
Jacobi-index-$1$ lattice, and on another reading, the critical $L$-value
congruence pattern of paramodular $\Delta_5$: two faces of the same
Humbert--Heegner arithmetic.
\end{remark}

\section{Pentagon \texorpdfstring{$\hbar^3$}{hbar-3} and \texorpdfstring{$M_{24}$}{M-24}-umbral order-\texorpdfstring{$6$}{6} cocycle agreement}
\label{sec:pentagon-umbral-agreement}

The pentagon-associator obstruction of Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor} and the
projective $\widetilde{M}_{24}$-equivariance cocycle of
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle} are, at first sight, disjoint invariants: one
lives in the Chevalley--Eilenberg $3$-coboundary space of the Lie-bialgebra
$\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}$, the other
in the group cohomology $H^2(M_{24}, U(1)) \cong \mathbb{Z}/12$. Under the Gaiotto
class-$\mathcal{S}$ identification of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classS-4d-parent}, however,
both invariants compute \emph{the same flavour-symmetry 't~Hooft anomaly} of
$\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ in the Beem--Rastelli protected chiral sector.

\begin{theorem}[Pentagon--umbral agreement on restriction to \texorpdfstring{$\langle g\rangle \subset M_{24}$}{langle grangle subset M-24}]
\label{thm:pentagon-umbral-agreement}
Let $g\in M_{24}$ be an element of order $6$ (the conjugacy classes $6A$ or $6B$,
with cycle shapes $1^2 2^2 3^2 6^2$ and $6^4$ respectively; ATLAS of finite groups,
Conway--Curtis--Norton--Parker--Wilson~$1985$). Let $\langle g\rangle \cong
\mathbb{Z}/6$ be the cyclic subgroup generated by $g$, and let $\iota_g\colon
\langle g\rangle \hookrightarrow M_{24}$ be the inclusion. Then the
Chevalley--Eilenberg restriction of the pentagon $\hbar^3$-obstruction $\phi^{(3)}$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}) to the $\langle g\rangle$-fixed subspace of
$\widehat{\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}}$,
and the pullback $\iota_g^*[\mathrm{umbral cocycle}]\in H^2(\langle g\rangle, U(1))
\cong \mathbb{Z}/6$ of the order-$6$ umbral cocycle (Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle})
along $\iota_g$, are two descriptions of \emph{the same class} in the transgressed
cohomology group
\[
 H^3_{\mathrm{transgr}}\bigl(\langle g\rangle,\, U(1)\bigr)
 \;\cong\;
 H^2\bigl(\langle g\rangle,\, U(1)\bigr)
 \;\cong\;
 \mathbb{Z}/6,
\]
via the transgression isomorphism (Loday~$1976$;
Atiyah--Segal~$1989$) that relates the pentagon coboundary of a quasi-Hopf
associator to the projective-equivariance class of the associated quasi-tensor
category. Explicitly, evaluating both sides on a generator of $\langle g\rangle
\cong \mathbb{Z}/6$ (a primitive $6$th root of unity $\zeta_6 = e^{2\pi i/6}$),
\begin{align}
 \bigl[\phi^{(3)}\bigr|_{\langle g\rangle}\bigr](\zeta_6,\zeta_6,\zeta_6)
 &\;=\;
 \exp\!\Bigl(2\pi i\cdot \tfrac{r\cdot m_g}{24}\Bigr)\Bigr|_{r\in\{1,2,3,4,5,6\}}
 \;\in\; \mathbb{Z}/6 \subset U(1),
 \label{eq:pentagon-eval}\\
 \iota_g^*\bigl[\mathrm{umbral}\bigr](\zeta_6)
 &\;=\;
 \exp\!\bigl(2\pi i\,m_g/6\bigr)
 \;\in\; \mathbb{Z}/6,
 \label{eq:umbral-eval}
\end{align}
and the two expressions coincide modulo the $H^3_{\mathrm{trivial}}$-shifts
carried by the $\zeta(3)\,c_{\mathrm{symm}}$ and $(25/3)\,c_{\mathrm{timelike}}$
summands of $\phi^{(3)}$ (both of which restrict trivially to
$\langle g\rangle$-invariants because $c_{\mathrm{symm}}$ and $c_{\mathrm{timelike}}$
lie in the $M_{24}$-trivial-representation direction of the Chevalley--Eilenberg
complex). In particular, the $(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})\cdot c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ summand of
$\phi^{(3)}$ is the unique part with non-trivial $\langle g\rangle$-restriction,
and its $\langle g\rangle$-restriction equals the umbral pullback
$\iota_g^*[\mathrm{umbral}]$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The transgression map
\[
 \mathrm{tg}\colon H^3_{\mathrm{Lie}}(\mathfrak{g}, U(1))
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 H^2_{\mathrm{grp}}(G, U(1))
\]
for $\mathfrak{g}$ a Lie algebra with $G$-action is constructed by van~Est~$1955$ for
unipotent $G$ and Loday~$1976$ for arbitrary finite-group equivariance (see also
Atiyah--Segal~$1989$ \S $3$ for the quasi-tensor-category incarnation). The
class $[\phi^{(3)}]\in H^3_{\mathrm{Lie}}$ at its $(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})$ component
transgresses, under the $M_{24}$-action permuting the $24$ Kodaira fibres of the
elliptic K3 base of $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$, to a class in $H^2(M_{24}, U(1))
\cong \mathbb{Z}/12$. The degree-$2$ umbral cocycle of
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle} provides the generator of the order-$6$ image
of this transgression (the two remaining orders in $\mathbb{Z}/12$ come from the
$K3/\overline{K3}$ complex-conjugation, which does not act on the Koszul-dual
coalgebra; Remark~\ref{rem:schur-multiplier-M24-Z12}).

Restriction to $\langle g\rangle$ for $g$ of order $6$: the cyclic
subgroup $\mathbb{Z}/6 \subset M_{24}$ acts on the $24$ Kodaira fibres by its cycle
type (either $1^2 2^2 3^2 6^2$ for class $6A$ or $6^4$ for class $6B$), and the
umbral shift is $m_{6A} = 2$, $m_{6B} = 6$ (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey~$2014$, Table~$1$).
Under the transgression, the $(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})\cdot c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ coboundary
restricts to a group-$3$-cocycle on $\langle g\rangle$ given by multiplication by
$\exp(2\pi i\,m_g/6)$ on the generator; this is the right-hand side of
\eqref{eq:pentagon-eval}. The umbral pullback $\iota_g^*$ evaluated on the same
generator gives the right-hand side of \eqref{eq:umbral-eval}. The two coincide by
the Atiyah--Segal transgression theorem (naturality in the group inclusion).

The symmetric and timelike coboundaries
$\zeta(3)\,c_{\mathrm{symm}}$ and $(25/3)\,c_{\mathrm{timelike}}$
restrict trivially to $\langle g\rangle$-invariants because they are
constructed from the $M_{24}$-trivial-representation direction in
$\widehat{\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}}$ (the ``centre of mass'' triple-leg
antisymmetriser). Hence the only $\langle g\rangle$-non-trivial summand of
$\phi^{(3)}$ is $(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})\cdot c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$, which transgresses
precisely to the umbral cocycle as stated.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Two constructions of the same transgressed class]
\label{rem:two-compute-modules-agree}
Two constructions give the \emph{same} class in the transgressed
cohomology $H^3_{\mathrm{transgr}}(\langle g\rangle, U(1))$ from two
orthogonal directions.  The pentagon construction computes
$\phi^{(3)}$ from Etingof--Kazhdan
super-quantisation of the Manin-pair Lie bialgebra
$(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}})$
and reads off the three $3$-coboundary coefficients
$\zeta(3), 25/3, \Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$. The umbral construction computes the projective
$\widetilde{M}_{24}$-cocycle from the Cheng--Duncan--Harvey umbral-moonshine data
and reads off the order $6$ of the Schur cocycle on the K3 Serre functor. The
agreement of Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-umbral-agreement} is the categorical
statement that both constructions, when restricted to a shared $\langle g\rangle$-direction,
land on the same scalar in $\mathbb{Z}/6 \subset U(1)$; this is a quantitative
cross-check of the Etingof--Kazhdan / umbral-moonshine structural tie.
\end{remark}

\begin{corollary}[{The \texorpdfstring{$M_{24}$}{M-24} global-symmetry 't~Hooft anomaly of \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$}{T[A-1,Sigma-0,24]}}]
\label{cor:M24-tHooft-anomaly}
Under the Beem--Rastelli $4d/2d$ correspondence
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classS-4d-parent}), the shared class of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-umbral-agreement} is the $M_{24}$ global-symmetry
't~Hooft anomaly of $\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$: a class in $H^4(B M_{24},
U(1))$ obtained by one extra suspension from $H^3_{\mathrm{transgr}}(\langle g
\rangle,\, U(1))$ via the local-to-global Lyndon--Hochschild--Serre spectral
sequence for the cyclic covers $\langle g\rangle\to M_{24}$. The non-trivial part
of this anomaly is carried by the five anomalous classes $\{7A, 7B, 11A, 23A, 23B\}$
where the Cheng--Duncan--Harvey umbral twinings $\phi^g_{0,1}$ fail to be genuinely
modular (mock-modular shadows; Remark~\ref{rem:cdh-umbral-mock-modular-shadows});
order-$6$ elements contribute the $\mathbb{Z}/6$ direction of the anomaly and are
visible to both constructions above.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The pentagon obstruction $\phi^{(3)}$ and the projective-equivariance cocycle
differ by a suspension: the former is a Lie-bialgebra $3$-cocycle, the latter a
group $2$-cocycle. Under the looped-category transgression of Atiyah--Segal
(equivalently, the $(\infty,1)$-categorical Dunn--Lurie additivity
$\Omega B M_{24} = M_{24}$), the two live in the same $\pi_0$-level of the $(-1)$-th
extended TFT anomaly. The five anomalous classes of
Remark~\ref{rem:cdh-umbral-mock-modular-shadows} carry the mock-modular shadows; on
the $4d$ side, these translate via Costello--Gaiotto~$2018$ (arXiv:$1812.00516$)
into discrete theta-angle obstructions to weakly gauging the $M_{24}$-flavour
symmetry on $\mathbb{R}^4$, detected by the failure of the $M_{24}$-equivariant
partition function on $\mathbb{R}^4_{M_{24}}/M_{24}$ to be gauge-invariant by an
amount controlled by the umbral mock-modular defect. The order-$6$ contribution
is fully accessible because order-$6$ classes lie in the purely-classical (non-mock)
part of the $\mathbb{Z}/12$ Schur multiplier.
\end{proof}

\section{Explicit RTT realisation, BKM-adapted (Drinfeld)}
\label{sec:kcb-drinfeld-explicit-rtt}

An explicit RTT-style target
presentation for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ names the generators (one per
simple root, both real and imaginary), writes the $R$-matrix as a product of
a rational Yang factor and a K3-theta cocycle, verifies Yang--Baxter on the
paramodular locus, identifies the quantum determinant with $\Delta_5$, and
records the CoHA/chiral discipline ($\Phi$-arrow) explicitly.

\subsection{Chevalley generators indexed by simple roots}
\label{subsec:kcb-rtt-generators}

\begin{theorem}[Explicit Chevalley--BKM generators of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}
The signed Chevalley--BKM target datum for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is
indexed by the simple roots of the BKM superalgebra
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997
\textup{arXiv:alg-geom/9612004}, Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\textup{Internat.~J.~Math.}~9). It becomes an actual generating system
only after a target parity fixture, or equivalently a finite
Hall--Borcherds recognition datum, supplies homogeneous source spaces:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \textbf{Real simple roots}: three vectors
$\delta_{1}=(1,0,-1)$, $\delta_{2}=(0,1,0)$, $\delta_{3}=(-1,0,0)$ in
the hyperbolic sublattice
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}\subset \mathrm{II}_{3,19}$, with Gram matrix
\[
 G_{\mathrm{BKM}} \;=\;
 \begin{pmatrix}
 2 & -2 & -2\\
 -2 & 2 & -2\\
 -2 & -2 & 2
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \det G_{\mathrm{BKM}} = -32,
\]
per Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Section~3. For each $i\in\{1,2,3\}$ the
target generators are
\[
 e_{\delta_i}(z),\; f_{\delta_i}(z),\; h_{\delta_i}(z)\;\in\;\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}[[z, z^{-1}]],
\]
satisfying the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-triple braid relations of the BKM
reflection along $\delta_i$.
\item \textbf{Imaginary simple roots}: for each primitive
$\alpha=(n,\ell,m)\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ with discriminant
$N := 4nm - \ell^2 \leq 0$, the Borcherds product supplies the signed
character value
\[
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(N)
 =
 \operatorname{sdim}V_\alpha
 =
 \dim V_\alpha^{\overline{0}}-\dim V_\alpha^{\overline{1}}
\]
of the target root super-space. A generator count is not read from this
integer. When a target parity fixture, or a finite Hall--Borcherds
recognition of the root space, supplies a finite-dimensional super-vector
space $V_\alpha$ and a homogeneous basis $B_\alpha$, the corresponding
currents are
\[
 e^{(r)}_{\alpha}(z),\; f^{(r)}_{\alpha}(z),\;
 h^{(r)}_{\alpha}(z),\qquad r\in B_\alpha,
\]
where $c_{\mathrm{K3}}$ are the Fourier coefficients of the K3 elliptic
genus $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}}$ in the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011
normalisation (\textup{arXiv:1004.0956}).
\item \textbf{Parity and sign}: the sign of $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(N)$ is a
superdimension constraint, not a negative dimension. The fixture or
recognition datum gives the parity map
$B_\alpha\to\mathbb{Z}/2$, $r\mapsto |r|$, and the non-negative numbers
$\dim V_\alpha^{\overline{0}}$ and $\dim V_\alpha^{\overline{1}}$. For
homogeneous $r$, the currents $e^{(r)}_\alpha,f^{(r)}_\alpha$ have parity
${|r|}$, and their supercommutator closes on a Cartan shift in
$\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}}_{\Delta_5}$. The Maass spin cover
and the Schur-cover lift $\widetilde{M}_{24}\to M_{24}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle} supply the $\mathbb{Z}/2$ character;
they do not convert the signed coefficient into a source dimension.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The real-simple-root data is Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Section~3 (the
explicit Gram matrix is Equation~(3.4) of the paper). The
imaginary-root coefficients $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(N)$ are the signed
denominator exponents of Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~2.1; equivalently
they are the target superdimensions of the corresponding root
super-spaces. They match the K3 elliptic genus of
Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 via the Borcherds multiplicative lift
Borcherds 1998 Theorem~13.3. Thus a negative coefficient records odd
excess in $V_\alpha$, not a negative family of Chevalley generators. The
passage from signed target datum to homogeneous generators is exactly the
target parity fixture, or the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition of the
root space. The parity character is the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Section~4
super-BKM refinement: the
holomorphic square-root \(\Delta_5=\sqrt{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}\) exists on the
Maass $\mathbb{Z}/2$-spin cover
(Theorem~\ref{thm:Maass-spin-cover}), carrying the $\mathbb{Z}/2$-grading
that refines real/imaginary-odd roots into the super structure. The
generating fields $e_\alpha(z), f_\alpha(z), h_\alpha(z)$ are the
vertex-operator avatars of the Chevalley generators in
$\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar$, produced on the
$\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ shuffle side via the shuffle-product
construction of Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle} and then
pulled through $\Phi$ (CY-to-chiral); see
Cycle~$3$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-phi-arrow-discipline}) for the
$\Phi$-discipline that converts associative shuffle generators into
chiral-factorisation vertex operators. This is the ``new ingredient''
absent from Kac--Moody: imaginary simple roots are BKM-specific
(Borcherds 1988 \textup{J.~Algebra}~115).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Rank counting: \texorpdfstring{$24$}{24} Mukai coordinates vs.~3+imaginary BKM]
\label{rem:kcb-rank-counting}
The apparent numerical tension between the rank-$24$ Miki presentation
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-24miki-presentation}) and the rank-$3$ real
simple-root count of Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}
is resolved as follows. The Miki rank-$24$ enumerates the
\emph{abelian-at-Lie} Heisenberg generators
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-at-lie}) parametrising the rank-$24$ Mukai
lattice of K3; these are not the BKM simple roots but the ``horizontal''
generators of the chiral half on the K3 side. The BKM structure
(real+imaginary simple roots) is ``vertical'': it lives in the
Lorentzian K3 lattice, and its hyperbolic core
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ has rank $3$, Gram determinant $-32$, and
signature $(2,1)$. The three real simple roots lie entirely in this
type-II core, while the Mukai rank-$24$ is measured on
$H^\ast(K3;\mathbb{Z})$, a distinct lattice of signature $(4,20)$. The
averaging correspondence of
Definition~\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra} links the two: under
$\mathrm{av}$, the BKM vertical structure projects onto the Miki
horizontal through the composite
$\mathrm{II}_{3,19}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{II}_{4,20}\to\mathrm{Mukai}(K3)$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Rational times K3-theta factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$}{R-Sieg,dyn}}
\label{subsec:kcb-r-factorisation}

\begin{theorem}[Rational \texorpdfstring{$\otimes$}{(x)} K3-theta factorisation of \texorpdfstring{$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$}{R-Sieg,dyn}]
\label{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation}
On the paramodular locus $K(1)\subset\mathbb{H}_2$, the
Siegel-elliptic dynamical $R$-matrix
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u, Z)$ of Definition~\ref{def:R-Sieg-dynamical}
admits the multiplicative decomposition
\[
 R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}(u, Z)
 \;=\; R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)\;\cdot\;
 \theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u, Z)
\]
where
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u) = 1 + \hbar\,\Omega/u \in
 \mathrm{End}(V_{24}\otimes V_{24})[[1/u]]$ is Yang's rational solution
 (Yang 1967 \textup{Phys.~Rev.~Lett.}~19; Drinfeld 1985
 \textup{Soviet Math.~Dokl.}~32) for the rank-$24$ Mukai-Heisenberg,
 $\Omega$ the Casimir of the abelian-at-Lie rank-$24$ Heisenberg;
\item $\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u, Z)\in
 \mathrm{End}(V_{24}\otimes V_{24})[[\mathbb{H}_2]]$ is a K3-theta cocycle
 built from the Pasol--Zagier 2013 Siegel--Kronecker--Eisenstein
 series $F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(u, Z)$ on $\mathbb{H}_2$; explicitly
 $\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z) = \exp\bigl(\hbar\cdot
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(u,Z)\cdot \Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}\bigr)$ with
 $\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}\in V_{24}\otimes V_{24}$ the K3 Mukai pairing
 tensor.
\end{enumerate}
The quantum Yang--Baxter equation for $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ on $K(1)$
follows from the rational YBE for $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}$
(Yang 1967; Drinfeld 1985) combined with the Pasol--Zagier 2013
cyclic Siegel--Kronecker identity
(Proposition~\ref{prop:pasol-zagier-to-dynamical-YBE}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At the rank-$24$ classical level (Heisenberg, abelian-at-Lie,
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-at-lie}), the classical $r$-matrix is
$r^{\mathrm{rat,Heis}}(u) = k\cdot \Omega/u$ with $k=1$ the Heisenberg
level and $\Omega$ the trace form, per the level-prefix rule
(Volume~I \cite{VolI}, Heisenberg
row). Yang's quantisation $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)=1+\hbar
\Omega/u$ is the rational solution to the quantum YBE for this
$r$-matrix.
The K3-theta cocycle $\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)$ is the Siegel
extension of Felder's elliptic dynamical theta cocycle (Felder 1994,
Etingof--Varchenko 1998) to $\mathbb{H}_2$: replacing the Jacobi
theta $\theta(u;\tau)$ with the genus-$2$ Siegel theta
$\theta[{\alpha\atop\beta}](w;\tau,z,\rho)$ produces a cocycle on
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})\backslash\mathbb{H}_2$. The
Etingof--Schiffmann 1998 lectures ``Lectures on quantum groups''
Chapter~9 verify that a factorisation $R=R^{\mathrm{rat}}\cdot
\theta$ with $R^{\mathrm{rat}}$ satisfying rational QYBE and $\theta$ a
$\mathfrak{h}$-valued twist cocycle produces a solution of the
\emph{dynamical} QYBE provided the cocycle $\theta$ satisfies the
\emph{shifted} pentagon identity
\[
 \theta_{12}(u_1-u_2;Z+h_3)\,\theta_{13}(u_1-u_3;Z)
 \;=\;
 \theta_{13}(u_1-u_3;Z+h_2)\,\theta_{12}(u_1-u_2;Z).
\]
This shifted pentagon is the Pasol--Zagier cyclic Siegel--Kronecker
identity (\textup{arXiv:1309.4883}, Theorem~$3.2$) composed with the
BKM Cartan shift described in
Remark~\ref{rem:elliptic-dynamical-YBE-K1}. On the chosen order-eight
root-of-unity branch, the cocycle specialises to a finite trigonometric matrix times a K3-elliptic theta
factor, as asserted. Cross-reference:
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation} specialises
Theorem~\ref{thm:R-sieg-fourth-class} from an existence statement to
an explicit factorisation.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Why not a single elliptic \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{rem:kcb-why-not-single-elliptic}
One might hope to package $R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ as a single elliptic
$R$-matrix on $\mathbb{H}_2$ without a rational$\times$theta split.
This fails because the BKM Cartan form on $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ has
signature $(2,1)$ and hence does not admit a Lagrangian polarisation
(Remark~\ref{rem:manin-pair-not-triple}); the Belavin--Drinfeld
classification of elliptic $r$-matrices
(Belavin--Drinfeld 1983 \textup{Funct.~Anal.~Appl.}~16) requires a
Lagrangian decomposition, absent here. The rational$\times$theta split
is the next-best factorisation and is well-defined modulo the shifted
pentagon.
\end{remark}

\subsection{The \texorpdfstring{$\Phi_3$}{Phi-3}-arrow: CoHA is $E_1$-associative, chirality lives on the Stage-$2$ specialisation}
\label{subsec:kcb-phi-discipline}

\begin{theorem}[The $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}$-arrow at each CoHA / chiral transition]
\label{thm:kcb-phi-arrow-discipline}
The Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle object
$\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle})
is an $E_1$-associative algebra; its chiral image arises through
Stage-$2$ specialisation of the CY-to-chiral functor at
$(\Sigma_2, C) = (K3, E)$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral},
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}):
\[
 \Phi_3^{(K3, E)}\bigl(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \SpCh_{K3, E}\!\bigl(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})\bigr)
 \;=\;
 \text{a chiral factorisation algebra on the curve }E,
\]
with the vertex-operator structure supported on $\mathrm{Ran}(E)$,
not on any K3-direction. The Hall--Drinfeld double construction
$\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(-)$ of
Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double} adds a comultiplication to
the $E_1$-algebra; the vertex-operator structure enters at the
Stage-$2$ specialisation. The target chirality for
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is inherited entirely from the
$\Phi_3^{(K3, E)}$-arrow and localises on the $E$-factor of the
CY-$3$-fold $K3\times E$; a compact source must preserve this
localisation under the comparison maps.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Part~(a): that $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ is associative and
\emph{not} vertex is Kontsevich--Soibelman 2011 Section~2 (the CoHA
multiplication is a convolution over the moduli of semistable
sheaves, an associative but not chiral operation). The distinction
(CoHA $\neq$ chiral) is structural: the CoHA lives in
$E_1$-algebras, the chiral algebra lives in factorisation
$E_1$-algebras on a curve; the two are distinct objects.
Part~(b): the $\Phi$-arrow's construction (Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral},
Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-platonic}) turns a CY-category input into a
chiral factorisation algebra on a curve, via the Lurie--Francis
factorisation-homology functor composed with the CY Serre functor and
the twist data. For a CY-$3$ that factorises as $K3\times E$, the
resulting chiral algebra lives on $E$; the $K3$-factor is absorbed as
a rank-$24$ coefficient system. This is the content of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classS-4d-parent} Part~(c) (the chiral
factorisation side is the Schur-sector of class-$\mathcal{S}$ on
$\Sigma_{0,24}$ reduced along the K3).
Part~(c): the Hall--Drinfeld double
$\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(-)$ is an operation on
$E_1$-bialgebras (Drinfeld 1986 Semenov-Tian-Shansky construction);
it doubles a Manin pair into a Manin-pair Drinfeld double, still in
the category of $E_1$-bialgebras. The vertex-operator structure
enters through the Stage-$2$ specialisation $\SpCh_{\Sigma_2, C}$ of
the $\Phi_3$-arrow on the $E_1$-bialgebra.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The $\Phi_3^{(\Sigma_2, C)}$-arrow carries the chiral passage]
\label{rem:kcb-phi-discipline}
The Schiffmann--Vasserot object $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ is an
$E_1$-associative algebra; its chiral image on $E$ is the
factorisation algebra
$\SpCh_{K3, E}(\PhiFA_3(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}))$ with
vertex-operator structure supported on $\mathrm{Ran}(E)$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-phi-arrow-discipline} makes this precise: every
claim in this chapter of the form ``$\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$
produces $\Delta_5$ via the BKM denominator identity'' is read as
``$\Phi_3^{(K3, E)}(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})$ produces $\Delta_5$
via the BKM denominator identity after Stage-$2$ chiralisation on
$E$''. The chart-explicit $\Phi_3^{(K3, E)}$-arrow carries the
CoHA-to-chiral passage.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Quantum determinant and the centre}
\label{subsec:kcb-quantum-determinant}

\begin{theorem}[Conditional quantum determinant of \texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{thm:kcb-quantum-determinant}
Assume the finite Hall--Borcherds recognition datum and the RTT
source-to-target comparison have been supplied, so that the target
\(T\)-matrix lifts to
\[
T(u, Z)\in\mathrm{End}(V_{24})\otimes\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}[[u^{-1}]].
\]
Let this \(T\)-matrix be built from the Lax operator associated to
$R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation}
via $R(u-v)T_1(u)T_2(v) = T_2(v)T_1(u)R(u-v)$. The \emph{quantum
determinant}
\[
 \mathrm{qdet}\,T(u, Z)
 \;:=\;
 \sum_{\sigma\in S_{24}}
 \mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\,
 T_{1\sigma(1)}(u)\,T_{2\sigma(2)}(u-\hbar)\,\cdots\,
 T_{24\,\sigma(24)}(u-23\hbar)
\]
(Molev 2007 \textup{``Yangians and Classical Lie Algebras,''}
Chapter~1, Definition~1.7.2) is central in $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ and
takes the value
\[
 \boxed{\;\mathrm{qdet}\,T(u, Z)
 \;=\; C(u)\cdot \Delta_5(Z)\cdot\mathrm{Id}\;}
\]
where $C(u)\in 1+u^{-1}\mathbb{C}[[u^{-1}]]$ is a $u$-dependent but
$Z$-independent normalisation and $\Delta_5(Z)$ is the
Gritsenko--Nikulin BKM denominator (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
Theorem~2.1). Equivalently, under the same recognition hypotheses, the
centre of the recognised object is generated by the Taylor coefficients
of \(C(u)\) and by the single Siegel-modular element \(\Delta_5(Z)\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For Yangians of classical Lie algebras, Molev 2007 Chapter~1
constructs $\mathrm{qdet}\,T(u)$ and proves centrality using the
$R$-matrix intertwining relation $R(u-v)T_1(u)T_2(v)=T_2(v)T_1(u)R(u-v)$;
the argument is a standard column-operation computation in the
matrix-algebra $\mathrm{End}(V^{\otimes n})$.
For the target datum, the $T$-matrix is not built on a finite
symmetrisable Kac--Moody Cartan (Theorem~\ref{thm:fourth-kind-quantum-group}),
so direct transcription fails; however, restricting to the rank-$24$
Mukai-Heisenberg subalgebra (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-abelian-at-lie})
gives a finite-rank target sub-\(T\)-matrix \(T^{\mathrm{Heis}}_{24}(u)\)
for which Molev's quantum determinant is well-defined.  If the
finite-source comparison lifts this target series to the completed
Hall--Drinfeld object and preserves the centre, its image after the BKM
extension via the imaginary-root Chevalley generators of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators} gives a central element
that, by the uniqueness of Siegel cusp forms of weight $5$ on the
paramodular cover with the correct vanishing on Humbert $H_1,H_4$
(Gritsenko 1999 Theorem~6.1) must equal $\Delta_5(Z)$ up to the
$u$-dependent normalisation $C(u)$. The centre thus decomposes into
the Heisenberg-rational factor (generated by $C(u)$) and the
BKM-Siegel-modular factor (generated by $\Delta_5$) only after that
centre-preserving recognition map exists.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Analogy with the Monster-BKM case]
\label{rem:kcb-monster-analogy}
For the Monster BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{m}$ of Borcherds 1992
\textup{Invent.~Math.}~109, the analogous centre element is the
Monster-function $j(\tau) - 744$, a weight-$0$ modular form of level
$1$ (Borcherds 1992 Theorem~1). The weight difference $(j \text{ has
weight } 0) \to (\Delta_5 \text{ has weight } 5)$ reflects the BKM
denominator weight: $j-744$ is the numerator-part of the Monster
denominator identity $p^{-1}\prod_{m>0,n\in\mathbb{Z}}(1-p^m q^n)^{c(mn)}$,
while $\Delta_5$ is the K3-version denominator itself. The
centrality statement sets $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ in the same genus
(centre $=$ BKM denominator function) as the Monster-VA case only under
the centre-preserving recognition map of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-quantum-determinant}.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Gritsenko additive-lift expansion through \texorpdfstring{$q^{10}$}{q-10}}
\label{subsec:kcb-gritsenko-lift}

\begin{theorem}[Gritsenko additive lift: Fourier expansion to order \texorpdfstring{$q^{10}$}{q-10}]
\label{thm:kcb-gritsenko-lift-order10}
The Gritsenko additive lift of the half-integer-index Jacobi form
$\eta^9\vartheta_1$ produces the Siegel paramodular cusp form
\[
 \Delta_5(Z)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)(Z)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{m\geq 1}\,(\eta^9\vartheta_1)\big|_{V_m}(Z)
\]
where $V_m$ is the $m$-th Hecke--Jacobi operator (Gritsenko 1995
\textup{Math.~USSR Izvestiya}~44, Theorem~1.12; Gritsenko 1999
\textup{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6, Theorem~6.1). Its Fourier
coefficients to order $q_\rho^{10}$ are computable via the compute
module. The
expansion agrees with the Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Theorem~2.1 product
side
\[
 \Delta_5
 \;=\;
 q_\rho\,q_\tau^{1/2}\,y^{1/2}\cdot
 \prod_{\substack{(n,\ell,m)>0}}
 (1 - q_\rho^n q_\tau^\ell y^m)^{c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4nm-\ell^2)}
\]
on the paramodular cover, via the Shimura--Waldspurger correspondence
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Section~4, Theorem~4.1), where
$c_{\mathrm{K3}}$ are the discriminant-indexed Fourier coefficients of
the K3 elliptic genus $\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}}$
(Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \textup{arXiv:1004.0956}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The additive-lift identity $\mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)=\Delta_5$
is Gritsenko 1999 Theorem~6.1; the proof proceeds by (i) verifying
that $\eta^9\vartheta_1$ is a Jacobi form of weight $9/2+1/2=5$ and
index $1/2$ on the Maass spin cover, (ii) constructing the Hecke--Jacobi
operators $V_m$ acting on such forms, (iii) summing and identifying
	the image with the unique Siegel cusp form of weight $5$ on the
	paramodular cover with the correct vanishing on $H_1+2H_4$.
The Borcherds multiplicative lift $\mathrm{Borch}(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}})
= \Delta_5$ is the product-side companion
(Borcherds 1998 \textup{Invent.~Math.}~132 Theorem~13.3), and the
equivalence ``additive $=$ multiplicative'' on the paramodular cover is
the Shimura--Waldspurger correspondence of
Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Section~4. The two Fourier expansions agree
after the Shimura--Waldspurger normalisation conversion.
The K3-elliptic-genus coefficient table (discriminants $-1$ through
$40$) is the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 Table~1 data, cross-checked
against Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012
\textup{arXiv:1106.4315} Table~1.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Lift discipline: Gritsenko vs.~Ikeda]
\label{rem:kcb-gritsenko-vs-ikeda}
The lift $\mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)=\Delta_5$ is
\emph{Gritsenko}, \emph{not} Ikeda. Ikeda 2001
\textup{Ann.~Math.}~154 produces \(\Delta_{10}=\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) on a
different source (weight-$16$ elliptic form to
degree-$2$ Siegel), covering \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) rather than its holomorphic
square-root $\Delta_5$. On the Maass spin cover, the square-root
\(\Delta_5=\sqrt{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}\) exists and is the Gritsenko lift of
$\eta^9\vartheta_1$; Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-gritsenko-lift-order10}
verifies this lift to order $q_\rho^{10}$.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Formal current presentation for the Super-Yangian completion problem}
\label{subsec:kcb-super-yangian-explicit}

The RTT data of the preceding subsections generates a quadratic
algebra; what the Kazhdan classical limit and the Borcherds--Drinfeld
extension $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}=\mathrm{Borch}(F_3,\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}})$
(Borcherds 1988 \emph{J.~Algebra}~115; Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997--98)
requires, for a full integrable object, more than a Drinfeld
``new realisation'' with explicit Serre--BKM current relations:
it also requires the compact Hall double, a nondegenerate Hall pairing,
PBW flatness, a compatible coproduct, centre control, and a completed
associator topology. The displayed currents give necessary finite-current
relations. Without the Hall pairing, coproduct, centre, and associator
data, the algebra is the Drinfeld--Borcherds current algebra, not the
compact Hall--Drinfeld Super-Yangian of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.

\begin{definition}[Formal Super-Yangian current algebra]
\label{def:kcb-super-yangian}
Let $F_3$ denote the hyperbolic rank-$3$ Borcherds lattice
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ with Gram matrix $G_{\mathrm{BKM}}$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}\textup{(i);}
$\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{K3}}$ the K3 weak Jacobi form of weight $0$ and index
$1$ (Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa 2011 \textup{arXiv:1004.0956}); and
$c_{\mathrm{K3}}:\mathbb{Z}\to\mathbb{Z}$ the signed Fourier coefficients
of its Borcherds lift (Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~132
Thm 13.3). A target parity fixture is a choice, for every
$\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$, of a finite homogeneous set
$B_\beta=B_\beta^{\overline{0}}\sqcup B_\beta^{\overline{1}}$ such that
\[
 \#B_\beta^{\overline{0}}-\#B_\beta^{\overline{1}}
 =
 c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-\beta^2).
\]
Equivalently, $B_\beta$ may be a homogeneous basis of the root space
obtained from a finite Hall--Borcherds recognition. Before such a fixture
or recognition, $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-\beta^2)$ is only signed
character/superdimension target data. The formal current algebra
\[
 Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})
\]
attached to this fixture is the
$\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]$-superalgebra generated by the formal currents
\begin{align*}
&\bigl\{e_i(u),\; f_i(u),\; h_i(u)\bigr\}_{i=1,2,3} & &
\text{(real-simple currents),}\\[2pt]
&\bigl\{E_\beta^{(r)}(u),\; F_\beta^{(r)}(u),\;
 H_\beta^{(r)}(u)\bigr\}_{\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}},\,
 r\in B_\beta} & &
\text{(imaginary-root currents),}
\end{align*}
where $u\in\mathbb{C}$ is the rational Yangian spectral parameter
and $\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}=\{\beta\in F_3:\beta^2\leq 0,\beta\in
\overline{\mathcal P}_{II}\}$ is Borcherds's positive imaginary cone. The
$\mathbb{Z}/2$-grading is
$|E_\beta^{(r)}|=|F_\beta^{(r)}|=|H_\beta^{(r)}|=|r|$ on imaginary
generators, extended trivially on real generators. Mode expansions are
$e_i(u)=\sum_{k\geq 0}e_{i,k}u^{-k-1}$ and analogously
for $f_i,h_i,E^{(r)}_\beta,F^{(r)}_\beta,H^{(r)}_\beta$, so that
$\hbar$-deformations enter through commutators of the modes
$\{e_{i,k}\}_{k\geq 0}$ etc. The notation
$Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ is reserved for a
flat topological quasi-Hopf completion of this current algebra after the
compact Hall--Drinfeld comparison conditions are supplied.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Super-Yangian/Hall--Drinfeld completion criterion]
\label{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}
The formal current algebra
$Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$
of Definition~\ref{def:kcb-super-yangian} promotes to a completed
quasi-Hopf superalgebra
$Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ and admits an
isomorphism
\[
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})
 \;\simeq\;
 D^\wedge_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})
\]
only under the following eight primitive conditions.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(C\arabic*)}, leftmargin=2.4em]
\item \emph{Reflection-equation sector.} The rank-$24$ Mukai form is
 treated by the orthogonal envelope
 $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4,20))$ or by its finite Hodge-parity
 $\mathbb{Z}/2$ theta-fixed extension
 $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4\mid 20))$, whose bilinear form is symmetric
 on both Hodge-parity parts. Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4\mid20)$ is only a
 comparison reflection-equation series, and
 $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{gl}(4\mid20))$ is only an ambient RTT comparison algebra.
\item \emph{Current presentation.} The Serre--BKM current relations
\textup{(Y1)}--\textup{(Y7)} hold with the signed Borcherds
superdimension data $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(4nm-\ell^2)$, the homogeneous
fixture bases $B_\beta$, the Frenkel--Kac sign cocycle $\epsilon$, and
the fixture parity $r\mapsto |r|$.
\item \emph{Hall primitive comparison.} There is a filtered morphism
 from the primitive Lie super-bialgebra of the completed Hall double to
 $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}[u]$ whose kernel is exactly the
 Borcherds--Serre ideal. Equivalently, no additional compact
 Hall classes and no orientation-data signs survive outside the
 Borcherds root spaces.
\item \emph{PBW flatness and completion.} The ordered monomials of
 Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-PBW} are linearly independent
 after completion, and
 $\operatorname{gr}Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}} \cong
 U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}[u])^\wedge$ as a cone-filtered
 $\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]$-module.
\item \emph{Coproduct and antipode.} The Hall coproduct and Drinfeld
 double pairing extend continuously to the completion, the displayed
 formulas~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-h}--\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-H}
 define an algebra morphism
 $\Delta:Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\to
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\widehat\otimes
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}$, and the antipode is continuous and
 compatible with the super sign rule.
\item \emph{Universal \(R\)-matrix convergence.} The product
 $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)$
 converges in the joint $\hbar$-adic and positive-root-height
 topology and satisfies the quasi-Hopf hexagon with the coproduct of
 \textup{(C5)}.
\item \emph{Associator topology.} The Siegel--Borcherds associator
 $\widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}
 [\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$ is a completed $K(1)$-equivariant
 super-Drinfeld associator for this same topological tensor product,
 and its pentagon holds to all orders, beyond the
 displayed order-$\hbar^3$ obstruction.
\item \emph{Denominator, reflection, and root-of-unity compatibility.}
 The reflection-equation central series maps to the
 Gritsenko--Borcherds denominator $\Delta_5$; the Borcherds
 denominator grading is preserved by the reflection-equation
 projector; and the completed object has a divided-power integral
 form stable under $\Delta$, $S$, and \(R\), so that the
 $\zeta_8$ real-root truncation and the imaginary-root tilting
 quotient are defined inside the same object.
\end{enumerate}
When \textup{(C1)}--\textup{(C2)} hold but any of
\textup{(C3)}--\textup{(C8)} is absent, the result is a formal
current presentation or a reflection-equation comparison algebra, not
the completed Hall--Drinfeld Super-Yangian.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Necessity is forced by the structures named in the conclusion.
A quasi-Hopf Hall--Drinfeld double has primitives, PBW filtration,
coproduct, antipode, universal \(R\)-matrix, associator, centre, and
integral form. Reading these data on the K3 input gives exactly
\textup{(C3)}--\textup{(C8)}; the BKM signed superdimension targets and
Frenkel--Kac sign cocycle give \textup{(C2)} only after the homogeneous
fixture or finite Hall--Borcherds recognition has turned signed
superdimension targets into source spaces; the Mukai form gives
\textup{(C1)} because it is symmetric on both Hodge-parity pieces.
Kac $\mathfrak{osp}(4\mid20)$ therefore cannot be substituted for the
Mukai-preserving envelope, and the ambient $\mathfrak{gl}(4\mid20)$
RTT algebra cannot see the Borcherds denominator.

Sufficiency is the standard Drinfeld-double construction in the
completed super-Manin-pair setting. Conditions \textup{(C3)} and
\textup{(C4)} identify the primitive and associated-graded objects with
the Borcherds current algebra; \textup{(C5)} supplies the quasi-Hopf
coalgebra and antipode; \textup{(C6)} and \textup{(C7)} give the
hexagon and pentagon; \textup{(C8)} supplies the root-of-unity integral
form and the denominator central character. The resulting completed
quasi-Hopf superalgebra has the same primitive Lie super-bialgebra,
same Hall pairing, same associator class, and same denominator central
series as $D^\wedge_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})$; by the
super-Etingof--Kazhdan uniqueness for the completed Manin pair, the two
are gauge-equivalent, hence isomorphic after fixing the Hall pairing
normalisation. This proves the criterion and isolates the obstruction:
the current relations alone do not construct the completed object.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite Mukai orthogonal boundary of the BKM current presentation]
\label{prop:kcb-finite-mukai-orthogonal-boundary}
Let $V=V_+\oplus V_-$ be the K3 Mukai space of signature
$(4,20)$, with $\dim V_+=4$, $\dim V_-=20$. Let
\(Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4,20))\) be the finite orthogonal envelope, and
let \(Y_\theta^{(4\mid20)}\), denoted
\(Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{so}(4\mid20))\) in the Hodge-parity convention, be
the theta-fixed finite-current comparison algebra of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian},
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-mukai-orthogonal-finite-closure}. Then these
finite envelopes supply the rational Mukai-Cartan boundary of
the formal BKM current algebra
$Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$:
\[
 r_{\mathrm{fin}}(u,v)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}}{u-v},
 \qquad
 \Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 =
 \sum_{a,b=1}^{24}
 \omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}^{ab}\,E_{ab}\otimes E_{ba}.
\]
The Hodge-parity trace on this boundary is
\[
 \operatorname{sdim}_{\mathrm{Muk}}(V)=4-20=-16,
 \qquad
 24^2=(-16)^2+4\cdot4\cdot20=(-16)^2+320.
\]
Under the comparison with Definition~\ref{def:kcb-super-yangian},
$r_{\mathrm{fin}}$ is the first summand of the classical
$r$-matrix~\eqref{eq:kcb-classical-rmatrix}; the remaining summand
\[
 \sum_{\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}}\sum_{r\in B_\beta}
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_\beta(u,v;Z)\,
 E_\beta^{(r)}\otimes F_\beta^{(r)}
\]
is not supplied by the finite orthogonal envelope. Consequently the
finite Mukai theorem proves the RTT/current boundary and the
Hodge-parity signs, but a completed Hall--Drinfeld/Super-Yangian
isomorphism is equivalent to the eight primitive conditions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}, and hence
to the seven structural compatibilities of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3e-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The finite orthogonal and theta-fixed envelopes are constructed in
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian},
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-mukai-orthogonal-finite-closure}. Their
quasi-classical rational term is the invariant Casimir of the Mukai
form, hence $\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}/(u-v)$. The Hodge-parity trace and
Pythagorean identity are the rank/signature calculation
\((4+20)^2=(4-20)^2+4\cdot4\cdot20\). The formal BKM current algebra
has additional generators indexed by the Borcherds imaginary cone
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$ and by the homogeneous fixture bases $B_\beta$
with signed cardinality
$\#B_\beta^{\overline{0}}-\#B_\beta^{\overline{1}}
=c_{\mathrm{K3}}(-\beta^2)$; these basis-indexed currents are the
second summand in
\eqref{eq:kcb-classical-rmatrix}. No finite type-$D_{12}$ RTT algebra
can produce those imaginary-root currents, their Hall pairing, or the
pro-Borcherds-cone completion. The Hall--Drinfeld criterion is
therefore exactly the missing coproduct, PBW, centre, and associator
datum rather than a consequence of the finite orthogonal closure.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Serre--BKM current relations]
\label{thm:kcb-super-yangian-serre-BKM}
\index{super-Yangian!K3 BKM}
\index{Borcherds axioms!Serre relations}
The current relations of
$Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$
of Definition~\ref{def:kcb-super-yangian} are the Borcherds 1988
axioms (\textup{GKM1})--(\textup{GKM5})
(\emph{J.~Algebra}~115, eqs.~6--10) deformed in the spectral
parameter $u$ by Drinfeld 1988 \textup{Soviet Math.~Dokl.}~36. Their
real-root and orthogonal-imaginary components are checked below; their
promotion to a flat completed Hall--Drinfeld object requires the
completion criterion of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
Throughout, an imaginary label $r$ means $r\in B_\beta$ for the displayed
root $\beta$, and similarly $s\in B_\gamma$; for a real root the fixture
basis is the singleton and the label is omitted.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(Y\arabic*)}, leftmargin=2.4em]
\item \textbf{Cartan--current relations.} For all $i,j\in\{1,2,3\}$ and
 all $\beta,\gamma\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$,
 \[
 [h_i(u),h_j(v)] \;=\; 0,\qquad
 [h_i(u),H_\beta^{(r)}(v)] \;=\; 0,\qquad
 [H_\beta^{(r)}(u),H_\gamma^{(s)}(v)] \;=\; 0.
 \]
\item \textbf{Real--real commutator.} For $i,j\in\{1,2,3\}$,
 \[
 [e_i(u),f_j(v)] \;=\; \hbar\,\delta_{ij}\,
 \frac{h_i(u)-h_i(v)}{u-v},
 \]
 with $h_i(u)=1+\hbar\sum_{r\geq 0}h_{i,r}u^{-r-1}$ the
 Drinfeld current.
\item \textbf{Real--imaginary commutator.} For $i\in\{1,2,3\}$,
 $\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$, $r\in B_\beta$,
 \[
 [e_i(u), F_\beta^{(r)}(v)] \;=\; 0,\qquad
 [f_i(u), E_\beta^{(r)}(v)] \;=\; 0
 \]
 (Borcherds axiom \textup{(GKM3)}: no pairing between real and
 imaginary Chevalley triples of distinct type).
\item \textbf{Imaginary--imaginary commutator (bilinear).} For
 $\beta,\gamma\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$ with
 $\langle\beta,\gamma\rangle=0$,
 \[
 [E_\beta^{(r)}(u),E_\gamma^{(s)}(v)\}
 \;=\; 0,\qquad
 [F_\beta^{(r)}(u),F_\gamma^{(s)}(v)\} \;=\; 0
 \]
 (Borcherds axiom \textup{(GKM5)} / super-graded $[\cdot,\cdot\}$),
 where $[\cdot,\cdot\}$ denotes the super-commutator consistent with
 the fixture parity $|E_\beta^{(r)}|=|r|$.
\item \textbf{Imaginary--imaginary Cartan action.} For
 $\beta,\gamma\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$,
 \[
 [H_\beta^{(r)}(u),E_\gamma^{(s)}(v)]
 \;=\;
 \hbar\,\langle\beta,\gamma\rangle\,
 \frac{E_\gamma^{(s)}(u)-E_\gamma^{(s)}(v)}{u-v},\quad\;
 [E_\beta^{(r)}(u),F_\gamma^{(s)}(v)\}
 \;=\; \hbar\,\delta_{\beta,\gamma}\,\delta_{rs}\,
 \frac{H_\beta^{(r)}(u)-H_\beta^{(r)}(v)}{u-v}.
 \]
\item \textbf{Standard Serre on real simples.} For $i\neq j$,
 $a_{ij} = -2$ from the Gram matrix $G_{\mathrm{BKM}}$, and
 $1-a_{ij} = 3$:
 \[
 \mathrm{Sym}_{u_1,u_2,u_3}\Bigl[
 e_i(u_1),[e_i(u_2),[e_i(u_3),e_j(v)]]\Bigr] \;=\; 0,
 \]
 and analogously for $f_i$ (Drinfeld 1988 eq.~2.12--2.15; Kac 1990
 \emph{Infinite-dimensional Lie Algebras} Thm 4.3).
\item \textbf{Borcherds imaginary Serre.} For $\beta$ imaginary with
 $\langle\beta,\beta\rangle\leq 0$ and $\gamma$ any simple root, with
 the index $s$ omitted when $\gamma$ is real,
 \[
 \bigl[E_\beta^{(r)}(u), E_\gamma^{(s)}(v)\bigr\}
 \;=\; 0
 \quad\text{whenever}\quad \langle\beta,\gamma\rangle = 0
 \]
 (Borcherds 1988 axiom \textup{(GKM4)}), and the non-vanishing bracket
 for $\langle\beta,\gamma\rangle\neq 0$ is
 \[
 [E_\beta^{(r)}(u), E_\gamma^{(s)}(v)\}
 \;=\; \hbar\,
 C^{\beta+\gamma,(r,s,t)}_{\beta,\gamma}(u,v)\,
 E_{\beta+\gamma}^{(t)}(w),
 \]
 with $w=(\beta\cdot u+\gamma\cdot v)/(\beta+\gamma)$ and
 $C^{\beta+\gamma,(r,s,t)}_{\beta,\gamma}(u,v) \in
 \mathbb{C}[u,v,(u-v)^{-1}]$ the rational OPE coefficient determined
 by the Borcherds sign cocycle
 $\epsilon\colon\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\times\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}
 \to\{\pm 1\}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-presentation-frenkel-kac-map}
 of Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian}.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(Y1)--(Y2) are the standard Drinfeld new-realisation relations
(Drinfeld 1988 \emph{Sov.~Math.~Dokl.}~36 eqs.~2.4--2.11) specialised
to the real $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-triples $(e_i,f_i,h_i)$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}(i) with Cartan matrix
$A_{\mathrm{BKM}}=G_{\mathrm{BKM}}$. (Y3) realises Borcherds axiom
(GKM3) in the finite-current algebra: distinct-type Chevalley triples commute.
(Y4) and the orthogonal part of (Y7) are the Borcherds--Serre
vanishing relations. (Y5) and the non-orthogonal
closure term in (Y7) are the finite-current compatibility conditions
forced by the compact Hall comparison, not an independent construction
of the completed double. In the orthogonal case
the $\hbar$-deformation of the trivial-bracket case
$\langle\beta,\gamma\rangle=0$ vanishes identically because the
classical $r$-matrix restricted to the orthogonal imaginary-current
sector is the abelian trace form
$r^{\mathrm{Heis}}(u-v) = \hbar\cdot\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{im}}}/(u-v)$
with $\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{im}}}$ the auxiliary Mukai trace
tensor (Volume~I
\cite{VolI},
Heisenberg row, level-prefix discipline). (Y6) is Drinfeld 1988
eq.~2.12 (the ``Serre relation with three spectral parameters''
symmetrised over $u_1,u_2,u_3$), which reduces to the classical
$(\mathrm{ad}\,e_i)^3 e_j=0$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-chevalley-bkm-explicit} in the limit
$\hbar\to 0$. (Y7) is Borcherds 1988 axiom (GKM4) at the current
level: imaginary-to-any brackets in the orthogonal case vanish; in
the non-orthogonal case they close on $E_{\beta+\gamma}^{(t)}$ with
the OPE coefficient $C^{\beta+\gamma,(r,s,t)}_{\beta,\gamma}(u,v)$
determined by the Frenkel--Kac sign cocycle $\epsilon$ on
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}\times\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (Frenkel--Kac
1980 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~62; Borcherds 1986 \emph{Proc.~Nat.~Acad.~Sci.}~83).
Verification of (Y7) at rank $3$: $\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$ intersects
$\{\beta:\beta^2=0\}$ in $10$ orbits under the Weyl group
$W(F_3)$, giving $c_{\mathrm{K3}}(0)=10$ as the lightlike target
superdimension (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 eq.~(1.18)); the closure of (Y7) on these
$10$ orbits is the shuffle closure of the
Schiffmann--Vasserot CoHA on $K3\times E$ at the $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ core,
Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}, conditional on the
Hall-to-Borcherds bracket map and orientation data named in
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}. Primary:
Drinfeld 1988
\textup{Sov.~Math.~Dokl.}~36 (new realisation); Borcherds 1988
\textup{J.~Algebra}~115 (GKM axioms); Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\textup{Duke~Math.~J.}~94 (rank-$3$ BKM data); Frenkel--Kac 1980
\textup{Invent.~Math.}~62 (sign cocycle).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[PBW normal-ordering criterion]
\label{prop:kcb-super-yangian-PBW}
Spanning by ordered monomials follows from the current relations.
Linear independence and flatness require the compact Hall PBW
comparison of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
Fix a total order $\leq_\Delta$ on the BKM positive roots
$\Delta_+(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ compatible with the height function
$\mathrm{ht}:\Delta_+\to\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$ (e.g.\ Humphreys 1990
\textup{Reflection Groups and Coxeter Groups} \S5). For each positive
root $\alpha$, let $B_\alpha$ be the singleton for a real root and the
homogeneous basis supplied by the fixture or finite Hall--Borcherds
recognition for an imaginary root space; order generator indices
lexicographically by mode index, fixture index $r$, and root $\alpha$.
Then the ordered monomials
\[
 \prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+,\,r\in B_\alpha,\,s\geq 0}
 (\;e_{\alpha,s}^{(r)}\;)^{n^+_{\alpha,r,s}}
 \;\cdot\;
 \prod_{i=1,2,3,\,s\geq 0}(h_{i,s})^{m_{i,s}}
 \;\cdot\;
 \prod_{\alpha\in\Delta_+,\,r\in B_\alpha,\,s\geq 0}
 (\;f_{\alpha,s}^{(r)}\;)^{n^-_{\alpha,r,s}}
\]
with $n^\pm_{\alpha,r,s}\in\{0,1\}$ for fermionic $(\alpha,r)$ (i.e.\
$|r|=1$) and $n^\pm_{\alpha,r,s}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}$ otherwise,
span
$Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ as a left
module over the polynomial subalgebra
$\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]][h_{i,s}, H_{\beta,s}^{(r)}]$. They form a
$\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]$-basis of the completed
$Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ exactly when the
PBW-filtration and completion conditions of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion} hold.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The Serre--BKM relations (Y1)--(Y7) are normal-ordering relations
expressing any monomial as a $\mathbb{C}[[\hbar]]$-linear combination
of ordered monomials, inductively on the standard Drinfeld filtration
by $u$-degree (Drinfeld 1988 Prop~2.3; Nazarov 1991
\emph{Lett.~Math.~Phys.}~21). The super refinement is Zelmanov--Shestakov
1999 \emph{J.~Algebra}~213: the PBW theorem for Lie superalgebras
replaces polynomial powers with Grassmann-odd exterior factors at
fermionic generators (parity rule of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators}(iii)). The Borcherds
extension is Ray 2004 \emph{J.~Algebra}~273 \S3: the PBW property
survives imaginary simple roots provided the Borcherds axioms (GKM1)--(GKM5)
are imposed (which they are, by
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-serre-BKM}) on the associated
graded. What remains is linear independence in the completed Hall
quotient: no extra primitive Hall generators and no extra Hall relations
beyond the Borcherds--Serre ideal. This is condition \textup{(ii)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}. The flatness
in $\hbar$ would then be the Drinfeld flat-deformation argument (Drinfeld 1989
\emph{Leningrad Math.~J.}~1): the rational $r$-matrix is quasi-classical
so the quantisation is formal-Hopf-flat (cf.\ Etingof--Kazhdan 1996--2008
\emph{Selecta~Math.}~I--V, super-case Part V).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Coproduct on the Cartan current]
\label{thm:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct}
\index{Yangian coproduct!super-BKM}
Assume the formal current algebra of
Definition~\ref{def:kcb-super-yangian} admits the completed
Hall--Drinfeld comparison datum of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}. Then its
completion $Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ is a
$\mathbb{Z}/2$-graded topological quasi-Hopf superalgebra with coproduct
$\Delta:Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\to
Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\widehat{\otimes}Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}$
specified on real-simple Cartan currents by
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-h}
 \Delta\bigl(h_i(u)\bigr)
 \;=\; h_i(u)\otimes 1 \;+\; 1\otimes h_i(u)
 \;+\; \hbar\,\sum_{\alpha\in\Delta_+}\sum_{r\in B_\alpha}
 (-1)^{|r|}\,\langle\alpha,\delta_i\rangle\,
 \bigl(e_\alpha^{(r)}(u)\otimes f_\alpha^{(r)}(u)\bigr)
 \;+\; O(\hbar^2),
\end{equation}
with the sum running over positive BKM roots $\alpha=\sum_j n_j\delta_j
+\sum_{\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}} m_\beta\beta$ and
$e_\alpha^{(r)}(u),f_\alpha^{(r)}(u)$ the root vectors of height
$\mathrm{ht}(\alpha)$ constructed by iterated (Y4)--(Y7) from the
Chevalley currents $(e_i,f_i,e^{(r)}_\beta,f^{(r)}_\beta)$. On imaginary
Cartan currents indexed by $\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$ and
$r\in B_\beta$,
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-H}
 \Delta\bigl(H_\beta^{(r)}(u)\bigr)
 \;=\; H_\beta^{(r)}(u)\otimes 1
 \;+\; 1\otimes H_\beta^{(r)}(u)
 \;+\; \hbar\,\beta^{\vee\,(r)}\!\cdot\!
 \bigl(E_\beta^{(r)}(u)\otimes F_\beta^{(r)}(u)
 +(-1)^{|r|}\,F_\beta^{(r)}(u)\otimes E_\beta^{(r)}(u)\bigr)
 + O(\hbar^2),
\end{equation}
where $\beta^{\vee\,(r)}\in\mathbb{C}$ is the imaginary-coroot
normalisation of Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 eq.~(1.17). The
counit $\epsilon$ and antipode $S$ are determined by
$\epsilon\bigl(e_i(u)\bigr)=\epsilon\bigl(f_i(u)\bigr)=0$,
$\epsilon\bigl(h_i(u)\bigr)=1$, $S\bigl(h_i(u)\bigr)=-h_i(u)+O(\hbar)$,
extended $\mathbb{Z}/2$-graded-algebra-morphism-wise.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Coassociativity and the Hopf/quasi-Hopf axioms would follow from the Drinfeld-double
construction (Drinfeld 1987 \emph{Berkeley ICM}; Chari--Pressley 1994
\emph{Guide to Quantum Groups} \S12.2) only after the Hall pairing,
coproduct, Serre ideal, centre, and completion compatibilities have been
supplied. Applied to the Manin pair
$(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}, \mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}\oplus
\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}})$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} \S2.1, this gives the
conditional completed coproduct. The $\hbar$-leading
term~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-h} matches the standard
Drinfeld Yangian $Y_\hbar(\mathfrak{g})$ coproduct (Drinfeld 1985
\emph{Sov.~Math.~Dokl.}~32 eq.~3.2) on the real-simple sector; the
imaginary correction~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-H} is
fixed by requiring $\hbar$-linear Sklyanin exchange
$\Delta(H_\beta^{(r)})\,R=R\,\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}(H_\beta^{(r)})$
with $R$ the quasi-triangular universal $R$-matrix of
Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-quasitriangular}. The parity
sign $(-1)^{|r|}$ in~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct-H} is
the super-exchange required by
Definition~\ref{def:kcb-super-yangian} and by the super-Yangian
conventions of Nazarov 1991 and Arnaudon--Cramp\'e--Doikou--Frappat--Ragoucy
2003 \textup{arXiv:math/0310420} \S2.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Universal quasi-triangular \texorpdfstring{$R$}{R}-matrix]
\label{prop:kcb-super-yangian-quasitriangular}
The rational and theta factors below are fixed by the finite
stable-envelope and Siegel-theta data; their interpretation as a
universal \(R\)-matrix requires the completed quasi-Hopf object.
Under the hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct}, the quasi-Hopf superalgebra
$Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct} is quasi-triangular with
universal $R$-matrix
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kcb-super-yangian-R-universal}
 R(u)
 \;=\;
 R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)\;\cdot\;\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)
 \;\in\;
 \bigl(Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\widehat{\otimes}
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\bigr)[[u^{-1}]],
\end{equation}
where $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)=1+\hbar\,\Omega/u$ is the
rational Yang solution for the rank-$24$ Mukai--Heisenberg Casimir
$\Omega$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation}(a)) and
$\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)$ is the K3 Siegel theta cocycle
(Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation}(b)). The object $R(u)$
satisfies the hexagon axioms
$(\Delta\otimes\mathrm{id})R = R_{13}R_{23}$,
$(\mathrm{id}\otimes\Delta)R = R_{13}R_{12}$, the quasi-classical
limit $R(u) = 1 + \hbar\,r(u) + O(\hbar^2)$ with
$r(u) = r^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u) +
\hbar\,r^{\mathrm{K3\text{-}imag}}(u,Z)$ the Kazhdan classical
$r$-matrix of Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-classical-limit-kazhdan} below.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The rational factor $R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)$ is the
standard Yang solution for a Heisenberg sector (Yang 1967; Drinfeld
1985 eqs.~2.7--2.8); its coassociativity on the real-simple sector is
Drinfeld 1988 Prop~2.4. The theta factor $\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)$
satisfies the shifted pentagon
(Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation} proof)
which, by Etingof--Schiffmann 1998
\textup{Lectures on quantum groups} Ch.~9, is equivalent to the
hexagon for the product~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-R-universal} once
the rational factor satisfies the hexagon. At the order-eight
root-of-unity branch, the hexagon
reduces to a finite-sum identity verifiable on
$\bigoplus_{|\beta|^2\leq 10}V_\beta^{\otimes 2}$ through the
Gritsenko additive-lift expansion
(Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-gritsenko-lift-order10}). These verifications fix
the candidate \(R\)-matrix. Universality as an element of
\((Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\widehat\otimes
Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}})[[u^{-1}]]\) is precisely the completion and
associator condition \textup{(vii)} of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\hbar^3$}{hbar-3} pentagon for the quasi-Hopf completion]
\label{thm:kcb-super-yangian-hbar3-pentagon}
\index{pentagon identity!$\hbar^3$}
Order-\(\hbar^3\) pentagon closure is checked for the
Siegel--Borcherds associator in
Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}. Its use as Hopf/quasi-Hopf
associativity for \(Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}\) requires the completed
object. Assume the quasi-Hopf completion
$Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$ exists. Its
associativity constraint is encoded in the
super-Drinfeld associator
$\widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}), and is equivalent to the
pentagon identity at order $\hbar^3$:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kcb-super-yangian-hbar3-pentagon}
 \Phi_{12,3,4}\,\Phi_{1,2,34}
 \;=\;
 \Phi_{2,3,4}\,\Phi_{1,23,4}\,\Phi_{1,2,3},
\end{equation}
evaluated modulo $\hbar^4$, where
$\Phi=\widetilde\Phi^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}_{\mathrm{Sp}_4}$ is the
paramodular $K(1)$-equivariant Drinfeld associator of the chapter's
Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}, with cocycle data
$[\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}]$ agreeing at order $\hbar^3$ with the
$\widetilde{M}_{24}$-umbral order-$6$ Schur cocycle
(Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}). The conditional coproduct and
antipode data of Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-coproduct},
together with \eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-hbar3-pentagon}, are then
the quasi-Hopf algebra axioms of Drinfeld (Drinfeld 1989
\emph{Algebra i Analiz}~1) with
$(\Phi,R)$ satisfying the combined hexagon+pentagon constraint.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The $\hbar^3$ pentagon is the general Drinfeld--Etingof--Kazhdan
obstruction for quantisation of a Manin pair to an associative
quasi-Hopf algebra (Etingof--Kazhdan 2008 Part V, Thm 4.3). On the
present Manin pair, the obstruction class lies in
$H^3(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\mathbb{C})^{K(1)}\cong\mathbb{C}\cdot
