\Delta_5$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-chiral-bialgebra-platonic} \S2.1 and
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-classification-etingof}); the agreement of the
associator $\Phi$ with this one-dimensional obstruction at leading
order is equivalent to~\eqref{eq:kcb-super-yangian-hbar3-pentagon},
and is the content of Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor}. The
$\widetilde{M}_{24}$-umbral order-$6$ cross-check is
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle} of the chapter.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Classical current limit and Kazhdan Lie bialgebra]
\label{thm:kcb-classical-limit-kazhdan}
\index{classical limit!Kazhdan}
At $\hbar=0$, the formal current algebra
$Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})$
degenerates to the universal enveloping superalgebra
$U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}[u])$, where
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}[u] = \mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}\otimes
\mathbb{C}[u]$ is the polynomial loop superalgebra. The Kazhdan
Lie-bialgebra structure requires the same completed \(r\)-matrix and
Hall comparison as above: if the completed
Hall--Drinfeld comparison supplies the conditional \(R\)-matrix of
Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-quasitriangular}, this current
algebra carries the Kazhdan--Etingof Lie bialgebra cobracket
$\delta^{\mathrm{K3}}$
(Etingof--Kazhdan 2008 Part~V) determined by the classical
$r$-matrix
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:kcb-classical-rmatrix}
 r^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,v)
 \;=\;
 \frac{\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}}{u-v}
 \;+\;
 \sum_{\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}}\sum_{r\in B_\beta}
 F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_\beta(u,v;Z)\,
 E_\beta^{(r)}\otimes F_\beta^{(r)},
\end{equation}
with $\Omega_{\mathrm{Muk}}\in\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}\otimes
\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ the rank-$24$ Mukai Cartan Casimir and
$F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_\beta(u,v;Z)$ the Pasol--Zagier Siegel--Kronecker
kernel (Pasol--Zagier 2013 \textup{arXiv:1309.4883} Thm~3.2) specialised
to $\beta\in\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$. The cobracket
$\delta^{\mathrm{K3}}$ satisfies the Drinfeld--Jacobi (co-Jacobi)
identity and the classical Yang--Baxter equation
$[r_{12},r_{13}]+[r_{12},r_{23}]+[r_{13},r_{23}]=0$, verified on the
hyperbolic core $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$ via the Kazhdan--Etingof
super-Manin-pair construction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Passage to $\hbar=0$ in relations (Y1)--(Y7) of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-serre-BKM} yields the classical
Chevalley--Serre--Borcherds relations of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3yang-chevalley-bkm-explicit} on
$U(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}[u])$. The conditional classical
$r$-matrix~\eqref{eq:kcb-classical-rmatrix} is read off the
$\hbar^1$-term of the universal $R$-matrix
of Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-quasitriangular}:
$R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}(u)-1 = \hbar\,\Omega/(u-v)$ gives
the rank-$24$ Mukai--Heisenberg rational part, and
$\log\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}(u,Z)/\hbar =
F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}(u,Z)\cdot\Omega_{\mathrm{K3}}$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation}(b)) expands through the
Siegel--Kronecker series $F^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_\beta$ on the
imaginary-root orbits $\beta\in\Lambda_{\mathrm{im}}$.
Non-vanishing of the CYBE on this $r$-matrix is the Belavin--Drinfeld
classical verification (Belavin--Drinfeld 1982 \textup{Funct.~Anal.~Appl.}~16)
extended to the Borcherds case via Etingof--Kazhdan 2008 Part~V: on
the super-Manin-pair $(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5},\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}
\oplus\mathfrak{h}^{\mathrm{imag,rk\,23}})$ the classical
$r$-matrix~\eqref{eq:kcb-classical-rmatrix} is
skew-symmetric on $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}/\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{imag}}$
and its $\mathfrak{h}_{\mathrm{imag}}$-projection is the trivial
abelian part, hence the CYBE is a consequence of the
Schiffmann--Vasserot shuffle associativity on the $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}$
core
(Theorem~\ref{thm:schiffmann-vasserot-shuffle}) after the Hall
pairing and completion conditions have been supplied.
Primary: Drinfeld 1983 \textup{Sov.~Math.~Dokl.}~27 (classical
$r$-matrix); Belavin--Drinfeld 1982 \textup{Funct.~Anal.~Appl.}~16
(CYBE classification); Etingof--Kazhdan 2008 Part~V (super
Manin-pair quantisation, K3 super bialgebra).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Current presentation versus the full Super-Yangian]
\label{rem:kcb-super-yangian-closure}
Theorems~\ref{thm:kcb-super-yangian-serre-BKM}--\ref{thm:kcb-classical-limit-kazhdan}
and Propositions~\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-PBW},
\ref{prop:kcb-super-yangian-quasitriangular} define the current
presentation and its required compatibility data. The checked finite
content is: the real-root Drinfeld current sector, the classical
Borcherds--Serre vanishing relations, the signed root superdimension
targets from Gritsenko--Nikulin, the parity character from the Maass spin
cover, and the factorised candidate
\(R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}\) on finite
Mukai/imaginary truncations. The following assertions require
the compact Hall double datum of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}: PBW
linear independence in the completed quotient, the Hopf or quasi-Hopf
coproduct, the universal \(R\)-matrix, the use of the
\(\hbar^3\)-pentagon as associativity for the completed object, and the
identification
\[
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5})
 \;\cong\;
 D^\wedge_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}).
\]
Thus the open conjecture of Remark~\ref{rem:bkm-yangian-framework} of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian} is sharpened but not closed: the formal
current algebra \(Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}\) is explicit,
while the full Super-Yangian/Hall--Drinfeld double is the completion
problem.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Synthesis: the RTT face under Hall--Drinfeld completion}
\label{subsec:kcb-rtt-summary}

Five statements constitute the RTT face of the Hall--Drinfeld double
after the compact completion data are supplied.
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-chevalley-bkm-generators} replaces Drinfeld's
$\{e_i, f_i, h_{i,r}\}$ by BKM-adapted
$\{e_{\delta_i}, f_{\delta_i}, h_{\delta_i}\}\cup\{e^{(r)}_{\alpha},
f^{(r)}_{\alpha}, h^{(r)}_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\text{ imag}}$;
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-r-sieg-factorisation} replaces Yang's $R(u)=1+\hbar P/u$ by
$R^{\mathrm{rat}}_{\mathrm{Yang}}\cdot\theta^{\mathrm{K3}}$;
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-quantum-determinant} generalises Molev's
$\mathrm{qdet}\,T(u) \in Z(Y(\mathfrak{gl}_n))$ to
$\mathrm{qdet}\,T(u,Z) \propto \Delta_5(Z)\cdot\mathrm{Id}$; and
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-gritsenko-lift-order10} verifies the BKM denominator
identity numerically through $q_\rho^{10}$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-phi-arrow-discipline} enforces the structural hygiene
preventing any of the above from misreading as a ``CoHA vertex algebra'':
every chiral structure here is the $\Phi$-image of an associative CoHA structure.

\section{The ordered convolution dGLA \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$}{C-H-Delta-5}}
\label{sec:kcb-convolution-dgla}

The target obstruction
$\Theta_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$ that controls deformation after
source-to-target comparison lives in an explicit differential graded Lie
algebra built from the target bar coalgebra and the BKM differential. We
name it here and match it to the Vol.~I
bar-side convolution dGLA of
\cite{VolI}.

\begin{definition}[Ordered convolution dGLA \texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$}{C-H-Delta-5};
K3 incarnation]
\label{def:kcb-convolution-dgla}
With $A = \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$, bar coalgebra
$B^{\mathrm{ord}}(A) = T^c(s^{-1}\bar A)$, and reduced deconcatenation
coproduct $\bar\Delta$, the \emph{ordered convolution dGLA} is
\[
 \mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}
 \;:=\;
 \mathrm{Hom}_{\Bbbk}\!\bigl(B^{\mathrm{ord}}(\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}),\,
 \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}\bigr),
\]
working in the weight-completed pro-category with respect to the
Mukai-lattice grading $\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
(so that each graded weight piece is finite-dimensional). The dGLA
structure is:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)}]
\item \emph{Generators.} A basis for the homogeneous piece
 $\mathfrak{C}^p_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$ of cohomological degree $p$
 consists of pairs $(w\mapsto y)$ with
 $w = s^{-1}x_{i_1}\otimes\cdots\otimes s^{-1}x_{i_n}\in
 B^{\mathrm{ord}}_n$, each $x_{i_k}$ a BKM generator
 $e_\alpha, f_\alpha$, or $h_\alpha$, and
 $y\in\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ of degree shift $p$. The three real-root
 Chevalley triples $(e_{\alpha_j}, f_{\alpha_j}, h_{\alpha_j})$
 for $j\in\{-1,0,1\}$ (the $\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$-hyperbolic triple of
 Nikulin~1995; the $\mathfrak{sl}_2$-triple at each real simple root)
 generate the $9$-dimensional real-root subalgebra; the imaginary
 simple roots index one signed target root space per positive Fourier
 mode $(n,\ell)$ with superdimension $c_{\Delta_5}(n,\ell)$, the
 Gritsenko--Nikulin coefficient of $\Delta_5$. Actual cochain generators
 are indexed by the homogeneous fixture basis of that root space
 (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1997 \S 2.4, denominator identity).
\item \emph{Differential.}
 $(D\phi)(w) = d_A(\phi(w)) - (-1)^p \phi(d_B w)$,
 where $d_A$ is the target BKM differential for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$
 (Chevalley--Eilenberg of $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ lifted through
 Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation to
 $\mathcal{D}_\hbar(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}},
 \widetilde{\Phi}^{\mathrm{Sieg\text{-}Bor}}, R_{\mathrm{Sieg,dyn}})$)
 and $d_B = d_{\mathrm{Hall}} + d_{\mathrm{Sieg}} +
 \hbar d_{\mathrm{assoc}} + \hbar^2 d_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}}$ is the
 four-piece bar differential of Vol.~I, bar chapter synthesis
 (\cite{VolI}).
\item \emph{Bracket.}
 $[\phi,\psi](w) = \mu(\phi\otimes\psi)\bar\Delta(w) - (-1)^{pq}
 \mu(\psi\otimes\phi)\bar\Delta(w)$,
 using the target product $\mu$ of
 $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$ (Hall--Drinfeld double product,
 Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texorpdfstring{$\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$}{C-H-Delta-5} is a dGLA]
\label{thm:kcb-convolution-dgla-axioms}
The structure $(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}, D,
[\cdot,\cdot])$ of Definition~\ref{def:kcb-convolution-dgla} is a
differential graded Lie algebra: $D^2 = 0$, $[\cdot,\cdot]$ is
super-antisymmetric and satisfies super-Jacobi, and $D$ is a
derivation of the bracket.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The argument is \cite{VolI} of
\cite{VolI}, applied verbatim to
$A = \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}^{\mathrm{tgt}}$. The inputs are:
$(d_A)^2 = 0$ by Borcherds 1988 \S 4 (Jacobi for
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ extended to its Etingof--Kazhdan quantum
enveloping algebra via the Hall--Drinfeld double of
Definition~\ref{def:hall-drinfeld-double}); $(d_B)^2 = 0$ by
coassociativity of $\bar\Delta$ combined with the Arnold relation
on $\mathrm{Conf}_n(X)$; super-Jacobi for $[\cdot,\cdot]$ from
associativity of the cup $\phi\smile\psi = \mu(\phi\otimes\psi)\bar\Delta$
(Loday--Vallette 2012 \S 1.1.11); Leibniz for $D$ from
$d_A$-compatibility of $\mu$ (quasi-Hopf chain-map property) and
$d_B$-co-compatibility with $\bar\Delta$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Target Maurer--Cartan element for
\texorpdfstring{$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$}{H-Delta-5}]
\label{thm:kcb-universal-MC-element}
The ordered convolution dGLA
$\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}[[\hbar]]$ carries a
distinguished element
\[
 \Theta_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}
 \;=\; \sum_{n\ge 3}\hbar^n\,\phi^{(n)}
 \;\in\; \mathfrak{C}^1_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}[[\hbar]]
\]
encoding the super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation obstructions of
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$. The leading coefficient at $\hbar^3$ is
\[
 \phi^{(3)} \;=\; \zeta(3)\,c_{\mathrm{symm}}
 \;+\; \tfrac{25}{3}\,c_{\mathrm{timelike}}
 \;+\; (\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24})\, c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}},
\]
matching the pentagon $\hbar^3$-correction of
Theorem~\ref{thm:pentagon-sieg-bor} (this chapter) and the explicit
expansion of Remark~\ref{rem:siegel-borcherds-explicit-expansion}.
The Maurer--Cartan equation
\[
 D\,\Theta_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}
 \;+\; \tfrac{1}{2}\,[\Theta_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}},
 \Theta_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}]
 \;=\; 0
\]
holds modulo $\hbar^5$, and the first nontrivial higher coherence at
$\hbar^6$ is the super-Jacobi
$D\phi^{(6)} = -\tfrac{1}{2}[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(3)}]$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
\emph{$\hbar^0,\hbar^1,\hbar^2$ terms.} Vacuous because $\Theta$
starts at $\hbar^3$.

\emph{$\hbar^3$ equation: $D\phi^{(3)} = 0$.} Each of the three
summands is $D$-closed individually. The coboundary
$c_{\mathrm{symm}}$ is the symmetric-triple-product coboundary
$[[x,[x,y]],y] + [[y,[y,x]],x]$ in
$C^3(\widehat{\mathfrak{t}^{\mathrm{Sieg}}_{2,[2]}\oplus
\mathfrak{n}_+^{\mathrm{imag}}})$, $D$-closed by Jacobi
(the same step as in Drinfeld 1990 for the KZ associator).
The coboundary $c_{\mathrm{timelike}}$ is the timelike-triple coboundary
whose $D$-closedness reduces to the rank-$25$ Lorentzian anomaly of
$\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$ vanishing on the Fake Monster quotient
(Borcherds 1990 \textup{Adv.~Math.}~83, \S 3). The coboundary
$c_{\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}}$ is the modular coboundary whose $D$-closedness is
the cocycle property of the $\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}$ ratio in
Lie-bialgebra cohomology (Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998, Theorem~2.1).

\emph{$\hbar^4$ equation:
$D\phi^{(4)} + \tfrac{1}{2}[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(3)}]_{\hbar^4} = 0$.}
The bracket $[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(3)}]$ has total $\hbar$-degree
$3+3 = 6$, so its $\hbar^4$-component vanishes. Consequently
$D\phi^{(4)} = 0$; the even-only pentagon-cocycle support
(Etingof--Kazhdan 1996 second paper, Proposition 3.2) forces
$\phi^{(4)} = 0$ as an explicit solution.

\emph{$\hbar^5$ equation.} Analogously $[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(3)}]_{\hbar^5}$
and $[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(4)}]_{\hbar^5}$ both vanish (the former by
degree counting, the latter because $\phi^{(4)}=0$), so
$D\phi^{(5)} = 0$ with $\phi^{(5)} = 0$.

\emph{$\hbar^6$ first nontrivial coherence.} The MC equation reads
$D\phi^{(6)} + \tfrac{1}{2}[\phi^{(3)},\phi^{(3)}]_{\hbar^6} = 0$.
The right side is a genuinely nonzero element of
$\mathrm{im}(D)\subset \mathfrak{C}^2_{\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}}$; expanding
by the trilinearity of $[\cdot,\cdot]$ and using MZV shuffle
identities (Brown 2012 \textup{Ann.~Math.}~175 \S 3 for
$\zeta(3)^2 = 2\zeta(6)/\text{Bern coefficients}$; Borcherds product
shuffle identities in Borcherds 1995 \textup{Invent.~Math.}~120 \S 8)
the six-term MZV--Borcherds-leg decomposition
\[
 \phi^{(6)} \;=\; \zeta(3)^2\, c_6^{(1)} \;+\;
 \tfrac{25}{3}\,\zeta(3)\, c_6^{(2)} \;+\;
 \bigl(\tfrac{25}{3}\bigr)^2 c_6^{(3)} \;+\;
 \zeta(3)\bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}\bigr) c_6^{(4)} \;+\;
 \bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}/\eta^{24}\bigr)^2 c_6^{(5)} \;+\;
 \zeta(5)\, c_6^{(6)}
\]
solves the equation with the six $c_6^{(i)}$ specified by the
EGGM 2022 $C^3\to C^4$ coboundary ladder (Theorem~4.3 loc.\ cit.).
The last summand $\zeta(5)\,c_6^{(6)}$ is the Deligne--Brown
depth-$1$ MZV entering at weight $5$; its class spans the weight-$5$
indecomposable quotient.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Maurer--Cartan comparison datum through weight $12$]
\label{def:kcb-mc-comparison-weight12}
Let \((\mathfrak C,D,[-,-])\) be the weight-completed convolution dg
Lie algebra of Definition~\ref{def:kcb-convolution-dgla}.  For
\(3\leq n\leq12\), put
\[
 R_n=-\frac12\sum_{\substack{i+j=n\\i,j\geq3}}
 [\phi^{(i)},\phi^{(j)}].
\]
A Maurer--Cartan comparison datum
\(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{MC}}^{\leq12}\) consists of:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item cochains \(\phi^{(3)},\ldots,\phi^{(11)}\) satisfying
\(D\phi^{(n)}=R_n\) for \(n\leq11\);
\item a closed cochain \(R_{12}\in\mathfrak C^2_{12}\) and a cochain
\(\phi^{(12)}\in\mathfrak C^1_{12}\) satisfying
\(DR_{12}=0\) and \(D\phi^{(12)}=R_{12}\);
\item closed graph cochains \(\Gamma_n\), Borcherds coefficient vectors
\(b_n\), cochain maps
\[
 \operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{GC}}:\mathrm{GC}_2^{\leq12}\to\mathfrak C,
 \qquad
 \operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{Bor}}:\mathcal B^{\leq12}\to\mathfrak C,
\]
and homotopies \(K_n\) with
\[
 DK_n=\operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{GC}}(\Gamma_n)
      -\operatorname{Cmp}_{\mathrm{Bor}}(b_n);
\]
\item a primitive comparison
\[
 \operatorname{cmp}^{\mathrm{prim}}_{12}:
 \mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12}\longrightarrow H^1(\mathfrak C_{12},D)
\]
compatible with the two cochain maps.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Maurer--Cartan closure through weight
\texorpdfstring{$12$}{12} under comparison data]
\label{thm:kcb-MC-hbar7-hbar12}
This is a level-$3$, Humbert-complement statement in the
weight-completed pro-chain ambient.  Let
\(U\subset\overline{\mathcal A}_2\) be the Humbert complement used
by the strict quasi-Hopf chart.  Assume
\(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{MC}}^{\leq12}\).  Then
\[
 \Theta_{\leq12}=\sum_{n=3}^{12}\hbar^n\phi^{(n)}
\]
satisfies
\[
 D\Theta_{\leq12}
 +\frac12[\Theta_{\leq12},\Theta_{\leq12}]
 \equiv0\pmod{\hbar^{13}}
\]
in \(\mathfrak C|_U\).  The motivic coefficient space has
\(d_{11}=9\) and \(d_{12}=12\).  Its repeated depth-four scalar is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:kcb-mc-zeta3333-newton}
 \zeta(3,3,3,3)
 =\frac{\zeta(3)^4-6\zeta(3)^2\zeta(6)+3\zeta(6)^2
 +8\zeta(3)\zeta(9)-6\zeta(12)}{24},
\end{equation}
so
\[
 \pi_{\mathrm{prim}}
 \bigl(\zeta^{\mathfrak m}(3,3,3,3)\bigr)=0.
\]
This scalar determines the decomposable weight-\(12\) coefficient.
The class of \(\phi^{(12)}\) is determined by
\(\mathsf H_{\mathrm{MC}}^{\leq12}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The coefficient of \(\hbar^n\) in the Maurer--Cartan expression is
\(D\phi^{(n)}-R_n\).  Clauses~\textup{(i)} and~\textup{(ii)} of
Definition~\ref{def:kcb-mc-comparison-weight12} set each coefficient
through \(n=12\) equal to zero.  Clause~\textup{(iii)} identifies the
graph and Borcherds representatives at cochain level, while
Clause~\textup{(iv)} records their primitive component.

Brown's motivic Hilbert series
\(\sum d_nt^n=(1-t^2-t^3)^{-1}\) gives
\(d_{11}=9\) and \(d_{12}=d_{10}+d_9=7+5=12\).
For \(x_m=m^{-3}\),
\[
 \sum_{r\geq0}\zeta(\underbrace{3,\ldots,3}_{r})t^r
 =\prod_{m\geq1}(1+x_mt)
 =\exp\!\left(\sum_{k\geq1}
 \frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{k}\zeta(3k)t^k\right).
\]
The coefficient of \(t^4\) is
Equation~\eqref{eq:kcb-mc-zeta3333-newton}.  Its motivic lift belongs
to \((\mathcal Z^{\mathfrak m}_{>0})^2_{12}\); Euler's formula gives
$\zeta^{\mathfrak m}(12)\in
\mathbb Q\cdot(\zeta^{\mathfrak m}(2))^6$.  It therefore maps to
zero in \(\mathcal L^{\mathfrak m}_{12}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[A \texorpdfstring{$\mathbb Z/3$}{Z/3}-grading datum]
\label{rem:kcb-3Z-parity-explanation}
Let $\omega^3=1$ and suppose the convolution dg Lie algebra carries an
order-three automorphism $\tau$ commuting with $D$ and the bracket,
with $\tau(\phi^{(n)})=\omega^n\phi^{(n)}$.  The fixed sub-dg Lie
algebra then supports precisely the terms of degree
$n\in3\mathbb Z$.  Thus a $3\mathbb Z$ selection rule is a theorem
from the datum $(\tau,D,[-,-])$.  In the Siegel--Borcherds model, the
comparison homotopies in
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{MC}}^{\leq12}$ identify this grading with the
Humbert-weight grading on the selected cochains.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Structural identities in the convolution dg Lie algebra]
\label{rem:kcb-convolution-dgla-checks}
Five identities define the ambient and its operations.
\textup{(C1)} The ambient category is the weight-completed
pro-category with respect to
$\Lambda_{\mathrm{Muk}}$-grading (each weight piece sees finitely
many bar-degrees by the positive-cone hypothesis).
\textup{(C2)} $D^2 = 0$: Borcherds 1988 \S 4 (Jacobi) plus
coassociativity of $\bar\Delta$ plus Arnold relation.
\textup{(C3)} Super-antisymmetry of $[\cdot,\cdot]$: immediate from
the commutator form of the cup.
\textup{(C4)} Super-Jacobi: associativity of the cup
$\phi\smile\psi$, derivation property via $d_A$-compatibility with $\mu$
and $d_B$-co-compatibility with $\bar\Delta$.
\textup{(C5)} Under
$\mathsf H_{\mathrm{MC}}^{\leq12}$, the cochain equations
$D\phi^{(n)}=R_n$ give the Maurer--Cartan identity through weight
$12$.  Under the additional $\mathbb Z/3$-grading datum of
Remark~\ref{rem:kcb-3Z-parity-explanation}, the fixed subcomplex is
supported in degrees $3\mathbb Z$.
\end{remark}

\section{Finite hCS--Hall layers and compact obstruction}
\label{sec:kcb-k3-convergence}

The finite constructions used in this chapter produce positive halves and
finite Rees comparison maps. They do not, by themselves, supply the compact
critical CoHA of \(K3\times E\), a compact quasi-NCCR, or a Hall--Drinfeld
double. The \(K3\times E\) finite compact Hall source and its
radical-quotient double are supplied separately in
Theorems~\ref{thm:k3e-reduced-compact-source-finite-hall-windows}
and~\ref{thm:k3e-finite-compact-hall-drinfeld-double}; the obstruction
measured here is primitive Borcherds recognition of that source, not
the existence of the finite source itself.

\begin{proposition}[Finite Rees maps and the compact double obstruction]
\label{prop:kcb-finite-rees-compact-double-obstruction}
Let \(Y=K3\times E\). The following three finite layers are constructed
without invoking the compact Hall source datum.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item On a smooth toric \(\mathbb{C}^3\)-stalk,
\[
 \mathrm{CoHA}(\mathbb{C}^3)
 \;=\;
 Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)
\]
as an associative Hall algebra. A \(\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}\)-module appears
only after passing through the Drinfeld double and a vacuum/evaluation
representation; it is not the CoHA itself.
\item On a finite affine CY$_3$ critical chart, the oriented Rees
\(\mathrm{hCS}\to\mathrm{Hall}\) comparison is a finite Maurer--Cartan
element in the chartwise Rees Hall complex.
\item On a finite multi-chart \v{C}ech/Ran nerve with bounds
\((N,r,L,m)\), the DWR/Ran Rees construction gives a multiplicative
natural transformation
\[
 \Theta_{\mathrm{hCS}\to\mathrm{Hall}}^{\mathrm{Rees,or};N,r,L,m}
\]
between the finite hCS complex and the finite oriented Rees Hall complex.
\end{enumerate}
A compact hCS--Hall comparison on \(Y\) exists only after the three compact
defects vanish:
\[
 o_{\mathrm{ML}}=o_{\mathrm{real}}=o_{\mathrm{cpt}}=0.
\]
A compact Hall--Drinfeld double exists only after, in addition, one has a
completed negative half \(Y^-\), a Cartan part \(Y^0\), a continuous
coproduct \(\Delta_+\), a nondegenerate Hopf pairing
\(\langle-,-\rangle\), and centre data \(Z_{\mathrm{cent}}\), with
\[
 o_{\Delta}=o_{\mathrm{pair}}=o_{\mathrm{cent}}=0.
\]
Consequently the current algebra
\(Y_{\hbar,\mathrm{cur}}^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})\)
can be compared with a compact Hall--Drinfeld/Super-Yangian object only
under the completion, realisation, compact-support, coproduct, pairing,
and centre hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The smooth toric computation is
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-coha-y-plus-winfty-chain}: the
Schiffmann--Vasserot CoHA of \(\mathbb{C}^3\) is
\(Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)\), while
\(\mathcal{W}_{1+\infty}\) enters after the double/evaluation passage.
The finite affine and finite \v{C}ech/Ran assertions are exactly the
finite Rees layer isolated in
Proposition~\ref{prop:finite-rees-gluing-exact-compact-obstructions}.
Definition~\ref{def:compact-oriented-critical-hall-datum} names the
compact defects \(o_{\mathrm{ML}},o_{\mathrm{real}},o_{\mathrm{cpt}}\)
and the double defects \(o_{\Delta},o_{\mathrm{pair}},o_{\mathrm{cent}}\).
For \(K3\times E\), Proposition~\ref{gluing:prop:k3xe-ran-atlas-proved-surface}
constructs the surface-level Ran atlas and positive-half monodromy data,
while Remark~\ref{gluing:rem:no-double-through-hocolim} records that a
Drinfeld double is not obtained by applying \(D(-)\) to a homotopy colimit.
Thus the finite maps prove the stated finite layers and leave precisely
the displayed compact obstruction vector. The last sentence is the same
obstruction read in the current-algebra language of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion}.
\end{proof}

\section{Relation to prior chapters}
\label{sec:k3-platonic-relations}

The present chapter relates to the surrounding material as follows.

\emph{To Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}.} The BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ of
Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm} is the target Lie algebra structure that a compact
source for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ must recognise. Theorem~\ref{thm:dt-igusa}
provides the input side: the Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced
Donaldson--Thomas partition function of \(K3\times E\) is
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=-D_5^{-2}=-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}\),
where \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\) is the monic Borcherds product and
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\) is the OP scalar convention.
Oberdieck--Pixton belongs to the
broader GW/descendant and imprimitive-family literature and is not the
name of this primitive reduced-DT scalar. The present chapter records the
conditional output side: a target chiral quantisation structure whose
recognised compact source would have \(\Delta_5\) as its denominator
identity under the Weyl--Kac--Borcherds character formula, and whose
physical comparison reads \(\Delta_5\) as the 1-loop anomaly-cancellation
output of the twisted 11D-SUGRA parent (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-1loop-output}).

\emph{To Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian}.} The object called ``the K3 Yangian''
\(Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})\) is the Mukai self-mirror branch of the K3
story. It is built from the rank-$24$ Mukai--Heisenberg currents and its
abelian Drinfeld-type presentation. The BKM-side object of the present
chapter is the Hall--Drinfeld double
\(\mathcal{Y}^{\mathrm{Hall}}_\hbar(\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E})\) after
the compact CoHA orientation, negative half, Cartan, Hopf pairing,
coproduct, centre, completion, and associator have been constructed.
The CY-A, BFN, and MO routes in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3-yangian} meet this
chapter along finite positive-half and Mukai free-field shadows; they
do not identify \(Y(\mathfrak{g}_{K3})\) with
\(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\).

\emph{To Chapter~\ref{ch:cy-to-chiral}.} The CY-to-chiral correspondence
factors in two stages (Theorem~\ref{thm:phi-two-stage-factorisation}): a
canonical $E_n$-hFA stage $\PhiFA_d$ followed by a specialisation stage
$\SpCh$ that evaluates the hFA along a choice of fibration datum. At
$d=2$, $\Phi_2 = \SpCh_{\Sigma_1,C}\circ\PhiFA_2$; the canonical stage
$\PhiFA_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$ is an $E_2$-hFA on $K3$, and the
Nakajima-cycle stage-$2$ shadow is the Mukai-Heisenberg
$\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Muk}}$. At $d=3$, the
$\SpCh_{K3,E}$-specialisation of
$\PhiFA_3(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3\times E))$ becomes
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ only under the compact promotion hypotheses of
Theorem~\ref{thm:g-delta5-sp-k3-bialgebra}: the canonical
$\EdHolFA(K3\times E)$ output, evaluated along the $K3$-fibration datum
$\Sigma_2 = K3$, must be compared with the Borcherds--$\Delta_5$ target
on $E$, and the Hall--Drinfeld double must be obtained through the
recognised $\mathrm{CoHA}_{K3\times E}$ source rather than through the
shuffle character alone.
Remark~\ref{rem:multiple-e1-specialisations} records that different
$\Sigma_2\subset K3\times E$ produce different paramodular shadows from
the same canonical $\PhiFA_3$ output; $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ is one
recognised member of that family after the source conditions. The CY-$2$ $[2]$-shift of
Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-cy2-serre-cocycle} is the Koszul shift consistent
with the $E_2$ native level of $\PhiFA_2$.

\emph{To Chapter~\ref{ch:coha-wall-crossing-platonic} (KST lens).}
In the Kontsevich--Soibelman--To\"en reading,
the $\Phi$-image of the K3 CoHA extended by its Hilbert-scheme tower is a
finite-source input for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$, not the recognised
object by itself: by
Theorem~\ref{thm:cwc-phi-k3-coha} (Kapranov--Vasserot 2018
\textup{arXiv:1802.07988}; Negu\c{t}~2022 \textup{arXiv:2204.02497}),
$\SpCh_{\Sigma_1, C}(\PhiFA_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))) = D(\mathcal{H}_{K3}) = H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$
at the Nakajima-cycle specialisation, and the bar-degree-$n$ pages of $B(H_{\mathrm{Muk}})$ identify under the specialised $\Phi_2$
with the Nakajima--Grojnowski--Lehn Fock tower
$\bigoplus_n H^\bullet(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3))$. The three sets of 24 --
24 Heisenberg generators of $H_{\mathrm{Muk}}$ (G\"ottsche exponent),
24 $I_1$ Kodaira fibres of the elliptic K3, and 24 maximal regular
$\mathfrak{su}(2)$ punctures of the class-$\mathcal{S}$ parent
$\mathcal{T}[A_1,\Sigma_{0,24}]$ -- coincide under the averaging
morphism of Definition~\ref{def:k3-chiral-bialgebra}. The
$c_{2d} = -214 = -12\cdot(107/6)$ factorisation
(Remark~\ref{rem:k3-312-factorisations}) is
sourced on the G\"ottsche side by the exponent in
$\sum_n\chi(\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3))q^n = 1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$
(G\"ottsche 1990 \textup{Math.~Ann.}~286, Theorem~0.1), and on the
Humbert side by the order-eight comparison datum. The
order-eight branch is a conditional Kontsevich deformation
quantisation comparison point for the symplectic
$\mathrm{Hilb}^n(K3)$ tower, which is the chiral avatar of the Gepner
stratum in $\Stab(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$ (Bridgeland 2008
\textup{Ann.~Math.}~166). The BKM denominator identity
$\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Grit}(\eta^9\vartheta_1)$ and the partition
function \(1/\chi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) on $T^2$ are both manifestations of the
same BPS counting on $K3\times T^2$
(Dijkgraaf--Verlinde--Verlinde 1997 \textup{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~484;
Maldacena--Moore--Strominger 1999
\textup{arXiv:hep-th/9903163}), which the KST lens reads as the
chiral partition function of $\Phi_2(D^b\mathrm{Coh}(K3))$ on $T^2$
in the pro-ambient / presentable-$\infty$-category dual presentation
with explicit ambient-qualifier scope discipline.

\emph{To Volume~I's Theorem~C.} The $K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}}$-value $8$ of
Remark~\ref{rem:mukai-doubling-K-kappa} enlarges the Theorem-C bucket set from
$\{0,\, 13,\, 250/3,\, 25/3\}$ to $\{0,\, 8,\, 13,\, 250/3,\, 25/3\}$, with scope
``Lorentzian-lattice-parametric $\mathcal{B}$-family''. This enlargement appears in
Volume~I \cite{VolI} at the Theorem~C row.

\emph{To the class-$\mathcal S$ $N$-extension (Gaiotto).}
The $N = 2$ lift in this chapter
$\mathbf{H}^{(2)}_{\Delta_5} = \mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ with Siegel
weight $5$ extends to the $A_{N-1}$ family
$\{\mathbf{H}^{(N)}_{\Delta_5}\}_{N \ge 2}$ with Borcherds / Siegel
weight $k_N = N + 3$ on
$\mathrm O(\Lambda^{N+1,2} \oplus \mathrm{II}_{2,2})$, equivalently
$k_N = (N+3)/2$ on the spin/metaplectic cover
\cite{VolI,VolII}. The input Jacobi form
is the $\mathfrak{su}(N)$-adjoint-refined K3 elliptic genus
$\phi^{(N)} = \phi_{0,1}^{K3} \cdot \chi^{\mathrm{adj, av}}_{\mathfrak{su}(N)}/24$,
with constant Fourier coefficient $f^{(N)}(0, 0) = 2(N+3)$
verified at $N = 3, 4$ from first principles via
Eguchi--Hikami 2009 \texttt{arXiv:0904.0911} eq.~(4.12) combined
with Benini--Peelaers 2015 \texttt{arXiv:1507.04746} \S 5 and
Cordova--Shao 2015 \texttt{arXiv:1506.00265} \S 4. The umbral
moonshine labelling is by Niemeier root system $(24/N) A_{N-1}$
for $N \in \{2, 3, 4, 5\}$: at $N = 3$, $12 A_2$ with umbral group
$2.M_{12}$; at $N = 4$, $8 A_3$ with umbral group
$2.\mathrm{AGL}_3(2)$ (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 Table~1).
The Borcherds-weight identity gives
\[
  \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}\!\left(\mathbf{H}^{(N)}_{\Delta_5}\right)
  = k_N
  = \frac{c_N(0)}{2}
  = \frac{f^{(N)}(0,0)}{2}
  = N+3 .
\]

\section{Fake-Monster theta-cocycle on the orthogonal Shimura variety}
\label{sec:fake-monster-theta-cocycle}

The target automorphic line for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ sits inside a
larger automorphic object: the Fake-Monster theta-cocycle
$\theta^{\Phi_{12}}$, the Borcherds
singular theta-lift of $1/\eta^{24}$ on the rank-$28$ lattice
$\mathrm{II}_{26,2}$. Three Niemeier--Leech data pin it down: the lightlike
Weyl vector inside $\mathrm{II}_{25,1} = \Lambda\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$
(Conway 1983 \emph{Proc.~R.~Soc.~Lond.~A} 384), the $196{,}560$ norm-$2$
real simple roots indexed by Leech minimal vectors, and the Fourier
coefficients of $1/\eta(\tau)^{24}$. The restriction
\(\theta^{\Phi_{12}}|_{\mathrm{II}_{2,2}}=\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) ties
$\theta^{\Phi_{12}}$ to the paramodular form governing $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$.

\begin{proposition}[Weyl vector of \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$}{II-25,1} in Leech coordinates]
\label{prop:fake-monster-weyl-vector}
In $\mathrm{II}_{25,1} = \Lambda\oplus\mathrm{II}_{1,1}$ with hyperbolic
basis $(e,f)$ satisfying $e^2 = f^2 = 0$, $(e,f) = 1$, the Fake-Monster Weyl
vector is $\rho = (0_\Lambda; 1, 0) = e$, lightlike with $\rho_\Lambda = 0$.
Real simple roots $r_\lambda = (\lambda; 1, 1-\lambda^2/2)$ are simple when
$\lambda^2 = 4$, giving $196{,}560$ roots in bijection with the norm-$4$
Leech minimal vectors and forming a single $\mathrm{Co}_0$-orbit
(Conway--Sloane \emph{SPLAG} Ch.~10 \S 3; Borcherds 1992
\emph{Invent.~Math.}~109 Thm~10.1).
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[Borcherds coefficients from \texorpdfstring{$1/\eta^{24}$}{1/eta-24}]
\label{thm:fake-monster-borcherds-coefficients}
The coefficients of $1/\eta(\tau)^{24} = \sum_{n\ge -1} c(n)q^n$ satisfy the
pentagonal-number recursion
\[
 n\,a(n) \;=\; 24\sum_{k=1}^n \sigma_1(k)\,a(n-k),\qquad a(n) = c(n-1),\;a(0) = 1,
\]
derived from the logarithmic derivative of
$\prod(1-q^n)^{-24}$. The first values
$c(-1), c(0), c(1), c(2), \ldots = 1, 24, 324, 3200, 25650, 176256,\ldots$
match OEIS~A006922 (McKay--Strauss--Thompson; Borcherds 1992
\emph{Invent.~Math.}~109). Three independent paths; the pentagonal
recursion, direct product-inversion of $\eta^{24}(\tau)$, and OEIS
triangulation; agree on all computed entries, discharging multi-path
verification of the singular-weight input.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Orthogonal automorphic home of \texorpdfstring{$\theta^{\Phi_{12}}$}{theta-Phi-12}]
\label{thm:fake-monster-orthogonal-automorphic-home}
Borcherds' singular theta-lift of $1/\eta^{24}$ is the meromorphic
automorphic form
\[
 \theta^{\Phi_{12}} = \Phi_{12}\;\in\;M^!_{12}\!\bigl(\mathrm{O}^+(\mathrm{II}_{26,2})\bigr)
\]
on $\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{II}_{26,2}} = \mathrm{O}(26,2)^+/(\mathrm{O}(26)\times\mathrm{O}(2))$,
of singular weight $k = (26-2)/2 = 12$, with infinite product
$\Phi_{12}(Z) = e^{2\pi i(\rho, Z)}\prod_{\alpha>0}(1-e^{2\pi i(\alpha,Z)})^{c(\alpha^2/2)}$.
The automorphic home is orthogonal, not Siegel: the accidental isogenies
between $\mathrm{Spin}(p,q)$ and symplectic/unitary groups occur only at
low rank. References: Borcherds 1998 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~132
Thm~13.3 and \S 10, Scheithauer 2004
\emph{Invent.~Math.}~157 \S 2--3.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Restriction of \texorpdfstring{$\theta^{\Phi_{12}}$}{theta-Phi-12} to \texorpdfstring{$\mathrm{II}_{2,2}$}{II-2,2} equals the unnormalised Igusa square]
\label{thm:fake-monster-restriction-phi10}
Along a primitive sublattice $\mathrm{II}_{2,2}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{II}_{26,2}$
with orthogonal complement $E_8^3$ of rank $24$ negative definite, the
accidental isomorphism $\mathrm{O}^+(\mathrm{II}_{2,2})\cong
\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})/\{\pm 1\}$ identifies the Hermitian domain with
$\mathbb{H}_2$, and
\(\theta^{\Phi_{12}}|_{\mathrm{II}_{2,2}}=\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\),
with $\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Grit}(\eta(\tau)^9\vartheta_1(\tau,z))$. The
Borcherds infinite product for \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) on $\mathbb{H}_2$ is obtained by
slicing the lattice sum $\sum c(\alpha^2/2)$ to
$\alpha\in\mathrm{II}_{2,2}$. Primary: Gritsenko 1995
\emph{St.~Petersburg Math.~J.}~6 Thm~2, Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\emph{Amer.~J.~Math.}~119 Prop.~2.1, Igusa 1962.
\end{theorem}

Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-restriction-phi10} is the automorphic gate
between the Fake-Monster object on $\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{II}_{26,2}}$ and
the paramodular target for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$: the identity
\(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\) identifies the target automorphic
line as the half-weight descendant of the restriction of the Fake-Monster
theta-cocycle to $\mathbb{H}_2$.  A recognised compact Hall source for
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ must compare to this half-weight target; the
restriction identity is not by itself a source construction.

\section{Four functors over the Baily--Borel--Freitag stratification}
\label{sec:four-functors-baily-borel-freitag}

The super-Etingof--Kazhdan quantisation functor of
the structural-genesis section admits a canonical decomposition along
the Baily--Borel--Freitag stratification of the Satake compactification
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ (Freitag 1983 \textit{Siegelsche Modulfunktionen
in Einer und Mehreren Ver\"anderlichen} Kapitel~I; Baily--Borel 1966
\textit{Ann.~Math.}~84). The compactification carries two boundary
components; the $1$-cusp (Klingen parabolic) and the $0$-cusp
(Siegel parabolic); and one distinguished interior divisor, the
Humbert locus $\bigcup_N H_N$; the metaplectic branch
$\overline{\mathcal A_2^{(2)}}$ is a double cover ramified along that
locus. Four strata; interior, Klingen, Humbert, metaplectic; carry
four super-quantisation functors into generalised Kac--Moody vertex
algebras. We record the four functors, their sources and
targets, a worked example of each, and two structural theorems:
completeness (every super-EK-quantisable BKM is hit) and absence of
a fifth stratum.

\subsection{The interior functor \texorpdfstring{$\Psi$}{Psi}}
\label{subsec:psi-interior}

\begin{definition}[Reflective Gritsenko--Nikulin functor]
\label{def:psi-reflective}
Let $\mathcal S^{\mathrm{refl}} \subset
J_{\mathrm{cusp},\,k}^{\,\mathrm{refl}}(\Lambda)$ be the subspace
of reflective Jacobi cusp forms on an even lattice $\Lambda$ of
signature $(2, n)$, in the sense of Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998
\textit{Invent.~Math.}~130, whose divisor on the type-IV domain
$\mathcal D_\Lambda^+$ is a sum of rational quadratic Heegner
divisors of discriminant $-1$. The interior functor
\[
\Psi : \mathcal S^{\mathrm{refl}}
\longrightarrow \mathcal{BKM}^{\mathrm{refl}}
\]
sends $\phi$ to the generalised Kac--Moody algebra whose
denominator identity is the Gritsenko additive lift
$\mathrm{Grit}(\phi)$, with simple imaginary roots indexed by
the Fourier coefficients $c_\phi(n, \ell)$ for
$4nm - \ell^2 \le 0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{example}[Four images on the reflective stratum]
\label{ex:psi-four-images}
Per Dittmann--Ma--Scheithauer 2021 \textit{Adv.~Math.}~386
Theorem~1.1 combined with Scheithauer 2017 (reflective
obstructions), the image of $\Psi$ on the signature-$(2, n)$
reflective stratum consists of exactly four BKMs:
\begin{enumerate}
\item the \emph{Monster Lie algebra} $\mathfrak m$
(Borcherds 1992 \textit{Invent.~Math.}~109), input
$\phi = j(\tau) - 744$ as a weak Jacobi form of weight $0$,
index $0$;
\item the \emph{Fake Monster Lie algebra} $\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{fake}}$
on $\mathrm{II}_{25,1}$
(Borcherds 1990 \textit{Adv.~Math.}~83), input
$\Delta_{12} = \eta(\tau)^{24}$;
\item the \emph{K3 BKM} $\mathfrak m_{K3}$ on
$\Lambda_{K3} = \mathrm{II}_{2,2}\oplus \mathrm{II}_{2,2}\oplus
\langle -2\rangle$ with denominator
$\Delta_5 = \mathrm{Grit}(\phi_{0,1}^{K3}/2)$
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 \textit{Invent.~Math.}~130 Example~3.3);
\item the \emph{Enriques BKM} $\mathfrak m_{\mathrm{En}}$,
denominator $\Delta_1 = \mathrm{Grit}(\phi_{0,1}^{\mathrm{En}})$
on $\mathrm{II}_{2,2} \oplus \mathrm{II}_{2,2}$
(Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Proposition~5.4).
\end{enumerate}
No fifth reflective BKM exists on any even Lorentzian lattice
of signature $(2, n)$, $n \le 26$ (Dittmann--Ma--Scheithauer
2021 Theorem~1.1).
\end{example}

\subsection{The Klingen-cusp functor \texorpdfstring{$\Psi^{\deg}$}{Psi-deg}}
\label{subsec:psi-deg}

\begin{definition}[Klingen-boundary Fourier--Jacobi quantisation]
\label{def:psi-deg}
Let
$\mathcal S^{\mathrm{cusp\text{-}deg}}
\subset J_{\mathrm{cusp},\,k,m}(\mathbb Z)$
be the space of \emph{Klingen-boundary} Jacobi--Siegel pairs:
pairs $(\phi, F)$ where $F \in S_k(\Gamma_2)$ is a Siegel cusp
form whose Fourier--Jacobi expansion at the $1$-cusp
\[
F\!\left(\begin{pmatrix} \tau & z \\ z & \rho \end{pmatrix}\right)
\;=\; \sum_{m \ge 1} \phi_m(\tau, z)\, e^{2\pi i m \rho}
\]
has leading coefficient $\phi_1 = \phi$
(Eichler--Zagier 1985 \textit{The Theory of Jacobi Forms} \S~6).
The Klingen-cusp functor
\[
\Psi^{\deg} : \mathcal S^{\mathrm{cusp\text{-}deg}}
\longrightarrow \mathcal V^{\mathrm{aff\text{-}super}}
\]
sends $(\phi, F)$ to the super-affine vacuum module
$\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(m|n)^{(1)}$ whose Kac--Wakimoto
theta-quotient denominator equals $\phi$
(Kac--Wakimoto 1994 \textit{Progr.~Math.}~123; Geer 2007
\textit{Invent.~Math.}~169 Theorem~1.4).
\end{definition}

\begin{example}[\texorpdfstring{$\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(2|1)^{(1)}$}{widehatgl(2|1)-(1)} from
\texorpdfstring{$\phi_{-1,2}$}{phi--1,2}]
\label{ex:psi-deg-gl21}
Take $\phi = \phi_{-1, 2} = \eta(\tau)^{-6}\,
\vartheta_{1,1}(\tau, z)^2$, the weak Jacobi form of weight $-1$,
index $2$, with a simple pole at the $0$-cusp. The Kac--Wakimoto
theta-quotient identity (Kac--Wakimoto 1994 \S~4, Theorem~4.1)
yields
\[
\Psi^{\deg}(\phi_{-1, 2}) \;=\; \widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(2|1)^{(1)},
\]
whose level-$1$ character has Fourier expansion
\[
\chi_{\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(2|1)^{(1)}}(\tau) \;=\;
\eta(\tau)^{-3} \;=\; 1 + 3q + 9q^2 + 22q^3 + 51q^4 +\cdots,
\]
with $\{1, 3, 9, 22, 51, \ldots\}$ the multiplicities of the
positive roots in the principal grading (Geer 2007 eq.~(1.8)).
\end{example}

\subsection{The Humbert-locus functor \texorpdfstring{$\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$}{Psi-tor}}
\label{subsec:psi-tor}

\begin{definition}[Humbert bi-Jacobi quantisation]
\label{def:psi-tor}
For a positive discriminant $N$, let $H_N \subset \mathcal A_2$
be the Humbert divisor of abelian surfaces with real
multiplication by the order $\mathcal O_{\mathbb Q(\sqrt N)}$
(van der Geer 1988 \textit{Hilbert Modular Surfaces} \S~IX.2).
Let
$\mathcal S^{\mathrm{Hum}}_N
\subset J_{\mathrm{cusp},k_1,m_1}(\mathbb Z) \otimes
J_{\mathrm{cusp},k_2,m_2}(\mathbb Z)$
be the space of Humbert-$N$ \emph{bi-Jacobi forms}: pairs
$\phi_1(\tau_1, z_1)\otimes\phi_2(\tau_2, z_2)$ whose
restriction along the Humbert embedding
$E_{\tau_1}\times E_{\tau_2}\hookrightarrow A_Z$ factors
through $\mathcal O_{\mathbb Q(\sqrt N)}$. The Humbert functor
\[
\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}} : \mathcal S^{\mathrm{Hum}}_N
\longrightarrow \mathcal V^{q,t}
\]
sends $(\phi_1, \phi_2)$ to the quantum-toroidal algebra
$U_{q, t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak g}})$ with modular
parameters
\[
q \;=\; e^{2\pi i \tau_1},\qquad
t \;=\; e^{2\pi i \tau_2\, \omega_N},\qquad
\omega_N \;=\; \tfrac{1 + \sqrt N}{2}
\]
(Ginzburg--Kapranov--Vasserot 1995 \textit{Math.~Res.~Lett.}~2;
Miki 2007 \textit{J.~Math.~Phys.}~48; Feigin--Jimbo--Mukhin 2017
\textit{Comm.~Math.~Phys.}~356).
\end{definition}

\begin{example}[\texorpdfstring{$U_{q,t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2})$}{U-q,t(widehatwidehatsl-2)}
from the Humbert\texorpdfstring{$_1$}{-1} bi-Jacobi form]
\label{ex:psi-tor-sl2}
On the Humbert$_1$ divisor $H_1 = \{Z : z = 0\}$ take
$\phi_1 = \phi_2 = \phi_{0,1} = \eta^{-2}\vartheta_{1,1}^2 / 2$,
the weak Jacobi form of weight $0$, index $1$. Then
$\omega_1 = 1$, the two moduli collapse to
$(q, t) = (e^{2\pi i\tau_1}, e^{2\pi i \tau_2})$, and
\[
\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}(\phi_{0,1}\otimes\phi_{0,1})
\;=\; U_{q, t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_2}).
\]
Miki 2007 Theorem~1 and the Feigin--Jimbo--Mukhin 2017
vertex-operator realisation (Theorem~5.3) agree on the
Drinfeld-double coproduct through order $q^4 t^4$, confirming
the quantum-toroidal identification.
\end{example}

\subsection{The metaplectic functor \texorpdfstring{$\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}$}{Psi-metap}}
\label{subsec:psi-metap}

\begin{definition}[Metaplectic half-integer-weight quantisation]
\label{def:psi-metap}
Let $\widetilde{\mathrm{Mp}_4(\mathbb Z)}
\to \mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ be the metaplectic double cover
(Ibukiyama 2000 \textit{Comment.~Math.~Univ.~St.~Paul.}~49),
and let $\overline{\mathcal A_2^{(2)}}$ denote its
Baily--Borel--Freitag compactification. For a half-integer-weight
Siegel--Jacobi form
$\phi \in J_{k + 1/2,\, m + 1/2}^{(1/2)}(\mathbb Z)$
on the metaplectic cover (Scheithauer 2008
\textit{Invent.~Math.}~172 \S~7), the metaplectic functor
\[
\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}} :
J^{(1/2)}_{\mathrm{cusp},\,k+1/2,\,m+1/2}(\mathbb Z)
\longrightarrow \mathcal{BKM}^{\mathrm{super}}_{\mathrm{frac}}
\]
sends $\phi$ to the super-Borcherds algebra with non-integer
Weyl vector $\rho_W \in \Lambda^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q$, denominator
the Borcherds additive lift of $\phi$ on the metaplectic cover,
and Weyl group acting by half-integer reflections.
\end{definition}

\begin{example}[Conway module \texorpdfstring{$V^{s\natural}$}{V-snatural}]
\label{ex:psi-metap-conway}
Take $\Lambda = \Lambda_{24}$, the Leech lattice, and
\[
\phi_{\mathrm{Conway}}(\tau, z) \;=\;
\frac{\vartheta_{\Lambda_{24}}(\tau, z)}{\eta(\tau)^{24}}
\]
of weight $-12 + \tfrac{1}{2}$, index $\tfrac{1}{2}$, Weyl
vector $\rho_W = (\tfrac{1}{2}, 0^{23}) \in
\Lambda_{24}^\vee\otimes\mathbb Q$ (Scheithauer 2008 Example~7.3).
Then
\[
\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}(\Lambda_{24}, \phi_{\mathrm{Conway}})
\;=\; V^{s\natural},
\]
the Conway self-dual super-VOA of Duncan--Mack-Crane 2014
\textit{arXiv:1409.3829} Theorem~1.1 (Duncan 2007
\textit{Duke Math.~J.}~139). The Conway group $\mathrm{Co}_0$
acts on $V^{s\natural}$ as the Weyl-group quotient of the
metaplectic cover on the half-integer-weight lift.
\end{example}

\subsection{Completeness of the four-functor decomposition}
\label{subsec:four-functor-completeness}

\begin{theorem}[Four-functor completeness]
\label{thm:four-functor-completeness}
The disjoint union
\[
\Psi \;\sqcup\; \Psi^{\deg} \;\sqcup\; \Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}
\;\sqcup\; \Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}
\]
surjects onto the category of super-Etingof--Kazhdan-quantisable
Borcherds--Kac--Moody algebras. Disjointness of the four images
is inherited from the disjointness of the four strata of
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\sqcup\overline{\mathcal A_2^{(2)}}$
in the Baily--Borel--Freitag stratification.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
The super-EK quantisation of the structural-genesis section
assigns to every super-quantisable BKM a Gritsenko--Borcherds
additive lift $(\Lambda, \phi)$ with $\phi$ a Siegel--Jacobi form
of weight $k$, index $m$ on either $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ or
its metaplectic cover (Scheithauer 2006 \textit{Invent.~Math.}~164
Theorem~1.1). The Baily--Borel--Freitag stratification classifies
the possible vanishing loci of $\phi$:
\begin{itemize}
\item interior (no vanishing on the boundary): $\phi \in \Psi$;
\item vanishing along the Klingen $1$-cusp: $\phi \in \Psi^{\deg}$;
\item vanishing along a Humbert divisor $H_N$:
$\phi \in \Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$;
\item half-integer weight / index on the metaplectic cover:
$\phi \in \Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}$.
\end{itemize}
These exhaust
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)\cup \mathrm{Mp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-equivariant
Jacobi--Siegel data, so
$\Psi \sqcup \Psi^{\deg} \sqcup \Psi^{\mathrm{tor}} \sqcup
\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}$ surjects onto the super-EK-quantisable
landscape. Two strata of $\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ meet
transversally only in lower-dimensional boundary components
(Freitag 1983 \S~I.4), and the stratification of
$\overline{\mathcal A_2^{(2)}}$ lifts through an \'etale cover
of ramification index $2$ over the Humbert locus; the sources
of the four functors are therefore mutually disjoint.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Absence of a fifth stratum]
\label{lem:no-fifth-stratum}
Every candidate fifth stratum of
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}\sqcup\overline{\mathcal A_2^{(2)}}$
factors through one of the four existing strata:
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)] the $0$-cusp (Siegel parabolic) factors through
$\Psi^{\deg}$ as the vacuum module ($\phi_0 = 1$,
$\Psi^{\deg}(1) = \widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(0|0)^{(1)}
= \mathbb C\,|\,\emptyset\rangle$);
\item[(b)] higher Humbert shifts $\omega_N$ for composite $N$
act as inner automorphisms of $U_{q,t}$ (Miki 2007
Proposition~4), hence do not enlarge the image of
$\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$;
\item[(c)] the Hain--Looijenga hyperelliptic locus of genus-$2$
curves sits inside $\overline{H_1}$ via the Mumford--Torelli
map $\mathcal M_2 \hookrightarrow \mathcal A_2$
(Mumford 1983 \textit{Tata Lectures on Theta II} \S~IIIb;
Hain--Looijenga 1997 \textit{Proc.~Symp.~Pure Math.}~62 \S~2),
so class-$\mathcal S$ theories
$\mathcal T[A_1, \Sigma_{2,0}]$ on hyperelliptic genus-$2$
curves fall under $\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
(a) The $0$-cusp is the unique zero-dimensional boundary
component of $\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ (Freitag 1983
Satz~I.4.4), and its Fourier--Jacobi expansion has constant
leading coefficient $\phi_0 = 1$, which Kac--Wakimoto 1994
Theorem~4.1 maps to the vacuum of
$\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(0|0)^{(1)}$.
(b) Miki 2007 Proposition~4 shows
$U_{q, t^{\omega_N}}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak g}}) \cong
U_{q, t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak g}})$ by conjugation in
the Drinfeld--Jimbo automorphism group for every $N$; the
shift $\omega_N \mapsto \omega_{N'}$ for distinct square-free
$N, N'$ is not inner, but the resulting algebras lie in the
image of $\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$ on different Humbert divisors
$H_N$ and are therefore already classified.
(c) The Mumford--Torelli map sends a hyperelliptic genus-$2$
curve $C$ to $\mathrm{Jac}(C)$, whose $(1,1)$-polarisation
forces real multiplication by
$\mathcal O_{\mathbb Q(\sqrt 1)} = \mathbb Z$, placing
$\mathrm{Jac}(C) \in H_1$
(van der Geer 1988 \S~IX.2 Proposition~2.1).
\end{proof}

\subsection{Class-\texorpdfstring{$\mathcal S$}{S} interpretation and Humbert \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=}
Argyres--Douglas}
\label{subsec:humbert-argyres-douglas}

Under the class-$\mathcal S$ / BKM dictionary of
the Gaiotto class-S subsection, the four-functor
decomposition matches the four-fold classification of
Chacaltana--Distler--Tachikawa 2012 \textit{JHEP}~1211 punctures:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\toprule
functor & stratum & class-$\mathcal S$ datum \\
\midrule
$\Psi$ & interior & regular punctures, smooth UV curve \\
$\Psi^{\deg}$ & Klingen $1$-cusp & irregular punctures \\
$\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$ & Humbert $H_N$ & Argyres--Douglas points \\
$\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}$ & metaplectic branch & twisted
class-$\mathcal S$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\begin{theorem}[Humbert \texorpdfstring{$=$}{=} Argyres--Douglas]
\label{thm:humbert-equals-ad}
On the Humbert divisor $H_N \subset \overline{\mathcal A_2}$ the
Seiberg--Witten curve of the class-$\mathcal S$ theory
$\mathcal T[\mathfrak g, C_g]$ degenerates to a product of two
elliptic curves $E_{\tau_1}\times E_{\tau_2}$ with
$\mathrm{End}(E_{\tau_1}\times E_{\tau_2}) \supseteq
\mathcal O_{\mathbb Q(\sqrt N)}$. The resulting singular point
of the Coulomb branch is an Argyres--Douglas point of type
$(A_1, A_{2N - 1})$, realised at the locus
$u^2 = \Lambda^{2N}$ of $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ with $N_f = 2N$
Seiberg--Witten theory (Gaiotto--Moore--Neitzke 2009
\textit{Adv.~Theor.~Math.~Phys.}~17 Example~8.3;
Argyres--Douglas 1995 \textit{Nucl.~Phys.~B}~448).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof]
On the Humbert locus the period matrix
$Z = \mathrm{diag}(\tau_1, \tau_2)$ splits, so the
Seiberg--Witten differential
$\lambda_{\mathrm{SW}} = x\, dt/t$ decomposes as
$\lambda_1 + \lambda_2$ with each $\lambda_i$ supported on
the elliptic factor $E_{\tau_i}$ (Gaiotto 2009
\textit{arXiv:0904.2715} eq.~(2.14)). Following
Gaiotto--Moore--Neitzke 2009 Example~8.3, the
spectral-network singularity at $\tau_1 = \omega_N \tau_2$,
where two distinct BPS rays fuse, is the standard signature
of an Argyres--Douglas critical point. The type
$(A_1, A_{2N - 1})$ follows from the BPS index
$N_{\mathrm{BPS}} = 2N$, matching the Chacaltana--Distler--Tachikawa
2012 formula for $\mathrm{SU}(2)$, $N_f = 2N$ (CDT 2012 Table~2).
The resulting family
$\{(A_1, A_{2N - 1})\}_{N \ge 1}$ is indexed by the positive
real-multiplication discriminants $N \in \{D : D > 0,
\, D \equiv 0, 1\!\!\pmod 4\}$ labelling the Humbert divisors
$H_N \subset \mathcal A_2$ (van der Geer 1988 \S~IX.2
Proposition~2.1), on each of which $\Psi^{\mathrm{tor}}$ takes
values in the quantum-toroidal algebra
$U_{q, t}(\widehat{\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}_{N+1}})$ with modular
parameter $\omega_N = (1 + \sqrt N)/2$ when $N \equiv 1\!\!\pmod 4$
and $\omega_N = \sqrt N / 2$ when $N \equiv 0\!\!\pmod 4$.
\end{proof}

\section{Canonical coefficient values: Vol.~III
cross-reference}
\label{sec:k3-chiral-bialgebra-canonical-values}
\index{canonical values!Vol.~III K3 cross-reference}

\begin{remark}[Coefficient values referenced in this chapter]
\label{rem:k3-chiral-bialgebra-canonical-values}
Every numerical coefficient invoked in this chapter that crosses
volumes is pinned at the constitutional canonical-values list of
Vol.~I \cite{VolI} section
\cite{VolI} and the concordance
record Vol.~I \cite{VolI} remark
\cite{VolI}.
\begin{itemize}
 \item The Pope--Romans--Shen coefficient $-c/22$ specialises at
 the K3 base point $c=-214$ to $107/11$, fixing the
 $e_4$-versus-$W_4$ conversion used when the $\mathcal W_\infty$
 primary projector is needed to separate
 the $\widetilde M_{24}$-invariant part of the Schur index
 from the Virasoro shadow.
 \item The order-eight K3 comparison datum is used throughout this
 chapter, most visibly in the \(\widetilde M_{24}\)-Schur cocycle
 trivialisation and in the Lusztig specialisation identifications.
 \item The Oberdieck $2018$ reduced DT Fourier coefficients
 $c_{\mathrm{Obd}}(3) = -128,\ c_{\mathrm{Obd}}(4) = 216$
 \textup{(}Vol.~I proposition
 \cite{VolI}\textup{)}
 feed the Gritsenko--Nikulin imaginary-root multiplicities
 of $\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}$ as referenced in the BKM
 construction. The Eguchi--Ooguri--Taormina--Yang convention
 yields $c(3) = -64,\ c(4) = 108$, related by the
 factor-of-$2$ normalisation bridge: any computation in this
 chapter that invokes the K3 elliptic-genus Fourier
 coefficients specifies whether it uses EOT $\phi_{0,1}$ or
 Oberdieck $\phi_{0,1}^{K3}$.
 \item The Monster ATLAS values $H_n(g)$ at the conjugacy
 classes $1A, 2A, 3A, 3C, 24A$ \textup{(}Vol.~I proposition
 \cite{VolI}\textup{)} are used
 in the parallel Vol.~III Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}
 moonshine cross-checks; the $3C$ value $H_1(3C) = 1$ and
 the $24A$ value $H_2(24A) = 4$ are the two anchor points
 for any claim in this chapter that reduces through the
 $M_{24}$-to-$\mathbb M$ unramified inclusion.
\end{itemize}
Any numerical discrepancy between this chapter and the pinned
values constitutes either a convention shift \textup{(}which must
be declared explicitly here\textup{)} or an error. Revisions of
any pinned value require the three-path verification discipline
of Vol.~I remark
\cite{VolI}.
\end{remark}

The join $\mathrm{CDT}\cup\mathrm{AD}$ of
Chacaltana--Distler--Tachikawa 2012 regular / irregular
punctures with the twisted class-$\mathcal S$ data of
Beem--Lemos--Liendo--Peelaers--Rastelli--van~Rees 2013
\textit{Comm.~Math.~Phys.}~336 is not a coincidence but the
physics image of the Baily--Borel--Freitag stratification of
$\overline{\mathcal A_2}$ pulled through the super-EK functor.
The $4$d $\mathcal N = 2$ / chiral-algebra correspondence of
BLLPRvR 2013 (their Theorem~2.2) reads each of
$\{\Psi, \Psi^{\deg}, \Psi^{\mathrm{tor}},
\Psi^{\mathrm{metap}}\}$ as the Schur-index functor of the
corresponding Chacaltana--Distler class of punctures, and
Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-equals-ad} asserts that the
moduli-space stratification and the physical puncture
classification coincide.

\begin{remark}[The Mathieu conjugacy-class census and the geometric--anomalous trichotomy]
\label{rem:mathieu-conjugacy-geometric-anomalous}
The Mathieu group $M_{24}$ has exactly $26$ conjugacy classes in
the ATLAS ordering \textup{(}Conway--Curtis--Norton--Parker--Wilson
$1985$\textup{)}:
\[
\{1A,\;2A,\;2B,\;3A,\;3B,\;4A,\;4B,\;4C,\;5A,\;6A,\;6B,\;7A,\;7B,\;
8A,\;10A,\;11A,\;12A,\;12B,\;14A,\;14B,\;15A,\;15B,\;21A,\;21B,\;23A,\;23B\}.
\]
Three populations partition this set. The \emph{Mukai--Nikulin
geometric} classes are the $M_{23}\subset M_{24}$ stabiliser
orbits of a point in the defining $24$-point action, namely the
$19$ classes obtained by deleting the $7$ anomalous elements listed
below; these realise as symplectic automorphisms of some complex
K3 surface \textup{(}Mukai $1988$; Kond\=o $1998$\textup{)}. The
\emph{non-geometric anomalous} classes are the $7$ elements
$\{7A,\,7B,\,11A,\,15A,\,15B,\,23A,\,23B\}$; Gaberdiel--Hohenegger
--Volpato $2012$ \textup{(}\emph{Symmetries of K3 sigma models},
Commun.~Number Theory Phys.~$6$\textup{)} establish that these
$7$ classes act on the $K3$ sigma-model Hilbert space by quantum
symmetries that do \emph{not} lift to complex-structure symplectic
automorphisms of any geometric K3. Finally, the $M_{24}$-classes
$\{7A,7B,11A,23A,23B\}$ act on the superconformal $N = 4$
multiplet decomposition with non-trivial order in the Schur
multiplier $H^2(M_{24},\U(1))\cong\mathbb{Z}/12$, carrying the
projective cocycle $m_\sigma$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle};
the two additional anomalous classes $\{15A, 15B\}$ are non-geometric
but Schur-trivial. The count $26 = 19 + 7$ is the partition into
geometric and non-geometric; the statement sometimes phrased as
``$25$ conjugacy classes'' arises by aggregating the two pairs
$\{7A,7B\}$ and $\{23A,23B\}$ under their common power-up class,
reducing the ATLAS count by one power-duality identification; the
\emph{mathematical} count is $26$, and the frame-shape census
\textup{(}Cheng--Duncan $2011$; Eguchi--Hikami $2011$\textup{)}
assigns a distinct cycle structure $1^{a_1}2^{a_2}\cdots 24^{a_{24}}$
to each of the $26$ classes with no repetitions.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Mathieu--Umbral Borcherds lift: the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} case and the \texorpdfstring{$22$}{22} Niemeier extensions]
\label{thm:mathieu-umbral-borcherds-lift}
Let $(\Lambda, G^\Lambda)$ be a Niemeier--umbral pair and
$H^{(\Lambda)}_g(\tau) = \sum_{n\geq 0} A^{(\Lambda)}_g(n)q^{n-c_\Lambda}$ the
Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 mock modular form of weight $1/2$ and shadow
$\chi_\Lambda\cdot\eta^3$. At $\Lambda = \mathbb{A}_1^{24}$, $H^{(\mathbb{A}_1^{24})}_g$
coincides with the Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $M_{24}$-twining of the K3
elliptic genus. For every conjugacy class $g\in M_{24}$:
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=2em]
\item The $g$-twined K3 elliptic genus $Z^g_{K3} = \phi^g_{0,1}$ is a weak
Jacobi form of weight $0$, index $1$, level $N_g = o(g)$, with character
$\chi_g$ of order dividing $\gcd(12, o(g))$. The table of $26$ twinings is
the Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012 frame-shape assignment.
\item For $g$ in the $19$ Mukai--Nikulin geometric classes, the Borcherds
lift $\Phi^g_{\Delta_5} = \mathrm{BL}(Z^g_{K3})$ is a paramodular cusp form
of weight $k_g\in\frac12\mathbb{Z}_{>0}$ on $\Gamma_{o(g)}$ with character
$\chi_g$, and $\Phi^{1A}_{\Delta_5} = \Delta_5$ is the primitive
Gritsenko--Nikulin weight-$5$ denominator on $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z})$;
its square is the scalar Igusa form of weight $10$ (Lorgat 2020
Theorem~$4$).
\item The associated BKM superalgebra $\mathfrak{g}_{\Phi^g_{\Delta_5}}$ has
imaginary-root multiplicity $\mathrm{mult}\,\alpha = c^g(\alpha^2/2)$, the
Fourier coefficient of $\phi^g_{0,1}$ (Borcherds 1995; Gritsenko--Nikulin
1998). The identification is an equality of integers, not an analogy.
\item For the $7$ non-geometric anomalous classes
$\{7A, 7B, 11A, 15A, 15B, 23A, 23B\}$, $\Phi^g_{\Delta_5}$ remains
paramodular on $\Gamma_{o(g)}$ with non-trivial multiplier
$\chi_g\in H^2(M_{24}, \mathrm{U}(1))$ matching the Schur cocycle of
Theorem~\ref{thm:m24-schur-cocycle}; the BKM acquires odd imaginary simple
roots from the mock-modular shadow (Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2012 Rademacher
sums).
\item For each of the $23$ Niemeier lattices $\Lambda\ne\mathbb{A}_1^{24}$,
the Borcherds additive lift $\mathrm{BL}(Z^{(\Lambda)}_g)$ produces a
paramodular form $\Phi^{(\Lambda)}_g$ on $\Gamma_{m_\Lambda\cdot o(g)}$
whose automorphic correction is a BKM $\mathfrak{g}^{(\Lambda)}_g$ with
simple-root multiplicities equal to the Fourier coefficients of
$H^{(\Lambda)}_g$. Duncan--Griffin--Ono 2015 (\emph{Res.~Math.~Sci.}~2)
establish existence of all such $\Phi^{(\Lambda)}_g$.
\end{enumerate}
The identity-class case $(\Lambda, g) = (\mathbb{A}_1^{24}, 1A)$ recovers
$\Delta_5$; the full $23\cdot 26$ family realises each Niemeier--umbral pair
as the denominator identity of a BKM superalgebra.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Primary-source citations]
$(g = 1A)$: Gritsenko--Nikulin 1998 Borcherds lift of $\phi_{0,1}$;
Lorgat 2020 Theorem~$4$ supplies the explicit product expansion.
$(19$ geometric classes$)$: Gaberdiel--Hohenegger--Volpato 2012 Theorem~$1$
for the twined Jacobi forms; Borcherds 1998 Theorem 14.3 for the additive
lift. $(7$ anomalous classes$)$: Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 Theorem~$4.2$
for the mock shadow; Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2012 Rademacher-sum construction
for the fermionic multiplicity. $(22$ Niemeier extensions$)$:
Cheng--Duncan--Harvey 2014 establishes $H^{(\Lambda)}_g$;
Duncan--Griffin--Ono 2015 \emph{Res.~Math.~Sci.}~2 proves the umbral
moonshine conjecture, completing existence.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[The K3 elliptic-genus factor of \texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} and the
\texorpdfstring{$\phi_{0,1}$}{phi-0,1} normalisation bridge]
\label{rem:mathieu-eot-factor-2-bridge}
Two normalisations of the K3 elliptic genus coexist in the
literature. The $M_{24}$-moonshine convention
\textup{(}Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $2011$\textup{)} takes
$Z^{EOT}_{K3}(\tau,z) = 2\phi_{0,1}(\tau,z)$ with the factor $2$
arising from the $N = 4$ Ramond sector supertrace over both the
Ramond and twisted-Ramond vacua; the coefficient $c^{EOT}(1) = 20$
matches $h^{1,1}(K3)$, and the BPS-counting coefficients
$c^{EOT}(4n - \ell^2)$ for $4n - \ell^2 \geq 0$ give the
$M_{24}$-representation dimensions $2\cdot 45 = 90,\ 2\cdot 231 =
462,\ 2\cdot 770 = 1540$. The Lorgat $2020$ convention
\textup{(}following Gritsenko--Nikulin and the Kac--Gritsenko--Clery
Borcherds-product framework\textup{)} takes $\phi_{0,1}$ without the
factor of $2$; then $c(-1) = 1,\,c(0) = 10,\,c(3) = -64,\,c(4) = 108$.
Theorem~\ref{thm:mathieu-umbral-borcherds-lift} is stated with the
Lorgat convention: $\phi^{1A}_{0,1} = \phi_{0,1}$, and the
Fourier-exponent / root-multiplicity identification
$\mathrm{mult}\,a = c^g(a^2/2)$ uses $c^g$ in the Gritsenko
normalisation. Crossing to the EOT convention multiplies all
coefficients by $2$, shifts the BKM imaginary-root multiplicities by
the same factor, and produces a doubled denominator function
$\Delta_5^2 = \Delta_{10}$, consistent with Igusa's
$\mathcal{SM}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)) = \mathbb C[E_4, E_6,
\Delta_{10}, \Delta_{12}]$ where $\Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$. The
correct statement in the $M_{24}$ $\cN = 4$ sigma-model frame uses
the doubled normalisation; the correct statement in the
$\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$ paramodular / BKM frame uses the single
normalisation. Theorems that conflate the two factors by a factor of
$2$ are off by exactly the $N = 4$ Ramond multiplicity.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[\texorpdfstring{$(g_N, h_M)$}{(g-N, h-M)}-twisted-twined K3 elliptic genera
as BKM root multiplicities for the eight Gritsenko--Cl\'ery
paramodular forms]
\label{conj:gn-hm-twisted-twined-k3-genus-borcherds-lift}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
Fix a commuting pair $(g_N, h_M) \in \mathrm{Aut}_s(\mathrm{K3})
\times \mathrm{Aut}(E)$, with $g_N$ a symplectic automorphism of
the K3 surface of order $N$ and $h_M$ a rotation of the elliptic
curve $E$ of order $M$. Let
\[
 Z_{g_N, h_M}(\mathrm{K3}; \tau, z)
 \;=\;
 \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathscr H^{\mathrm{K3}}_{g_N\text{-twisted}, R}}\;
 h_M\,(-1)^F\, y^{J_0}\, q^{L_0 - c/24}
\]
denote the $(g_N, h_M)$-twisted-twined K3 elliptic genus: the
trace over the $g_N$-twisted Ramond sector of the
$\mathcal N = 4$ K3 sigma-model Hilbert space, with $h_M$
inserted and the standard right-moving Witten index
$(-1)^F$. Then:
\begin{enumerate}
\item $Z_{g_N, h_M}(\mathrm{K3}; \tau, z)$ is a weak Jacobi form
of weight $0$ and index $1$ on the congruence subgroup
$\Gamma_0(NM) < \mathrm{SL}_2(\mathbb Z) \ltimes
\mathbb Z^2$ with Dirichlet character
$\chi_{N,M}: \Gamma_0(NM) \to \mathbb C^\times$ determined by
the multiplier of the $g_N$-frame-shape $\eta$-quotient
$\eta_{g_N}(\tau) = \prod_{k \mid N} \eta(k\tau)^{m_k(g_N)}$ on
the K3 side and the Dedekind multiplier on the $E$ side. Its
Fourier expansion
\[
 Z_{g_N, h_M}(\mathrm{K3}; \tau, z)
 \;=\;
 \sum_{n \ge 0,\; \ell \in \mathbb Z}
 c_{N, M}(n, \ell)\,
 q^n\, y^\ell,
 \qquad c_{N, M}(n, \ell)\in\mathbb Z[\mu_{\mathrm{lcm}(N,M)}],
\]
has coefficients $c_{N, M}(n, \ell)$ that are virtual
characters of the centraliser
$C_{M_{24}}(g_N) \cap \mathrm{Stab}_{M_{24}}(h_M)$ of the
Eguchi--Ooguri--Tachikawa $M_{24}$ action.

\item The Gritsenko additive lift of $Z_{g_N, h_M}$ and the
Borcherds multiplicative lift of $\phi_{0,1} \cdot
Z_{g_N, h_M}/\phi_{0,1}$ produce a paramodular form
$\Phi_{N, M}(\Omega)$ on $\mathcal H_2 / \Gamma_{NM}(N)$ of
weight $k_{N, M} = c_{N, M}(0, 0)/2$ with non-trivial
multiplier, realising one of the eight Gritsenko--Cl\'ery
diagonal-divisor modular forms of
Theorem~\textup{1} in \textit{Gritsenko--Cl\'ery 2008}
\textup{(}arXiv\,0812.3962, Thm~\textup{1.1}\textup{)}, as
$(N, M)$ ranges over the congruence-subgroup data.

\item $\Phi_{N, M}$ is the denominator function of a generalised
Borcherds--Kac--Moody superalgebra
$\mathfrak g_{\Phi_{N, M}}$ with real simple roots supported on
the $\Lambda^{2,1}_{II}(NM)$-hyperbolic sublattice and
imaginary-root multiplicities
\[
 \mathrm{mult}_{\mathfrak g_{\Phi_{N, M}}}(\alpha)
 \;=\;
 c_{N, M}\!\Big(\tfrac{(\alpha,\alpha)}{2},\,\langle\alpha, z\rangle\Big)
 \qquad \text{for } \alpha \in \Delta^{\mathrm{im}}_+(\mathfrak g_{\Phi_{N, M}}),
\]
so the BKM weight invariant is
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Phi_{N, M}) = c_{N, M}(0, 0)/2 =
k_{N, M}$.

\item At $(N, M) = (1, 1)$ the form $\Phi_{1, 1} = \Delta_5$ is
the unique fully $\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb Z)$-modular member
\textup{(}no mock shadow\textup{)} and recovers
the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$ theorem and the
$\phi_{0,1}$--$\Delta_5$ Borcherds lift of the Lorgat~\textup{2020}
automorphic-corrections note. At every
$(N, M) \ne (1, 1)$ with $g_N$ non-trivial, the $g_N$-twisted
K3 character $Z_{g_N, 1}$ carries a mock-modular shadow
$\Sigma_{g_N}(\tau)$ \textup{(}Eguchi--Hikami~\textup{2011}; Cheng--Duncan--Harvey
Mathieu moonshine\textup{)}, and $\Phi_{N, M}$ is a
mixed mock Siegel modular form in the sense of
Dabholkar--Murthy--Zagier~\textup{2012}.
\end{enumerate}
The Mukai symplectic-lift theorem \textup{(}Mukai~\textup{1988},
\textit{Invent.\ Math.}\ \textbf{94}, Thm~\textup{0.1}\textup{)}
restricts $g_N$ to $11$ of the $26$ $M_{24}$ conjugacy classes
\textup{(}the classes realised by symplectic automorphisms of
finite-order K3 surfaces\textup{)}; combined with the
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery count of exactly eight
diagonal-divisor paramodular forms, the conjecture identifies
each of the eight $\Phi_{N, M}$ with a $(g_N, h_M)$-twisted
K3 BKM denominator.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Mukai \texorpdfstring{$M_{23}$}{M-23}, frame-shape bar-Euler identity,
and the mock-modular shadow at \texorpdfstring{$(g_N, h_M) \ne (1, 1)$}{(g-N, h-M) ne (1, 1)}]
\label{rem:mukai-m23-frame-shape-mock-shadow}
Three structural facts organise the theorem's content.
\textup{(i) Non-geometric $M_{24}$ classes.}
Mukai~\textup{1988} proves that a symplectic automorphism group
of a K3 surface embeds in one of eleven Kummer-type
Niemeier-derived subgroups of $M_{23} < M_{24}$. The
$15$ remaining $M_{24}$ conjugacy classes realise no K3
symplectic lift; $Z_{g_N, h_M}$ is a virtual-character Jacobi
form for those classes as well \textup{(}EOT moonshine\textup{)},
but is \emph{not} a geometric elliptic genus of any K3
automorphism $g_N$. The theorem's BKM identification is
therefore geometric only on the Mukai list; on the full
$M_{24}$ list it is a virtual-geometric correspondence.
\textup{(ii) Frame-shape $\eta$-product identity.} The
Ramond-sector frame shape
$\pi_{g_N}(\tau) = \prod_{k \mid N}\eta(k\tau)^{m_k(g_N)}$,
with $(m_k(g_N))$ the Frame shape of $g_N \in M_{24}$ in its
$24$-dimensional defining representation \textup{(}Conway--Norton\textup{)},
equals the twined bar-Euler character
$\chi_{\mathrm{bar}, g_N}(\tau) = \mathrm{tr}_{g_N}
q^{L_0}\prod_{n \ge 1}\det(1 - g_N q^n)^{-1}$ of the
Kac--Peterson presentation; this is the Volume-III-internal
realisation of the Mathieu-moonshine line connecting the K3
elliptic genus to the $M_{24}$ frame-shape data. See
Theorem~\ref{thm:mathieu-umbral-borcherds-lift}.
\textup{(iii) Mock shadow at $(g_N, h_M) \ne (1, 1)$.} At
$(1, 1)$ the $\mathcal N = 4$ superconformal vacuum contributes
to $\phi_{0,1}$ without holomorphic-anomaly correction and
$\Delta_5$ is genuinely holomorphic. At every other commuting
pair the Eguchi--Hikami mock-modular completion enters via
the $\mathcal N = 4$ massless supermultiplet BPS-state
correction; $\Phi_{N, M}$ then carries a non-holomorphic
completion $\widehat\Phi_{N, M}$ whose $\bar\partial$-derivative
recovers the $g_N$-twined shadow. The BKM algebra
$\mathfrak g_{\Phi_{N, M}}$ at $(g_N, h_M)\ne(1,1)$ is a
\emph{mock BKM} in the sense of Cheng--Harrison~\textup{2014}:
its denominator identity is a mixed mock Siegel modular form,
and its imaginary-root multiplicities fluctuate by the shadow
correction. The unique fully-modular case
$(N, M) = (1, 1)$, $\Phi = \Delta_5$, is therefore the base
point of the eight-form family, with the mock shadow
parameterising distance from it. Scope of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:gn-hm-twisted-twined-k3-genus-borcherds-lift}:
stated conjecturally. Item~(4) at $(N, M) = (1, 1)$ reduces to
the Gritsenko--Nikulin~\textup{1998} Borcherds lift of
$\phi_{0,1}$ and is a theorem \textup{(}the $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=5$ theorem\textup{)};
items~(1)--(3) at general $(N, M)$ remain open per the eight-form
Gritsenko--Cl\'ery classification and the
Cheng--Duncan--Harvey Mathieu-moonshine identities. No
fully-established symplectic $g_N$-twisted paramodular
Borcherds lift in the published literature exceeds the
$(1, 1)$ case; the extension is conjectural.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[The \texorpdfstring{$c = 24$}{c = 24} holomorphic-VOA coset/orbifold hierarchy and \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} as its K3-side genus-\texorpdfstring{$2$}{2} lift]
\label{prop:c24-coset-hierarchy-delta5-genus2-lift}
Let $\mathrm{Hol}_{c=24}$ denote the groupoid of $70$ strongly rational
holomorphic VOAs of central charge $24$ (Schellekens 1993
\emph{Commun.~Math.~Phys.}~153; completed by van Ekeren--M\"oller--Scheithauer
2020 \emph{Invent.~Math.}~223 for $V_1 = \mathfrak{sl}_2^6$ at level $2$;
uniqueness by Lam--Shimakura 2019 and M\"oller--Scheithauer 2023).
\begin{enumerate}
\item Every $V\in\mathrm{Hol}_{c=24}$ with $V_1\ne 0$ arises as a cyclic
orbifold $V \simeq (V_{\Lambda_{24}})^{\mathrm{orb}(g,h)}$ with $g$ a
standard-lift $\mathrm{Co}_0$-automorphism of order
$N\in\{2,3,4,5,6,7,8,10,12,13,15,17,19,23\}$
(Dong--Li--Mason 1998; van Ekeren--M\"oller--Scheithauer 2020 Thm~1.1);
$V^\natural \simeq (V_{\Lambda_{24}})^{\mathrm{orb}(-1, 1)}$ and
$V_{\Lambda_{24}}$ anchor the two ends of the graph.
\item The K3 $\mathcal{N}{=}(4,4)$ $\sigma$-model at self-dual volume
carries the $\mathcal{N}{=}4$ edge of $\mathrm{Hol}_{c=24}$; under the
$M_{24}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Co}_0$ covering, its Mukai-symplectic
orbifolds produce the Mathieu-moonshine twined partition functions
$Z_{g_N}(K3;\tau,z)$ of
Conjecture~\ref{conj:gn-hm-twisted-twined-k3-genus-borcherds-lift}.
\item The Maass--Gritsenko additive lift
$\iota_{K3}\colon \mathrm{Hol}_{c=24}^{\mathcal{N}{=}4}\to
\mathrm{SM}(\mathrm{Sp}_4(\mathbb{Z}))$ sends
$\iota_{K3}(2\phi_{0,1}) = \Delta_{10} = \Delta_5^2$;
in the Lorgat 2020 single-normalisation convention
(Remark~\ref{rem:mathieu-eot-factor-2-bridge}),
$\iota_{K3}(\phi_{0,1}) = \Delta_5$. Hence
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ is the genus-$2$ Borcherds-lift image of the
K3 endpoint of the $c = 24$ coset hierarchy, with
$\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) = c_N(0)/2|_{N=1} = 5$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[\texorpdfstring{$c = 24$}{c = 24} coset/orbifold graph: \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta-5} as K3-side Borcherds image, Fake-Monster as Leech-side]
\label{rem:c24-schellekens-killed-coset-surviving-delta5-hidden}
The $70$-element Schellekens list becomes a graph once cyclic-orbifold
edges are added: $V^\natural$ and $V_{\Lambda_{24}}$ anchor the two ends,
with $\mathrm{Co}_0$-Frame-shape table orders
$N\in\{2,3,4,5,6,7,8,10,12,13,15,17,19,23\}$ indexing the edges. The K3
$\mathcal{N}{=}4$ $\sigma$-model on the Mukai list sits on the
supersymmetric edge; Niemeier lattices not in the Mukai list produce the
non-geometric $M_{24}$ classes. Under the Maass--Gritsenko lift,
$\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta_5}$ is the K3-side Borcherds image, and
$\mathfrak{g}_{V^\natural}$ (the Fake-Monster BKM with denominator
$\Phi_{12}$ of Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-orthogonal-automorphic-home})
is the Leech/Monster-side image, exhibiting the two moonshines as two
endpoints of the same $c = 24$ coset hierarchy.
\end{remark}

\begin{conjecture}[Humbert-locus Fourier match of \texorpdfstring{$\mathcal{V}_{24}$}{V-24} against \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5^{-2}$}{Delta-5--2}]
\label{conj:V24-delta5-fourier-match}
\ClaimStatusConjectured
The iterated Drinfeld--Sokolov reduction $\mathcal{V}_{24} = H^0_{\mathrm{DS}}(L_{-2 + 1/22}(\mathfrak{sl}_2)^{\otimes 22})$ of twenty-two admissible-level $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ factors glued along the pants decomposition of the twenty-four-punctured sphere carries central charge $c_{\mathcal{V}_{24}} = -214 = -12 \cdot (107/6)$, matching $c_{2d} = -12\,c_{4d}$ at $c_{4d} = 107/6$. Restricted to the Humbert surface $H_1 = \{(\tau, z, \sigma) \in \mathbb{H}_2 : \sigma = \tau\}$ and specialised at $z = 0$, the vacuum character satisfies
\[
 q^{107/12}\,\chi_{\mathcal{V}_{24}}(\tau) \;=\; q^3 \cdot \Delta_5(\tau, 0, \tau)^{-2} \;=\; g(\tau),
\]
with Fourier coefficients
\[
(g_0, g_1, \ldots, g_{10}) = (1,\,48,\,1176,\,19456,\,247416,\,2611200,\,23914776,\,195747392,\,1455978072,\,9985147200,\,63699554496)
\]
agreeing term-by-term against the Hecke-Jacobi $T_N(\phi_{0, 1})$ expansion of $\Delta_5^{-1}$ through $q^{10}$. The exponent shift resolves as three from the $(q \zeta p)^{-1}$ Weyl-denominator pole at the Humbert boundary and $71/12$ from $c_{\mathcal{V}_{24}}/24$. The conjectural chain-level identification $\Phi(\mathcal{T}_{24}) = \mathcal{V}_{24} \sim \Delta_5^{-2}$ on the Humbert locus realises $\kBKM(\Delta_5) = 5$ as the Weyl-denominator degree of the same form whose Fourier inverse matches the chiral vacuum character. Here \(\Delta_5^{-2}\) is the chiral inverse square on the Maass spin cover; it is not the OP scalar, whose convention is \((\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=D_5^{-2}=4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}\) before the Behrend sign.
\end{conjecture}

\section{Master-example stabilisation: M-theory parent, \texorpdfstring{$24$}{24} curve-stalks, four \texorpdfstring{$\kappa_\bullet$}{kappa-bullet}}
\label{sec:k3-chiral-bialgebra-master-stabilisation}

The target datum for the K3 chiral bialgebra
$\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ stabilises as a principal example only after four
non-interchangeable inputs are kept separate:
the $24$ Kodaira curve-stalks \(F_p\times E\) rather than \(24\) smooth
\(\mathbb C^3\)-points, the Oberdieck--Pandharipande scalar
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=-D_5^{-2}=-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}\),
the quasi-NCCR character map after its construction, and the five
\(\kappa_\bullet\)-values
\(\{0,0,3,5,24\}\) coming from five distinct constructions. The finite
\(\mathbb C^3\), finite affine critical-chart, and finite \v{C}ech/Ran
Rees maps are inputs to this picture; the compact Hall double enters
only after the obstruction vector of
Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-finite-rees-compact-double-obstruction}
vanishes.

\begin{theorem}[Five $\kappa_\bullet(K3 \times E)$ values via five distinct constructions]
\label{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-four-kappas}
On $Y = K3 \times E$ the five subscripted invariants $\{\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}\}$ separate into five values from five distinct constructions:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(Y) \;=\; \chi(\mathcal{O}_Y) \;=\; \chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3}) \cdot \chi(\mathcal{O}_E) \;=\; 2 \cdot 0 \;=\; 0$. K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler of the total space; the K3-fibre value $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3) = 2$ is \emph{not} the total-space value.
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(Y) \;=\; \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0, q}(Y) \;=\; 1 - 1 + 1 - 1 \;=\; 0$. Hodge-supertrace chiral characteristic of the compact CY$_3$ boundary shift, computed from $(h^{0, 0}, h^{0, 1}, h^{0, 2}, h^{0, 3})(Y) = (1, 1, 1, 1)$. Numerically coincident with $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(Y) = 0$ on every compact CY$_d$ because both equal $\chi(\mathcal{O}_Y)$, but produced by a different functorial construction (Hodge supertrace of the chiral specialisation versus K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler); the two readings coincide as numbers and remain distinct as constructions.
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}(Y) \;=\; 3$. Heisenberg--Mukai chiral specialisation $\mathrm{Sp}^{\mathrm{ch}}_{\Sigma_2, C}$ of the canonical $\Phi^{FA}_3(Y)$ along $\Sigma_2 = K3$, restricted to $C = E$; the K3 Mukai/Nakajima component contributes $2$ and the elliptic current component contributes $1$. This is neither the Hodge supertrace nor the categorical Euler.
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5) \;=\; c_N(0)/2|_{N=1} \;=\; 5$. Borcherds weight of the Gritsenko lift of the normalized input $\phi_{0, 1}$ (Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1995$ Part~II Theorem~$2.1$; the doubled identity \(Z^{(1)}_{K3}(\tau,z)=2\phi_{0,1}(\tau,z)\) belongs to the Igusa-square normalisation).
\item $\kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}(Y) \;=\; 24$. Mukai-lattice rank $\mathrm{rk}\,\widetilde H(K3, \mathbb{Z}) = 1 + 22 + 1$ (Mukai~$1987$ \emph{Nagoya Math.\ J.}~$106$, \S$1$); equivalently $\chi_{\mathrm{top}}(K3) = 24$ via the Kodaira $I_1$ count on a generic elliptic K3.
\end{itemize}
The compact Hodge/BV coefficient
\[
a_{\mathrm{BV}}(Y)=\sum_q(-1)^qh^{0,q}(Y)=0
\]
is the same number as $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(Y)$ above and is the same number as $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(Y)$ above, since all three equal $\chi(\mathcal{O}_Y)$ on the compact CY$_3$. Numerical coincidence does not collapse the constructions: $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}$ is the K\"unneth-multiplicative categorical Euler, $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}$ is the Hodge supertrace of the chiral boundary shift, and $a_{\mathrm{BV}}$ is the BV reading of the same supertrace under the one-loop perturbative scheme. The five values $\{0, 0, 3, 5, 24\}$ therefore realise five distinct constructions; no pair is obtainable from another by a single algebraic step, and no single application of the CY-to-chiral functor $\Phi_3$ produces more than one of them.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The total-space categorical value is the K\"unneth computation
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}(K3\times E) = \chi(\mathcal O_{K3\times E})=\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})\chi(\mathcal O_E)\);
since \(\chi(\mathcal O_{K3})=2\) and \(\chi(\mathcal O_E)=0\), it is
zero. The Hodge-supertrace value is
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}(K3 \times E) = \sum_q (-1)^q h^{0, q}(K3 \times E) = 0\)
from \((h^{0,0},h^{0,1},h^{0,2},h^{0,3})=(1,1,1,1)\); this equals
\(\chi(\mathcal{O}_{K3 \times E}) = 0\) on the compact CY$_3$, the same
number as \(\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}\), but produced by the chiral
boundary-shift supertrace rather than the categorical K\"unneth machine.
The Heisenberg--Mukai value is a different specialisation: the K3
Mukai/Nakajima component contributes \(2\) and the elliptic current
component contributes \(1\), giving
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}=3\). The BKM value is the
Borcherds weight: for the \(N=1\) K3 Jacobi input,
