\(c_1(0)=10\), hence
\(\kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}(\Delta_5)=c_1(0)/2=5\). Finally the fibre value is the
Mukai rank \(1+22+1=24\), equivalently the sum of the Euler
contributions of the \(24\) Kodaira \(I_1\)-fibres. These five
computations use five different functorial constructions, so
agreement of the first two as numbers (both equal $\chi(\mathcal{O}_Y)$
on the compact CY$_3$) does not collapse them as constructions, and
no single algebraic step transports any one value to any other.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$24$ curve-stalks underlie the target factorisation Ran space]
\label{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-24-curve-stalks}
The bi-based target factorisation datum
$(\mathrm{Ran}(E^{\mathrm{nod, sm}}_{24}), \overline{\mathcal{A}_2})$
for $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ has its $24$ singular-fibre data indexed by
the $24$ Kodaira $I_1$-nodes $F_p$, $p \in \Delta(\pi) \subset
\mathbb{P}^1$ of a generic elliptic fibration
$\pi \colon K3 \to \mathbb{P}^1$.  On the CY$_3$ $Y = K3 \times E$ each
$F_p$ lifts to a \emph{curve-stalk} $F_p \times E$ of complex dimension
$2$, \emph{not} a smooth $\mathbb{C}^3$-point.  The local completed
algebra at a Kodaira node $p \in F_p$ times an $E$-point $q \in E$ is
\[
 \widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y, (p, q)} \;\simeq\; \mathbb{C}[[x_1, x_2, z]]/(x_1 x_2), \qquad z \in T_q E,
\]
a two-sheet crossing times a smooth curve direction. The CoHA of the CY$_3$ germ at each curve-stalk is therefore the $E$-fibered CoHA of the $A_1$ ordinary double point, \emph{not} the Schiffmann--Vasserot~$2013$ $\mathbb{C}^3$ plane-partition CoHA $Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1)$ which governs smooth toric $\mathbb{C}^3$-stalks on a different CY$_3$. Conflating a curve-stalk with a toric $\mathbb{C}^3$-stalk raises the local dimension by one and replaces the nodal local ring with a smooth one; the two mistakes compound and undercount the protected sector by one complex direction.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Kodaira~$1963$ (\emph{Ann.\ Math.}~$77$:$563$, \S$6$) classifies singular fibres of elliptic surfaces and proves $c_2(K3) = \sum_p \chi_{\mathrm{top}}(F_p) = 24$. Miranda~$1989$ (\emph{The basic theory of elliptic surfaces}, Dottorato di Ricerca, ETS Editrice Pisa) supplies the modern treatment. Base-change along $\mathrm{pr}_E \colon Y \to E$ preserves the fibre type pointwise; the curve-stalks $F_p \times E$ are therefore $\dim_{\mathbb{C}} = 2$ rather than $\dim_{\mathbb{C}} = 3$. The local-ring identification $\widehat{\mathcal{O}}_{Y, (p, q)} \simeq \mathbb{C}[[x_1, x_2, z]]/(x_1 x_2)$ is the K\"unneth product of the nodal local model $\mathbb{C}[[x_1, x_2]]/(x_1 x_2)$ on $F_p$ (Miranda~$1989$, \S$V$) with the smooth local model $\mathbb{C}[[z]]$ on $E$ at $q$. The factorisation-Ran-space structure on $E^{\mathrm{nod, sm}}_{24}$ is documented in Sections~\ref{sec:k3-bi-based-factorization} and~\ref{subsec:nearby-cycles-each-node-k3} above.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quasi-NCCR character in OP/Igusa normalization]
\label{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-quasi-nccr}
$K3 \times E$ admits no global non-commutative crepant resolution (five obstructions, Theorem~\ref{thm:quasi-nccr-character-phi10} in Chapter~\ref{ch:k3e-bkm}). If the Serre-equivariant quasi-NCCR $\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{qNCCR}}(K3 \times E)$ is locally constructed from Seidel--Thomas~$2001$ (arXiv:math/$0001043$, \S$3$) spherical twists on $D^b(K3)$ pulled back along $\mathrm{pr}_1$ and glued via the factorisation-homology pushforward of Costello--Gwilliam~$2017$ over the complement of the $24$ curve-stalks, then its derived character is
\[
 \chi\bigl(\mathcal{A}^{\mathrm{qNCCR}}(K3 \times E)\bigr)(q, \tilde q, y)
 \;=\;
 -\bigl(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}(\tau,z,\sigma)\bigr)^{-1}
 \;=\;
 -D_5(\tau,z,\sigma)^{-2}
 \;=\;
 -4096\,\Delta_5(\tau,z,\sigma)^{-2},
\]
where
\[
 D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5,
 \qquad
 \Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2=4096^{-1}\Delta_5^2.
\]
The unnormalised Igusa square is \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^2\)
(Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1998$, arXiv:alg-geom/$9611028$); it becomes the OP
scalar denominator only after the monic Borcherds normalization is restored.
The minus sign is fixed by the Behrend-weighted virtual orientation on the
DT moduli (Maulik--Toda~$2018$). Up to this sign the character agrees with
the Oberdieck--Pandharipande~$2016$ reduced Donaldson--Thomas partition
function
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3 \times E)
 =
 -(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1};
\]
the categorification statement is exactly the quasi-NCCR input.
This sentence uses Oberdieck--Pandharipande for the primitive reduced-DT
scalar; Oberdieck--Pixton and Bryan--Oberdieck--Pandharipande--Yin belong
to the broader descendant, imprimitive, and orbifold families.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Attribution]
Oberdieck--Pandharipande~$2016$ (arXiv:$1411.1514$, Theorem~$1$) gives the
reduced-DT scalar; in the OP normalization used here it is
\(Z^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathrm{DT}}(K3 \times E)=-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}\)
with \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\) and \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\), hence
\(-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}\). Gritsenko--Nikulin~$1998$
(arXiv:alg-geom/$9611028$; see also \cite{GritsenkoNikulin1998}) gives
the unnormalised Igusa square \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}=\Delta_5^{\,2}\)
(Maass--Gritsenko additive lift and Borcherds multiplicative lift coincide
on the genus-$2$ weight-$10$ Igusa cusp form); Seidel--Thomas~$2001$
(arXiv:math/$0001043$) supplies spherical twists; Maulik--Toda~$2018$
tracks the Behrend sign on CY$_3$; Van den Bergh~$2004$ (\emph{Duke}~$122$,
\S$3$) gives NCCR obstructions; Bridgeland--King--Reid~$2001$
(\emph{JAMS}~$14$) gives derived McKay; Kuznetsov~$2007$
(\emph{Adv.\ Math.}~$210$) gives HPD; Mukai~$1987$ supplies the Mukai
vanishing input; PTVV~$2013$ (\emph{Publ.\ IH\'ES}~$117$) supplies shifted
symplectic geometry; Schiffmann--Vasserot~$2013$ (\emph{Publ.\ Math.\
IH\'ES}~$118$, Theorem~$8.1$) supplies the CoHA character framework. No
assertion in this theorem uses the Fake-Monster Borcherds form
\(\Phi_{12}\); \(\Phi_{12}\) restricts to \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{un}}\) in
Theorem~\ref{thm:fake-monster-restriction-phi10}, while the primitive K3
object here is the square root \(\Delta_5\) on the Maass spin cover.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Cartan rank of the \texorpdfstring{$\Delta_5$}{Delta5} source]
\label{rem:k3-chiral-bialgebra-rank-23-cartan}
The $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$ source has the rank-$3$ Cartan lattice
$\Lambda^{2,1}_{\mathrm{II}}$. The quotient
$\mathrm{II}_{4,20}/U\simeq\mathrm{II}_{3,19}$ is a rank-$22$ K3
transverse lattice, and the full Mukai lattice
$\widetilde H(K3,\mathbb Z)$ has rank $24$. Neither lattice is the
Cartan of $\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5}$. Leech data belongs to the
Fake-Monster denominator on $II_{25,1}$, not to the $\Delta_5$ source.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Humbert $H_1$ order $8 = 2 \cdot c_+ \cdot \nu(\mu_0)$ with $c_+ = 4$ from Mukai $(4, 20)$]
\label{rem:k3-chiral-bialgebra-H1-order-8-mukai}
The order-$8$ monodromy of the Humbert divisor $H_1 \subset \overline{\mathcal{A}_2}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:humbert-order-K-kappa}) decomposes as
\[
 8 \;=\; 2 \cdot c_+ \cdot \nu(\mu_0), \qquad c_+ = 4, \quad \nu(\mu_0) = 1,
\]
where $c_+ = 4$ is the positive-signature component of the Mukai lattice $\widetilde H(K3, \mathbb{Z})$ of signature $(4, 20)$ (Mukai~$1987$), $\nu(\mu_0) = 1$ is the multiplicity of the fundamental Humbert eigenvalue (the norm-$\mu_0$ Fourier coefficient of $\eta^9 \vartheta_1$, unit-index), and the prefactor $2$ is the Jacobi $\mathbb{Z}/2$-cover $\mathrm{Sp}_4 \to \mathrm{SO}_+(\Lambda^{3, 2})$. The polarisation by the positive Mukai four-plane splits signature $(4, 20)$ into summands of signatures $(4, 0)$ and $(0, 20)$; the positive summand has rank $4$, supplying $c_+ = 4$. The universal identity
\[
 K^{\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}} \;=\; \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^! \;=\; 2 c_+ \;=\; 8
\]
entering the Vol~I Theorem~C bucket $\{0,\, 8,\, 13,\, 250/3,\, 25/3\}$ as the $\mathcal{B}$-row is the Mukai lattice calculation. Bruinier Heegner Chern-class reciprocity (Bruinier~$2002$ arXiv:math/$0212081$, Proposition~$5.1$) supplies the automorphic Chern-class comparison at \(H_1\). It does not by itself identify the Mukai integer with a Lusztig root-of-unity class.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[M-theory parent of $\mathbf{H}_{\Delta_5}$: constructed shadows and compact obstruction]
\label{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-m-theory-parent-stratified}
The phrase ``M-theory parent of \(\mathbf H_{\Delta_5}\)'' expands into
five different assertions.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item \emph{Reduced-DT character.} The B-model topological string
partition function \(Z^{\mathrm{top, B}}_{K3 \times E}\) has numerical
value
\[
 Z^{\mathrm{top, B}}_{K3 \times E}(q, \tilde q, y)
 \;=\;
 -(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}
 \;=\;
 -D_5^{-2}
 \;=\;
 -4096\,\Delta_5^{-2},
\]
via the Oberdieck--Pandharipande reduced-DT theorem and the
normalized Gritsenko--Nikulin identity
\(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\), \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\).
Identifying this scalar with a quasi-NCCR character requires the
quasi-NCCR construction and character map.
\item \emph{Smooth toric CY$_3$ positive half.} On a smooth
\(\mathbb C^3\)-stalk,
\[
 \mathrm{CoHA}(\mathbb C^3)=Y^+(\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}_1).
\]
This is the positive Hall half; it is not \(\mathcal W_{1+\infty}\).
\item \emph{Finite hCS--Hall comparison.} At fixed finite affine
critical charts and fixed finite \v{C}ech/Ran bounds \((N,r,L,m)\), the
DWR/Ran Rees construction gives the finite comparison maps
\(\Theta_{\mathrm{hCS}\to\mathrm{Hall}}^{\mathrm{Rees,or};N,r,L,m}\).
\item \emph{Physical interpretation.} The 5D \(\mathcal N=2\) reduction
and the M5 \((0,4)\)-sigma-model account explain why the reduced-DT
character is expected to count protected states, but this interpretation
does not construct the compact Hall pairing, coproduct, or completion.
\item \emph{Compact bialgebra.} A quasi-Hopf
identification
\[
 \mathcal A^{\mathrm{M},\Omega}_{\mathrm{prot}}(K3\times E)
 \;\cong\;
 Y_\hbar^{\mathrm{super}}(\mathfrak g_{\Delta_5})
 \;\cong\;
 D^\wedge_\hbar\!\bigl(Y^+_{\mathrm{Hall}}(K3\times E)\bigr)
\]
requires the compact comparison defects
\(o_{\mathrm{ML}},o_{\mathrm{real}},o_{\mathrm{cpt}}\) and the double
defects \(o_{\Delta},o_{\mathrm{pair}},o_{\mathrm{cent}}\) to vanish,
with the negative half, Cartan, coproduct, Hopf pairing, and centre data
specified.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Item \textup{(i)} is Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-quasi-nccr}
with the quasi-NCCR interpretation removed from the numerical
Oberdieck--Pandharipande equality. Item \textup{(ii)} is the
\(\mathbb C^3\) calculation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:qgf-coha-y-plus-winfty-chain}. Item \textup{(iii)}
is the finite part of
Proposition~\ref{prop:kcb-finite-rees-compact-double-obstruction}. The
physical reductions in \textup{(iv)} supply an interpretation of the
same character, not a construction of the compact Hall double. Item
\textup{(v)} is exactly the obstruction criterion of
Theorem~\ref{thm:kcb-hall-drinfeld-super-yangian-criterion} rewritten
with the compact defects named in
Definition~\ref{def:compact-oriented-critical-hall-datum}. Thus the
M-theory slogan is true at the level of the reduced-DT character and
finite positive-half shadows, while the compact bialgebra statement is
equivalent to the displayed vanishing and pairing/completion data.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Master example: what ``K3$\times$E is the Vol~III principal example'' means]
\label{rem:k3-chiral-bialgebra-master-example-meaning}
The phrase ``K3$\times$E is the Vol~III principal example of the CY-to-chiral correspondence'' means all five of the following hold:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)}]
\item The five $\kappa_\bullet \in \{0, 0, 3, 5, 24\}$ separate via five distinct constructions (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-four-kappas}), documenting that the five subscripts $\{\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Heis}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{BKM}}, \kappa_{\mathrm{fiber}}\}$ are not one number read several ways; the numerical coincidence $\kappa_{\mathrm{cat}} = \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^{\mathrm{Hodge}} = 0$ on the compact CY$_3$ is the K\"unneth-vs-Hodge-supertrace shared value of $\chi(\mathcal{O}_Y)$, not a collapse of constructions.
\item The $24$ Kodaira curve-stalks (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-24-curve-stalks}) supply the bi-based Ran-space datum, not a toric $24$-point configuration.
\item The quasi-NCCR character
\(-(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}})^{-1}=-D_5^{-2}=-4096\,\Delta_5^{-2}\)
(Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-quasi-nccr}) couples the geometric
side (Oberdieck--Pandharipande DT) to the chiral side (CoHA character)
via the Schiffmann--Vasserot framework and the normalized Gritsenko--Nikulin
identity \(D_5=64^{-1}\Delta_5\), \(\Phi_{10}^{\mathrm{OP}}=D_5^2\).
\item The Theorem-C complementarity $\kappa_{\mathrm{ch}} + \kappa_{\mathrm{ch}}^! = 2 c_+ = 8$ places $K3 \times E$ on the $\mathcal{B}$-row of the canonical five-archetype ceiling $\{0, 8, 13, 250/3, 25/3\}$. Humbert \(H_1\) monodromy and Lusztig \(\ell\)-specialisation are comparison data, not consequences of this lattice equality.
\item The M-theory parent (Theorem~\ref{thm:k3-chiral-bialgebra-m-theory-parent-stratified}) consists of the reduced-DT character, the smooth-toric \(\mathbb C^3\) positive half, finite DWR/Ran Rees hCS--Hall maps, a physical protected-state interpretation, and the compact bialgebra assertion after the completion, realisation, compact-support, coproduct, pairing, and centre data are supplied.
\end{enumerate}
The master-example assertion is therefore a convergence of separate
constructions, not a single universal theorem.
\end{remark}
